# Retrieval and answer frames: findings and evidence

The verified findings behind each issue in this area. Claims and fixes are shown as corrected by verification. Finding ids starting with r2- come from the second round.

## RETR-1 · Policy numbers are extracted and looked up loosely, anchoring frames on the wrong policy

Severity high · effort M

### corpus-frames-01 — Clinical tokens such as "3D", "3b" and "4B" are parsed as explicit policy numbers and anchor the frame on an unrelated policy

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts:154`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** A question token made of digits followed by one letter (optionally with a hyphen suffix) is extracted as an explicit NC policy number with no cue word or corpus check, and coverage routing returns lookup documents for it before anything inferred from retrieval. "3D mammogram", "stage 3b kidney disease" and "stage 4B cancer" therefore scope the coverage frame to Policy 3D (Hospice Services), 3B (PACE) or 4B (Orthodontic Services), all of which exist as current clinical policies in the graph. In a fixture probe the frame's first authoritative document and both primary sections were Hospice, including "4.1 General Criteria Not Covered", with cite_scope_and_not_covered true. The correct 1K-1 breast-imaging sections were demoted to supporting when no Hospice hit was retrieved, and dropped from the frame entirely when Hospice hits were present (likely in production, because the policy_scoped backend also keys on the same false policy number). Where the breast-imaging section lands (supporting, avoid, or absent) depends on the fixture; 1K-1 is not reliably kept as a second preferred source. The final agent answer was not run.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts:154-155 `const lookupDocuments = await collectLookupDocuments(query);` / `if (lookupDocuments.length > 0) {` packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:445 `...(query.match(/\b\d+[A-Za-z](?:-[A-Za-z0-9]+)?\b/g) ?? []),`
- **Impact:** "Does NC Medicaid cover 3D mammograms?" gives explicitPolicyNumbers ["3D"]. In a probe at HEAD built on the repo's own fixtures, authoritativeDocuments[0] was 3D Hospice Services and primarySections were Hospice 2.1.1 and "4.1 General Criteria Not Covered", with cite_scope_and_not_covered true. The breast-imaging section came back in avoidSections with content null. The agent plan generated from that frame says to answer yes or no first, pair the scope section with the not-covered section, and say plainly if the policy "governs a different service category". The repo's parsed Policy 1K-1 says breast tomosynthesis is "also known as 3D mammography" and is billable with screening and diagnostic mammograms. So the frame points to "no" where the corpus says "yes". The second preferred source is still listed as 1K-1, so a careful model can recover. I did not run the agent, so the final answer is unverified.
- **Fix:** Make one change in extractExplicitPolicyNumbers (packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts) and drop both the corpus-membership set and the "hint" tier. 1. Keep hyphenated forms (\d+[A-Za-z]-[A-Za-z0-9]+) and prefixed forms (MA-/DMA-/DHB-/DSS-) unconditional. 2. Accept a bare digit+letter token only when a cue immediately precedes it: policy, policies, CCP, No., number or #, optionally followed by "(", ":" or "#". Allow a short list continuation ("policies 8C and 8A", "policy 8C, 8A"). Do not use a query-level cue: "What is the NC Medicaid policy on 3D mammograms?" must not extract 3D. 3. Leave uncued tokens out entirely. Coverage routing then falls through to the existing collectPolicyDocumentsFromHits inference, which already demands focus-term corroboration. Because analysis.ts, retrieval-planning.ts and routing.ts all call this function, the policy_scoped backend and EXACT_POLICY_NUMBER_BOOST are fixed by the same change. 4. Close the path the original recommendation missed. In rerank.ts scorePolicyNumberMatches, drop the queryMentionsPolicyNumber / POLICY_NUMBER_MATCH_BOOST branch for bare digit+letter policy numbers, or fold it into the exactPolicyNumbers check, so "3D mammogram" no longer boosts Hospice hits. Keep it for digits-only policies such as "policy 9" and "policy 15", which the extractor never matches and the existing queryMentionsPolicyNumber tests cover. 5. Add regression cases to tests/internal/query-intent.test.ts. - Must extract nothing: "3D mammogram", "stage 3b kidney disease", "stage 4B cancer", "2x daily", "90d supply", "policy on 3D mammograms". - Must extract: "policy 3D", "Clinical Coverage Policy No. 8C", "policy (5B)", "policies 8C and 8A", "1A-15", "MA-2180". - Via analyzeQuery, must still yield 8C through the alias: the held-out question "...for the 8C outpatient behavioral health policy?". - One frame-level case in tests/frames-coverage-routing.test.ts asserting that "Does NC Medicaid cover 3D mammograms?" does not anchor on Policy 3D.
- **How it was verified:** The claim holds; I could not refute it. **What I checked** - **Quoted evidence:** both quotes are verbatim at the cited lines. `routing.ts:154-155` returns lookup documents before `collectPolicyDocumentsFromHits` is ever called. `query-intent.ts:445` has the bare digit-then-letter regex with no cue word and no corpus check. - **Guards:** I found none. `collectPolicyNumbersForQuery` (`retrieval-planning.ts:77-81`) passes the regex output straight to `lookupPolicy`. The only filter is whether the policy exists in the graph, and the local Neo4j graph holds current clinical policies 3D (Hospice Services), 3B (PACE), 4B (Orthodontic Services) and 3A (Home Health Services). - **Extraction:** running the real functions gave 3D, 3B, 4B, 3A, 2X and 90D for the reviewer's phrases. The three "cover" questions classify as `coverage`, so they reach `buildCoverageFrame`, which calls `collectCoverageR…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted through the context lens; high severity stands, with the caveat that the end-to-end wrong answer is still unproven. What I opened: - packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts (extractor) - packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/retrieval-planning.ts (collectPolicyNumbersForQuery) - packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts (lookup-first coverage routing) - packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts and packages/agent/src/prompt.ts (how the frame is consumed) - packages/policy-corpus/tests/query-analysis.test.ts - docs/development/gotchas.md and docs/decisions (grep), the open issue list, and the live local graph (read-only) Is it an accepted or docum…

### corpus-frames-02 — A policy number inferred from hits is resolved by prefix lookup and takes the newest sibling (1A-2 resolves to 1A-27)

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts:140`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** collectPolicyDocumentsFromHits turns each hit's policy number back into a document by calling lookupPolicy and taking the first row. lookupPolicy matches by prefix and orders by effective date descending with no preference for the exact number. When a policy number is a prefix of newer siblings, the hits are attributed to a different policy.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts:140 `const [document] = await lookupPolicy({ policyNumber });` packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:65 `OR d.policy_number STARTS WITH pn)` and :80 `d.effective_date DESC,`
- **Impact:** The crawler registry holds 1A-2 (Preventive Medicine Annual Health Assessment) and 1A-20 to 1A-28. Saved eval evidence records 1A-2 effective 2019-12-04 and 1A-27 (Electrodiagnostic Studies) effective 2024-06-01, so the first row for "1A-2" is 1A-27. In a probe at HEAD with that lookup order, "Does NC Medicaid cover an annual preventive health assessment for adults?" (hits all from 1A-2) produced authoritativeDocuments[0] = 1A-27. Primary sections were 1A-27 2.1.1 and "4.1 General Criteria Not Covered"; 1A-2's own 3.1 and 1.0 sections landed in avoid with content null. Other real prefix collisions in the registry: 1A-3 (7 longer numbers), 1A-4 (1A-40, 1A-42), 11A-1 (6), 1S-1 (4), 8A (12), 8A-1 (4), 3L/3L-1, 9/9A-9E. 3L and 9 resolve correctly today only because of how their dates happen to sort. The dates are from July 2026 artifacts; I did not query the live graph.
- **Fix:** Do this in two steps; the first fixes the finding, the second is a separate decision. **Step 1: exact-first in `lookupPolicy` (`packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts`).** - Add `CASE WHEN any(pn IN $pns WHERE toLower(d.policy_number) = toLower(pn)) THEN 0 ELSE 1 END` as the first ORDER BY key, so `LIMIT 25` can never cut exact rows. Leave the `WHERE any(pn IN $pns WHERE ...` clause text unchanged; the test matcher keys on it. - In TypeScript, after mapping, return only the rows whose `policyNumber` equals a candidate case-insensitively when at least one exists; otherwise return the prefix rows as today. - Do not add a "sibling" label or any new field. - Add a `tests/policy-lookup.test.ts` case where the session returns `[1A-27, 1A-2]` for "1A-2" and assert only 1A-2 comes back. The TypeScript filter is what makes this fixture meaningful. - Add one frame test where hits from 1A-2 yield `authoritativeDocuments[0]` = 1A-2. This also fixes `collectLookupDocuments`, `collectImplementationLookupDocuments` and the agent's `lookupPolicy` tool, which currently return the whole 1A-20 to 1A-28 family for "1A-2". **Step 2 (separate change, own tests): documentId on hits.** This is for the 54 policy numbers shared by several distinct documents among routing types, such as the four "1915(c)" waivers and the MA-2252 year editions. - Add `${alias}.id AS documentId,` to `retrievalDocumentMetadataReturn`, map it in `recordToRetrievalHit`, and add it to `CorpusRetrievalHit`. Check `keyword.ts` and `amendment-context.ts`, which may not use the shared helper. - In `collectPolicyDocumentsFromHits`, key the score map by `documentId`, not policy number. Without this, it is undefined which document wins a merged bucket. - Resolve with `getDocument(documentId)`, and decide deliberately whether routing documents should carry its section list (only sections with content, plus `chars`, no supersession) instead of the lookup's. - Run the answer evals before and after; this changes which year edition or waiver becomes authoritative.
- **How it was verified:** The claim holds: the code is as quoted, and the local graph returns 1A-27 as the first row for a "1A-2" lookup. What I opened and found: - `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts` lines 100-148. `collectPolicyDocumentsFromHits` upper-cases each hit's policy number, aggregates scores, then calls `lookupPolicy({ policyNumber })` per number and keeps only the first row (line 140, verbatim as quoted). Nothing afterwards checks that the returned document's policy number equals the requested one; the only post-step is `uniqueBy` on document id. - `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts` lines 53-99. The WHERE clause includes `OR d.policy_number STARTS WITH pn` (line 65, verbatim). The ORDER BY has no exact-match term: it sorts by change-notice title, amendment status, null date, then `d.effective_date DESC` (line 80, verbatim), then title length and id. - Reachabi…
- **Why this severity:** I tried to dismiss this as contrived, already known, or dependent on stale July dates, and could not. What I opened: packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts (lines 30-179), packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts (lines 14-104), docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/decisions, docs/operations, RUNBOOK.md, the open issue list, and the local Neo4j graph (read-only). 1. The reviewer had not queried the live graph, so I replayed lookupPolicy's exact WHERE and ORDER BY for every non-amendment policy number in the local graph (667 distinct numbers, 176 clinical). Six numbers resolve to a different document as the first row: 1A-2 to 1A-27, 1A-3 to 1A-33, 1A-4 to 1A-40, 1S-1 …

### corpus-retrieval-04 — Policy-number identity is lossy end to end: extraction misses real numbers, lookup matches by prefix without ranking the exact policy first, and the family key merges unrelated manuals

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:65`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** All four defects are confirmed, with one correction to the impact example. In the current graph lookupPolicy('3L') returns 3L first, because 3L-1 has a NULL effective_date and sorts last, so the 3L/3L-1 swap is latent, not live. The live misroutes are six other policies whose first returned row is an unrelated prefix sibling: 1A-2 returns 1A-27, 1A-3 returns 1A-33, 1A-4 returns 1A-40, 1S-1 returns 1S-11, 8A-1 returns 8A-12, 11A-1 returns 11A-10. When hits point at any of these, routing.ts:140 builds the frame on the wrong policy. Synthetic change_notice revision nodes are returned by the lookup but sort after current documents, so they never take [0]; they do enter collectLookupDocuments, which flattens all rows. The q-036 eval file is at packages/pipeline/data/eval-results/milestones/2026-09-22-verified-release/final-gate-full.json.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:63-65 `d.policy_number = pn` / `OR toLower(d.policy_number) = toLower(pn)` / `OR d.policy_number STARTS WITH pn)`; :80 `d.effective_date DESC,` (the ORDER BY at :76-82 has no exact-match key). packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts:140 `const [document] = await lookupPolicy({ policyNumber });`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:445 `...(query.match(/\b\d+[A-Za-z](?:-[A-Za-z0-9]+)?\b/g) ?? []),`. packages/policy-corpus/src/query/analysis.ts:55-58 `const explicitPolicyNumbers = dedupeTerms([` / `...extractExplicitPolicyNumbers(query),` / `...aliasedPolicyNumbers,`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/document-selection.ts:210 `return policyNumber.toUpperCase().replace(/-[A-Z0-9]+$/g, '');`. packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/references/amendments.py:262 `policy_number=policy,`
- **Impact:** Against the 176 clinical policy numbers in the local crawler registry, nine inputs match several distinct policies: 3L matches 3L and 3L-1; 9 matches 9, 9A, 9B, 9D, 9E; 1A-2 matches 1A-2 plus 1A-20 to 1A-28; 8A matches 12 policies; also 8A-1, 1A-3, 1A-4, 1S-1 and 11A-1. When hits point at policy 3L (in-home PCS), collectPolicyDocumentsFromHits calls lookupPolicy('3L') and keeps [0], which is whichever of 3L and 3L-1 (congregate settings) was revised more recently. The frame is then built on the sibling and the answer is grounded, cited and about the wrong policy, which a groundedness verifier cannot detect; gotchas.md already warns about confusing these two. The extractor misses 1-I, 1-O-1/2/3/5, 7, 9 and 15. lookupPolicy('1-H') returns [] because the stored number is 1H, as do 'Policy 8C', 'MA 2180' and 'SSA Title XIX'. "Does policy 1-H cover telehealth for speech therapy?" yields explicitPolicyNumbers ['10A']: the policy the user named is ignored and another is boosted and routed. Golden q-036 (Policy 1-H) scored 0 with primary_hit false in the saved 2026-09-22 gate; that is corroboration, not proven causation, since the frame still located document 1H by another path. narrowInferredCoverageDocuments([3K-2 (top), MA-2180, MA-3550]) returns the two unrelated MA manuals and drop…
- **Fix:** Keep the fix inside the existing seams; no data-loaded PolicyNumber module and no pipeline change. 1. **lookupPolicy Cypher (policy-lookup.ts).** Compare on a normalised key on both sides: upper-case, strip spaces and hyphens. This is safe today: 674 stored numbers give 674 keys. Replace `STARTS WITH pn` with a family match on the '-' boundary only (stored upper-cased number starts with pn + '-'). Add an exact-match term as the first ORDER BY key. Exclude `document_type = 'change_notice'` rows (all 4,267 are synthetic; revisions still arrive through getAmendments). Return a matchType of exact or family. 2. **candidatePolicyNumbers.** Extend this existing tested function rather than adding a module: strip a leading "Policy", "CCP", "Clinical Coverage Policy" or "No."; turn "MA 2180" into "MA-2180". 3. **routing.ts:140.** Take the first exact match only, never a family row. This keeps the gotchas.md:94 behaviour for MA-2270, whose three documents share one number. 4. **extractExplicitPolicyNumbers.** Widen the regex to accept an optional hyphen before the letter and repeated numeric suffixes (1-H, 1-I, 1-O-1). Accept bare numerals (7, 9, 15) only after a cue such as "policy", "CCP" or "clinical coverage policy". Do not token-match bare numerals from a dictionary; "age 9" and "15 days" would fire. 5. **Canonical form for retrieval.** Emit both the typed and hyphen-stripped forms into explicitPolicyNumbers (or normalise the stored side in policy-scoped.ts:30 and rerank.ts:255). Otherwise a typed "1-H" still never matches stored "1H" in the scoped backend or the exact boost. 6. **Aliases.** Split by role rather than boost size. Only typed numbers go into explicitPolicyNumbers (exact boost and policy_scoped scope). Aliases stay in aliasedPolicyNumbers and in collectPolicyNumbersForQuery, so 10A still arrives as a routed supporting document; gotchas.md:104 says it holds the speech-therapy telehealth text. When the user typed a number, order typed documents ahead of alias documents. 7. **policyFamilyKey.** Tighten the string rule: strip a trailing -digits suffix only when the remainder is clinical-shaped (/^\d+-?[A-Z]$/, e.g. 3L-1 to 3L, 11A-14 to 11A); otherwise the full number is its own family (MA-2180, 42-CFR-440, 21-0002, 1-O-1). Replace the tests that pin 'MA' (document-selection.test.ts:131, 147, 170-175) with a table over real stored numbers. 8. **Verification.** Re-run golden q-036 and the pharmacy questions. The '9' alias currently pulls 9A/9B/9D/9E through the prefix match and will stop doing so, and typed 1-H will now scope retrieval.
- **How it was verified:** All four defects are real; the only error is the worked 3L example, which does not misroute in the current local graph. I read the cited code and its callers, ran the pure functions in a throwaway tsx script, and ran the lookup predicate and ORDER BY read-only against the local Neo4j graph (the reviewer had not executed Cypher). (1) Lookup. The quoted lines are verbatim. The predicate is exact / case-insensitive / STARTS WITH, and the ORDER BY has no exact-match key. `candidatePolicyNumbers` only rewrites CFR and "SSA Title <digits>" forms, so '1-H', 'Policy 8C', 'MA 2180' and 'SSA Title XIX' pass through unchanged; the graph stores '1H'. `routing.ts:140` takes element [0], and no caller guard checks that the returned policy number equals the requested one. Live result: for every distinct policy number among routing document types, I checked whether the first row returned is the request…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted: the core defect is real in the live graph and is not documented, tracked or an accepted trade-off. Several of the reviewer's examples are wrong or overstated, so the claim needs correcting, but the severity holds. What I opened: policy-lookup.ts, routing.ts, query-intent.ts, analysis.ts, document-selection.ts, the coverage pipeline, gotchas.md, the agent lookup tool, the saved 2026-09-22 gate, and the local Neo4j graph (read-only, running the exact WHERE/ORDER BY from lookupPolicy). **What survives** - In the live graph, the first row returned for six real clinical policies is a different policy: 8A-1 (Assertive Community Treatment) returns 8A-12 (Substance Abuse Intensive Outp…

### r2-critic-policy-number-bakeoff-01 — Neither round-one patch is sufficient alone; one combined patch fixes the three highs

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:62`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** recommendation · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** True in substance, with these corrections. (1) combined.patch does not apply with `git apply` as written: the new file internal/policy-number.ts lacks a /dev/null header. The other ten files apply cleanly to HEAD, so the new file must be created separately or the patch regenerated. (2) "18 question-type flips" is for widened extraction plus both forms; with HEAD extraction plus both forms the figure is 15 of 155. (3) "8h" prefix-matches six 8H-x policies, five of them titled 1915(i) and one (8H-5) titled Community Living and Supports. (4) All anchor examples and counts describe the local July 2026 graph with embeddings stubbed to empty. "Removes every measured wrong-policy anchor" is limited to the reviewer's 62 look-alike and 155 benchmark questions and is not a production measurement; the replay totals (674-number lookup, rerank pairs, frame replay 92 to 98) are reviewer-measured and were not independently re-run.
- **Evidence:** HEAD code: packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:445 `...(query.match(/\b\d+[A-Za-z](?:-[A-Za-z0-9]+)?\b/g) ?? []),`; packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:62-65 `WHERE any(pn IN $pns WHERE` / `d.policy_number = pn` / `OR toLower(d.policy_number) = toLower(pn)` / `OR d.policy_number STARTS WITH pn)`; packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/rerank.ts:252 `if (queryMentionsPolicyNumber(context.queryLower, hit.policyNumber)) {`; packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:301 `return explicitPolicies.length >= 2 || queryLower.includes('difference between') || queryLower.includes('vs ');`. Measured (scripts in SCRATCHDIR): 62 look-alike questions, questions with a token that resolves to a document under the current lookup: HEAD 27, cue-only 1, widened 29, combined 0. With HEAD extraction, lookups still resolve for 27 under exact-first with prefix fallback, 27 under hyphen family, 17 under exact key. Benchmark (155): cue-only changes 2 (h-043, j-007: typed number lost, alias keeps it); widened changes 6; widened plus both forms changes 21 with 18 question-type flips; combined changes 7 with 0 flips and 0 golden policies lost. Lookup replay over 674 stored numbers: current rule 6 wrong-first, 10 multi-policy, 73% synthetic rows; combined 0 / 0 / 0 nothing, 674 of 674 identical between the real patched lookupPolicy and the replayed rule. policy_scoped backend on the real graph: "Does NC Medicaid cover 3D mammograms?" injects 35 Hospice sections at…
- **Impact:** With unmodified code on the local graph, "Does NC Medicaid cover 3D mammograms?" produces a frame whose authoritative document is 3D Hospice Services and whose primary sections are Hospice 1.0 and "4.1 General Criteria Not Covered". "stage 3b kidney disease" anchors on PACE, "stage 4B cancer" on Orthodontic Services, "8h private duty nursing shifts" on six 1915(i) policies, and "Does Medicaid cover Assertive Community Treatment?" on 8A-12 Substance Abuse Intensive Outpatient. Applying only one reviewer's patch leaves some of these in place or, for the extraction-only fix, moves questions onto the inferred path where finding 3 applies.
- **Fix:** Adopt `combined.patch` as the base, with four changes before merging: 1. **Recover the common uncued phrasings.** Add a small set of positive cues for bare tokens: - a preceding "in", "per", "of", "according to", "compare" or "section N of"; - a following "cover(s)", "say(s)", "state(s)", "require(s)", "section" or "policy"; - two bare tokens joined by "vs" or "versus" qualifying each other. Re-run the reviewer's 62 look-alike set and keep only cues that leave its resolving count at 0. 2. **Tighten or drop the "under" cue.** Accept "under 3L" only when the token ends the clause or is followed by punctuation or a policy-style word, so "under 3d imaging" no longer anchors on Hospice. 3. **Ship tests with the patch.** Convert the 54 scratch checks in `patch-checks.mts` into a table test for `internal/policy-number.ts`, including the look-alikes (3D mammogram, stage 3b, 8h shifts) and the phrasings in point 1. Update `tests/policy-lookup.test.ts` to assert the exact-key query and the `change_notice` exclusion. Rename the stale "most common policy family" case in `tests/internal/document-selection.test.ts`. 4. **Gate on current data.** Re-run the key-collision query and the "`change_notice` rows have no sections" query against the current corpus release, since the measurements are from the July 2026 local build. Then run the paid hybrid eval once, as the reviewer says. Keep the reviewer's do-not-adopt list: the ORDER BY key, both-forms emission, `matchType`, the alias split and the tightened `policyFamilyKey`.
- **How it was verified:** The finding holds in substance: the mechanisms are real at HEAD, no guard prevents them, and the local graph has the documents that the look-alike tokens hit. I reproduced the core behaviour independently; the larger replay counts are the reviewer's own measurements, which I checked only for consistency. **Confirmed by my own checks** - **Quoted code:** all four quotes are verbatim at HEAD (query-intent.ts:445 and :301, policy-lookup.ts:62-65, rerank.ts:252). - **No guard between extraction and lookup:** routing.ts `collectLookupDocuments` (lines 39-45) passes every extracted number straight to `lookupPolicy` and flattens all rows. `collectCoverageRoutingDocuments` returns those rows before any inference from hits. `collectPolicyDocumentsFromHits` (line 140) takes the first row of a prefix lookup. policy-scoped.ts filters on `analysis.explicitPolicyNumbers` with no cue or corpus check. …
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted. The finding is a reconciliation of three round-one highs, not a repeat, and nothing in the project context makes it dismissible. I did not re-run the reviewer's measurements; that is another lens. What I opened: the digest entries for corpus-frames-01, corpus-frames-02, corpus-retrieval-04, neo4j-live-02 and neo4j-live-11; query-intent.ts, policy-lookup.ts, rerank.ts and the agent lookup tool at HEAD; gotchas.md and docs/decisions; the open issue list; the reviewer's combined.patch; and the local Neo4j graph (read-only). 1. **Not a duplicate.** Round one left the owner three high findings whose improved fixes contradict each other. corpus-frames-02 keeps the prefix clause and a…

### r2-critic-policy-number-bakeoff-02 — The exact-first ORDER BY key recommended in corpus-frames-02 crashes lookupPolicy on the pinned Neo4j 2026.05

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:76`
- **Severity:** medium (reviewer proposed high) · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim holds as stated, with two refinements. (1) The planner error on Neo4j 2026.05.0 (`AssertionError: Expected a sorted plan but got ...`) occurs whenever the `any(pn IN $pns WHERE ...)` ORDER BY key follows an aggregating or DISTINCT projection, not only `collect(...)`; `RETURN DISTINCT` triggers it too. (2) In my reproduction the reported plan begins with `.aggregation(...)`, not `.apply()`. Reproduced on the full lookupPolicy statement: the current statement returns 25 rows, the statement with the round-one key fails, and the IN-list form returns 25 rows. Today's code is unaffected; the defect is in the corpus-frames-02 patch as written. The 13-of-14 frame replay figure and the `WITH ... AS exactRank` workaround were not independently rerun.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:70 `collect({id: s.id, number: s.section_number, title: s.title}) AS sections,` and :76 `ORDER BY`. docker-compose.yml:5 `image: neo4j:2026.05-community` (the same tag is in .github/workflows/ci.yml:31 and docs/operations/blue-green-corpus-releases.md:14-15). Running the lookup statement with that key added, read-only, with $pns = ['1A-2']: `50N00: general processing exception - internal error. Internal exception raised AssertionError: Expected a sorted plan but got .apply() ... .aggregation(Seq("` d@1`.id AS id", ...), Seq("collect({id: s.id, number: s.section_number, title: s.title}) AS ` UNNAMED5`"))`. A minimal form fails the same way: `... RETURN d.id AS id, d.policy_number AS policyNumber, count(s) AS n ORDER BY CASE WHEN any(pn IN $pns WHERE toLower(d.policy_number) = toLower(pn)) THEN 0 ELSE 1 END, d.id ASC LIMIT 2`. The same ORDER BY without aggregation works; a scalar comparison works; `toLower(d.policy_number) IN [x IN $pns | toLower(x)]` works; computing the flag in a `WITH d, CASE ... AS exactRank` before the OPTIONAL MATCHes works. packages/policy-corpus/tests/policy-lookup.test.ts:44 `const run = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({ records: [] });`.
- **Impact:** If the round-one patch is applied as written, every lookupPolicy call throws against a real database: the agent's lookupPolicy tool, every frame for a question with a typed or aliased number, and every inferred-routing lookup. In the frame replay with only that change, all 13 frames that reached a lookup failed with this error. CI's unit tests would be green.
- **Fix:** 1. **Make the WHERE exact and add no ordering key.** In `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts`, replace the three-way predicate with `WHERE any(pn IN $pns WHERE toLower(d.policy_number) = toLower(pn))`. Keep the existing ORDER BY and LIMIT. Verified on Neo4j 2026.05.0: it runs, returns 1A-2 first for "1A-2", and leaves the 42-CFR-440 lookup unchanged. This replaces Step 1 of corpus-frames-02 (both the ORDER BY key and the TypeScript post-filter). No test depends on the old WHERE text. 2. **Accept the behaviour change knowingly.** A partial number such as "1A" or "3K" will return nothing instead of up to 25 family rows. No caller or prompt relies on that. If a family fallback is ever wanted, compute the flag before aggregation (`WITH d, CASE ... AS exactRank`) or use `toLower(d.policy_number) IN [x IN $pns | toLower(x)]`. Both run on 2026.05.0. Never put `any()` in an ORDER BY that follows an aggregating RETURN. 3. **Extend the existing integration test; do not add a new opt-in file.** In `packages/agent/tests/integration/neo4j-tools.integration.test.ts`, which CI already runs against `neo4j:2026.05-community`, add two fixture documents with unique sibling numbers, for example `TEST-PN-2` (older effective date) and `TEST-PN-27` (newer). Assert that `lookupPolicy('TEST-PN-2')` returns only the `TEST-PN-2` document. This guards both the statement failing and the sibling order. 4. **Keep the rest of the bakeoff patch separate.** Key normalisation ("1-H" to "1H") and excluding `document_type = 'change_notice'` rows belong to that reviewer's finding 1 and should be judged there. Neither is needed to fix the sibling bug or avoid the planner error. 5. **Optional:** add one line to `docs/development/gotchas.md` recording the planner error, so the ORDER BY form is not reintroduced.
- **How it was verified:** I could not refute it; I reproduced it on the local Neo4j 2026.05.0 container, so the claim is confirmed in substance. What I opened: - `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts` (whole file): the quoted lines 70 and 76 are there, and the RETURN aggregates with `collect(...) AS sections` before the ORDER BY. - The round-one corpus-frames-02 fix text: it does recommend exactly that `CASE WHEN any(pn IN $pns WHERE toLower(d.policy_number) = toLower(pn)) THEN 0 ELSE 1 END` as the first ORDER BY key, with the WHERE clause unchanged. - `packages/policy-corpus/tests/policy-lookup.test.ts:44`: the session is mocked, so unit tests cannot see a failing statement. - Image tags: `neo4j:2026.05-community` in `docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.ab.yml`, `ci.yml` and the blue-green operations doc. What I ran (read-only, `$pns = ['1A-2']`), using the real statement text extracted from …
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted, but high is not defensible; medium. ### r2-critic-policy-number-bakeoff-07 — A form number with a letter suffix is read as two policy numbers, routes to the policy-variant pipeline and looks up nothing

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:445`
- **Severity:** low · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** A DHHS form number with a letter suffix (for example DHB-5125B) is extracted twice by extractExplicitPolicyNumbers, as "5125B" by the clinical pattern and as "DHB-5125B" by the manual pattern. Two numbers make isPolicyVariantQuery true, so a single-form question is typed policy_variant. Neither token matches a stored number, because the loader stores only PREFIX-NNNN (DHB-5125), so the exact-number lookup returns nothing. The policy-variant pipeline then falls back to documents inferred from a 16-hit search, so the frame is usually not empty; it is built with variant-comparison framing and without the exact-number anchor. A model lookupPolicy call for the number as printed returns []. "MA 2180" and "MA2180" are not extracted or resolved at all; that part is already in corpus-retrieval-04. Lookup counts were measured on the local, older graph; the extraction and question-type behaviour is HEAD source.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:445-446 `...(query.match(/\b\d+[A-Za-z](?:-[A-Za-z0-9]+)?\b/g) ?? []),` / `...(query.match(/\b(?:MA|DMA|DHB|DSS)-\d+[A-Za-z0-9-]*\b/gi) ?? []),`; :300-301 `const explicitPolicies = extractExplicitPolicyNumbers(queryLower);` / `return explicitPolicies.length >= 2 || ...`. packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/loader.py:313-315 `_DHHS_POLICY_NUMBER_RE = re.compile(` / `r"^((?:MA|DMA|DHB|DSS)-\d+)"`. Run on HEAD: "Where do I send form DHB-5125B after a transportation no-show?" -> extractExplicitPolicyNumbers = ["5125B","DHB-5125B"], analyzeQuery(...).questionType = 'policy_variant', lookups return nothing. The same happens for all 12 stored numbers whose document title carries a single-letter suffix (DHB-2040B, DHB-5001N, DHB-5008A/B/E, DHB-5125A/B, DHB-5150A/B, DHB-7078A/R, DMA-5202D). "MA 2180" and "MA2180" are extracted for 0 of 251 stored manual numbers; lookupPolicy('MA 2180') returns nothing.
- **Impact:** A caseworker who asks about a notice by its printed form number gets a frame from the policy-variant pipeline with no looked-up document, and the model's lookupPolicy call for the number as printed comes back empty.
- **Fix:** Fold this into the corpus-retrieval-04 / corpus-frames-01 patch rather than shipping a separate one. 1. Extraction: bound the bare clinical pattern so it cannot match inside a longer token (for example a negative lookbehind for [A-Za-z0-9-]), or rely on the cue-word rule already accepted for corpus-frames-01, which also stops "5125B" being extracted. Do not apply both that rule and a separate "one or two leading digits" limit without reconciling them. 2. Manual and form numbers: accept a hyphen, a space or nothing after MA/DMA/DHB/DSS, and derive the stored key PREFIX-digits. Use \d{3,5} or the loader's \d+ with a boundary, not exactly four digits. Do this in candidatePolicyNumbers too, so the lookupPolicy tool resolves "DHB-5125B", "MA 2180" and "MA2180". 3. Keep the typed suffix. Reducing to DHB-5125 returns six documents (base, a, Asp, B, Bsp, sp). Add an ORDER BY key, or a post-filter in collectLookupDocuments, that prefers documents whose title starts with the full typed token, case-insensitive and followed by whitespace. Otherwise the base notice or a Spanish variant can outrank the form the user asked about. 4. Tests: assert that one suffixed form number yields exactly one extracted number, questionType is not policy_variant, and the first lookup document's title starts with the typed number. Use DHB-5125B and DHB-5008A. Longer term, storing the full form number in the loader would remove the title tie-break, but it needs a graph reload and is not required for this fix.
- **How it was verified:** Not refuted; severity low stands. One correction to the impact and one to the recommendation. (1) Is it true? Yes, reproduced on HEAD. - I opened packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts, primitives/policy-lookup.ts, frame/pipelines/policy-variant.ts, frame/context/routing.ts and the loader regex in packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/loader.py. - A throwaway tsx script importing the HEAD source showed a suffixed form number yields two tokens and detectAnswerQuestionType returns policy_variant. Unsuffixed numbers (DHB-5097, MA-2180) yield one token and stay general. - "MA 2180" and "MA2180" yield no tokens. - The loader stores only the PREFIX-digits head of the title. In the local graph (older build) all 371 MA/DMA/DHB/DSS documents, 251 distinct numbers, are PREFIX plus four digits. - Running lookupPolicy's own WHERE clause read-only against that graph returned 0 rows for D…

## RETR-2 · Alias rules force the wrong policy for over-the-counter, outpatient therapy and CAP/Choice questions

Severity high · effort S

### r2-domain-facts-01 — Over-the-counter questions are pinned to Policy 9 and its exclusion list; Policy 9A, which covers OTC products, never enters the frame

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:488`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The alias table (`query-intent.ts:235-249`, `:487-488`) maps over-the-counter medication and drug wording ('over-the-counter medication(s)', 'over the counter medication(s)', 'otc drug(s)', 'otc medication(s)') to policy number 9. It does not match 'over-the-counter products' or a product name on its own. For matching queries, `collectCoverageRoutingDocuments` returns the alias lookup documents before retrieval hits are considered. `lookupPolicy('9')` matches by prefix, so it does return 9A, 9B, 9D and 9E as candidates. `scoreVariantDocument` then gives the alias-derived '9' the +2.5 exact-match bonus (3.10 against 0.69 for 9A; without the bonus 9A would rank first), and the coverage frame is scoped to Policy 9 alone. On the local July 2026 graph with embeddings disabled, the frame is PRIMARY 9 § 1.0 and 9 § 4.1, SUPPORTING 9 § 4.0, 4.2, 4.2.1 and 4.2.2. Policy 9 § 3.2.1 and every 9A section are absent. On that build 9A is also marked `answer_ready = false`, which may separately keep its sections out. The saved q-032 answer cites only Policy 9. That question names Policy 9 and asks about non-prescription fertility supplements, so its 'No' is correct; it shows the missing 9A exception, not a wrong answer. A wrong 'No' for prescribed Claritin or Prilosec is inferred, not observed. The live 9A policy (amended 07/01/2026) still lists proton pump inhibitors, second-generation antihistamines and nicotine smoking deterrents as covered OTC classes.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:241-248 `'over-the-counter medication',` ... `'otc drug',` `'otc drugs',` `'otc medication',` `'otc medications',`; :487-488 `if (isOutpatientPharmacyPolicyQuery(queryLower)) {` / `policyNumbers.push('9');`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/document-selection.ts:108-110 `if (document.policyNumber && policyNumbers.includes(document.policyNumber.toUpperCase())) {` / `score += 2.5;` / `reason = 'Exact policy-number match from the query.';`. packages/agent/src/prompt.ts:20 `open with the definitive answer ("No." / "Yes.") — do not hedge`. Local graph, Policy 9 section 3.2.1: `Selected OTC products including insulin are covered when they meet the criteria listed in clinical coverage policy 9A, Over the Counter Products`; section 4.2.1: `Medicaid shall not cover the following: OTC products. Refer to Subsection 3.2.1(b)`. Policy 9A section 1.0: `Selected non-legend, over-the-counter (OTC) products may be covered as an optional benefit`; 9A Attachment B lists `Smoking deterrent agents (nicotine)`, `Proton pump inhibitors`, `Second generation antihistamines`. Probe, 'Does NC Medicaid cover over-the-counter medications?': `authoritative: 9 "Lock-in Program" [clinical_policy] (Exact policy-number match from the query.)`; `PRIMARY: 9 § 1.0; 9 § 4.1`; `SUPPORTING: 9 § 4.0; 9 § 4.2; 9 § 4.2.1; 9 § 4.2.2`; checklist `For this yes/no coverage question, answer yes or no first, then cite both the scope section § 1.0 and the not-covered se…
- **Impact:** A case manager asks whether Claritin or Prilosec is covered when a doctor writes a prescription. The frame offers only Policy 9: its description, the generic not-covered template and an exclusion list that begins with OTC products. The prompt and the plan tell the model to open with a definite yes or no and to answer from the frame before widening. The likely answer is 'No', cited to Policy 9 section 4.2.1, and the verifier accepts it because the cited text says so. The correct answer is yes when prescribed, because second-generation antihistamines and proton pump inhibitors are on the 9A list. No model was run, so the wrong final answer is inferred; the saved q-032 answer shows the model does repeat the exclusion without the exception.
- **Fix:** 1. In packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts, split isOutpatientPharmacyPolicyQuery. The eight OTC phrases (keep the existing narrow list) push '9A' only. 'outpatient pharmacy', 'pharmacy benefit', 'pharmacy claims' and 'prescription drug(s)' keep pushing '9'. Keep the Medicare Part D exclusion on both. Do not emit 9 and 9A together for OTC wording: the frame keeps one preferred document and the two scores tie or differ by 0.09. 2. Drop the proposed change to scoreVariantDocument. Gating the +2.5 on extractExplicitPolicyNumbers removes it from "Policy 9", "Policy 15" and "Policy 7" and flips golden q-032 to 9A. If a typed-versus-alias distinction is wanted later, use queryMentionsPolicyNumber, which is token-bounded, and only after extraction handles letterless numbers. 3. Tests: - Flip tests/internal/query-intent.test.ts:75-77 to expect '9A' for the OTC question. - Add a case that "Does Policy 9 (Outpatient Pharmacy) cover over-the-counter fertility..." still yields '9'. - Add one frame test for "Does NC Medicaid cover over-the-counter medications?" requiring 9A as the authoritative document with a 9A section primary. Do not require Policy 9 section 3.2.1. - Add that question to the held-out set, not the tuned benchmark. 4. Replace the load-time graph check with a static unit-test table listing each alias row beside its official policy title (3K-1, 3K-2, 10A, 8C, 15, 9, 9A, MA-2180), reviewed once against the NC Medicaid policy listing. 5. Sequencing: land this with or before the corpus-retrieval-04 lookup fix. Once prefix matching goes, the alias is the only route by which 9A enters the frame.
- **How it was verified:** The finding is true in substance and I reproduced it. The frame result is measured on the local July 2026 graph with embeddings disabled; the scoping mechanism is from the current code on main. What I opened and found: 1. **Alias row.** `query-intent.ts:235-249` lists the OTC medication/drug phrases inside `isOutpatientPharmacyPolicyQuery`, and `:487-488` pushes `'9'`. A test pins this behaviour (`tests/internal/query-intent.test.ts:76-77`). 2. **Mechanism.** It is as described, and I confirmed it more directly than the finding did. - `collectCoverageRoutingDocuments` (`routing.ts:154-158`) returns the alias lookup documents before any retrieval hits are considered, so scoping does not depend on retrieval mode. - `lookupPolicy('9')` matches by `STARTS WITH`, so it returns 9, 9A, 9B, 9D, 9E and their change notices. Policy 9A is therefore a candidate. - `scoreVariantDocument` then gives …
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted. The finding matters for this project at high severity, and the saved release run supports it more strongly than the reviewer claimed. **What I opened** - `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts` and `document-selection.ts` - The golden, held-out and Jev paraphrase datasets - The 2026-09-22 milestone `final-gate-full.json` (q-032 and q-040, including the saved evidence sources) - The round-one digest: index, rejected list, and corpus-retrieval-04, corpus-retrieval-07, rag-design-06, architecture-01, pipeline-eval-01 - `docs/development/gotchas.md`, `docs/operations/answer-coverage-scope.md`, the validation READMEs - Open GitHub issues (read-only search) - Local Neo4…

### r2-triage-corpus-03 — Alias rules hard-wire the wrong policy: "outpatient therapy" forces Policy 10A although the corpus uses the phrase only for behavioural health, and "cap/c" also matches CAP/Choice, CAP/CD and CAP/CH

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:462`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** extractAliasedPolicyNumbers (query-intent.ts:450-495) maps bare substrings to policy numbers, and analysis.ts:54-58 and retrieval-planning.ts:77-81 merge them into the explicit policy numbers. Two rules misroute ordinary wording. 1. "outpatient therapy" or "outpatient therapies" forces Policy 10A. In the parsed corpus the phrase occurs only in behavioural-health policies (8C, 8A-1, 8A-12, 8A-13, 8D-3, 8D-4, 8D-5) and never in 10A. 2. "cap/c" forces Policy 3K-1 and also matches CAP/Choice (47 mentions) and CAP/CD (26 mentions), both CAP/DA consumer-direction options defined in Policy 3K-2. 3K-1 never says CAP/Choice. Those questions get 3K-1 and not 3K-2. CAP/CH (38 mentions) is also matched, but the corpus does not show it to be a CAP/DA option. It appears in EOB crosswalks, an SPA and nursing-facility forms, where it reads as the children's programme, so 3K-1 may be correct for that token. Drop CAP/CH from the list of wrong matches. Aliased numbers drive the always-enabled policy_scoped backend, the exact-policy-number boost, and coverage routing, which returns lookup documents before anything inferred from hits. Once one hit from the aliased policy is present, coverage.ts:71-74 discards all other hits. On the local (older) graph that hit is available: 37 of 79 sections in 10A and 86 of 336 in 3K-1 contain a search term from the example questions. The frame-level outcome (10A or 3K-1 as the only authoritative document) was shown on fixtures; the agent was not run.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:458-459 `if (queryLower.includes('cap/c')) {` `policyNumbers.push('3K-1');` | :462-463 `if (queryLower.includes('outpatient therapy') || queryLower.includes('outpatient therapies')) {` `policyNumbers.push('10A');` | packages/policy-corpus/src/query/analysis.ts:54 `const aliasedPolicyNumbers = federalAuthorityQuery ? [] : extractAliasedPolicyNumbers(normalizedQuery);` and :57 `...aliasedPolicyNumbers,` inside explicitPolicyNumbers | packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/policy-scoped.ts:57 `AND toUpper(coalesce(d.policy_number, '')) IN $policyNumbers` and :74 `WHERE sectionScore > 0` | packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/rerank.ts:255-256 `if (hit.policyNumber && context.exactPolicyNumbers.includes(hit.policyNumber.toUpperCase())) {` `score += EXACT_POLICY_NUMBER_BOOST;` | packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts:154-155 `const lookupDocuments = await collectLookupDocuments(query);` `if (lookupDocuments.length > 0) {` | packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/coverage.ts:71-74 `const scoped = hits.filter(` `(hit) => hit.policyNumber && preferredPolicyNumbers.has(hit.policyNumber.toUpperCase()),` `);` `return uniqueBy([...(scoped.length > 0 ? scoped : hits), ...supplementalCandidateHits], (hit) => hit.sectionId);` | packages/pipeline/data/parsed/clinical-policies: 'outpatient therapy' count is 5 in the 8C file, 1 each in 8a-1, 8a-12 and 8a-13, present in 8d-3, 8d-4 and 8d-5, and 0 in 10a_clinical-coverag…
- **Impact:** "Does NC Medicaid cover outpatient therapy for depression?" In a probe on the repo's fixtures: - The frame's only authoritative document was 10A. - Primary sections were 10A 1.0 and "4.1 General Criteria Not Covered", with cite_scope_and_not_covered true. - The 8C psychotherapy section, the top hit at 0.97, was not in the frame at all. - The generated plan named 10A as the only preferred source, told the model to answer yes or no first, and told it to say so plainly if the policy governs a different service category. That is a short path to a cited "no" for a service Policy 8C covers. "Does NC Medicaid cover personal assistance services under CAP/Choice?" produced a frame made only of the children's waiver. Two caveats. Without any hit from the aliased policy, the right sections survive as supporting evidence (first probe). The claim that production always supplies such a hit is read from policy-scoped.ts (a lexical match on any search term inside the aliased policy, plus a 0.2 boost), not run. The agent was not run.
- **Fix:** Keep the change inside extractAliasedPolicyNumbers (packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:451) and its unit tests. Drop steps 3 and 5. 1. Replace the "outpatient therapy" / "outpatient therapies" rule with the phrase Policy 10A actually uses: "outpatient specialized therap". Leave the telehealth + speech/audiology rule as is. 2. Replace `includes('cap/c')` with a bounded match that keeps the children's waiver names: `/cap\/c(?:hildren)?(?![a-z])/` -> 3K-1. 3. Add "cap/choice" to the existing CAP/DA rule -> 3K-2. Do not alias CAP/CD or CAP/CH; let retrieval handle them. 4. Add unit assertions in tests/internal/query-intent.test.ts, in the existing landmine block: - "outpatient therapy for depression" does not contain 10A. - "CAP/Choice", "CAP/CD" and "CAP/CH" do not contain 3K-1. - "CAP/Choice" contains 3K-2. - "CAP/C" and "CAP/Children" contain 3K-1. 5. On the next scheduled eval run, check golden q-004 and held-out paraphrase line 184, which lose their 10A anchor. If they regress, do not restore the bare phrase. Add a narrow positive rule instead (for example "outpatient therapy" together with physical, occupational, speech or audiology), or accept inference from hits. Removing aliases from `explicitPolicyNumbers` wholesale should wait for the golden regression gate proposed in corpus-retrieval-07, and would need a real candidate-only path built first. This fix is independent of corpus-frames-01, which edits extractExplicitPolicyNumbers.
- **How it was verified:** The claim holds in substance; one sub-detail (CAP/CH) is not supported by the corpus. What I opened: query-intent.ts (extractAliasedPolicyNumbers), query/analysis.ts, internal/retrieval-planning.ts, retrieval/backends/policy-scoped.ts, profiles/retrieval-signals.ts, internal/rerank.ts, frame/context/routing.ts, frame/pipelines/coverage.ts, the parsed policy files, the reviewer's probe script and output, and the local Neo4j graph (read-only). What I found: - **Quoted code is all present as cited.** The alias rules are bare `includes()` substring tests with no token boundary and no negative guard. The only bypass is a federal-authority query. - **Aliases become explicit numbers.** I ran `analyzeQuery` myself: the depression question yields explicit=["10A"], and the CAP/Choice, CAP/CD and CAP/CH questions all yield explicit=["3K-1"] and never 3K-2. - **Nothing filters the alias downstream.…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted; high severity stands, on the same footing as corpus-frames-01 (wrong-policy frame demonstrated on fixtures, final agent answer not run). **What I opened** - `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts` (alias rules), `query/analysis.ts`, `internal/retrieval-planning.ts`, `retrieval/backends/policy-scoped.ts`, `frame/context/routing.ts`, `frame/pipelines/coverage.ts`. - `docs/development/gotchas.md`, `docs/decisions` (grep), the round-one digest (index, rejected, corpus-frames and corpus-retrieval scope files), the golden and held-out datasets, and the parsed clinical policies. - I did not re-run the reviewer's probes; the code paths they rest on read as described. **Is…

## RETR-3 · Coverage frames fill slots with template sections and strip the answering section's text

Severity high · effort M

### corpus-frames-03 — The avoid list is the top leftover hits, and yes/no frames lead with template scope plus "General Criteria Not Covered"

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/coverage.ts:268`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The core claim stands: `buildSectionCandidates` only orders hits, the avoid list is the top three leftover hits whether or not they match an avoid term, and yes/no frames lead with a template scope section plus "4.1 General Criteria Not Covered". Three details are wrong. 1. In the HEAD probe the primary scope section is "2.1.1 General", not "1.0 Description". 2. Section 5.8 does not end up in `avoidSections`. `rebalanceCoverageFrame` pools the avoid sections back in and re-emits 5.8 inside `supportingSections`, still with content null and the avoid reason. Saved runs show the same leak: 10 primary and 5 supporting sections with avoid-derived reasons and no content across 250 frames. 3. The Policy 9 "5.1 Prior Authorization" example (h-018) is a `prior_authorization` frame, so it comes from the equivalent avoid construction in `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/prior-auth.ts:50`, not from `coverage.ts:268`. One condition applies: the profiles fill all four primary slots only when the preferred document has at least four title-matching template sections, which is typical for NC clinical coverage policies.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/coverage.ts:268-274 `const avoidCandidates = buildSectionCandidates(` `candidateHits,` `[...PRIOR_AUTH_AVOID_TERMS, ...COVERAGE_AVOID_TERMS, ...(flags.telehealthQuery ? flags.telehealthAvoidTerms : [])],` `[],` `3,` `'Tangential or process-focused section to avoid unless explicitly requested.',` packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/section-views.ts:58-59 `// Score is only used to order the candidates emitted here; everything` / `// downstream of this function selects positionally.`
- **Impact:** Probe at HEAD: a 9-section NC template policy, question "Does NC Medicaid cover therapeutic leave for nursing facility residents?", top hit "5.8 Therapeutic Leave" (score 0.97). The frame's primary sections were "1.0 Description" and "4.1 General Criteria Not Covered". Section 5.8, which holds the answer, came back with content null and the reason "Tangential or process-focused section to avoid unless explicitly requested." In the last five saved full runs, 25 of 241 avoid sections matched no avoid term at all. Others matched only body substrings: HCWD "B. Premiums" was in avoid with "Matched phone." for a premiums question, and Policy 9 "5.1 Prior Authorization" was in avoid for a prior-authorization question. The frame design presumes the answer to a yes/no question is "no", which fits the 17 golden negative-answer questions and not positive ones. Golden primary sections landed in avoid in only 2 of 250 recorded frames; the more common failure is absence (finding 11).
- **Fix:** Keep scope + "General Criteria Not Covered" in primary (commit 9c45426 and the verifier's cite_scope_and_not_covered hint depend on it) and add a third slot instead of swapping one out. 1. **Avoid nomination.** In `coverage.ts:268` and `prior-auth.ts:50`, either delete the generic `buildSectionCandidates(candidateHits, avoidTerms, ...)` call or filter hits to those whose section title contains an avoid term before calling it. Do not use a word-bounded body match. Leave the classified avoid groups in `assembly.ts` alone. Both options passed the 21 existing coverage frame tests in a scratch copy; I did not run the prior-auth tests. 2. **Primary slots.** In `initial-selection.ts`, make primaryLimit 3 when `flags.yesNoCoverageQuery` and service focus terms exist. In `coverage.ts`, put the top seed hit whose title matches a service focus term ahead of `documentPrimaryHydrated` in primaryCandidates, so it is hydrated with content. In the scratch probe this gave primary 1.0, 4.1, 5.8 with all 21 tests passing. I did not test the reviewer's "service-specific section retrieved" guard separately. 3. **Regression and validation.** Add the therapeutic-leave probe as a test asserting 5.8 is in primarySections with non-null content and is not in avoidSections. Run the answer eval on the yes/no subset, positive and negative, before merging; the fixture tests cannot show whether a third primary section changes answer quality.

### corpus-frames-08 — Rebalance pools content-stripped avoid sections back into primary and supporting, shipping evidence with content: null

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/rebalance/coverage.ts:21`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim stands as written, with two corrections. First, the frame-selection files are not wholly unchanged since the eval commit 210ced2: six files under `frame/` and `internal/` changed (69 insertions), but `finalize.ts` and the three `rebalance/*` modules are untouched and a probe at HEAD reproduces the promotion. Second, the "last five saved full runs" date from 2026-07-09/10 on the heldout-paraphrase set. The 15-in-250 count is exact (h-009 supporting, h-023 primary, h-026 primary in each of five runs).
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/rebalance/coverage.ts:20-23 `const surfacedSections = uniqueBy(` `[...frame.primarySections, ...frame.supportingSections, ...frame.avoidSections],` (same pooling at rebalance/federal-authority.ts:133 and rebalance/prior-auth.ts:19) packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/finalize.ts:83-85 `const avoidSections = (await hydrateSectionCandidates(avoidCandidates, FRAME_AVOID_LIMIT, seenSectionIds)).map(` `(candidate) => ({ ...candidate.section, content: null }),`
- **Impact:** In the last five saved full runs, 15 primary or supporting sections across 250 frames had an avoid-derived reason and no content, deterministically for 3 of 50 questions. h-023 and h-026 each had a primary section with reason "Matched contact." and no text. h-009 (HCWD) had a change notice in supporting with the avoid fallback reason and no text. Probes at HEAD reproduce it. In a waiver-application frame, "441.304 Duration, extension, and amendment of a waiver" was an avoid candidate because its body mentions public notice; it was promoted to primary with content null, because the waiver hint's `!includesPhrase(text, WAIVER_APPLICATION_AVOID_TERMS)` check ran on stripped text. The verifier ignores sections without content, so the slot is wasted and the plan's "Cover first" line points at text the model has not read. tests/frame/finalize.test.ts pins the stripping, but nothing asserts that surfaced evidence has content.
- **Fix:** Take the first alternative only, and do it at the single choke point. 1. **Move the strip.** Remove `content: null` from `finalizeFrame` (`packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/finalize.ts:83-85`), keeping the shared seen-set. Apply `avoidSections.map(s => ({ ...s, content: null }))` once in `createEvidencePlanner.resolveAnswerFrame` (`packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/evidence-plan.ts:35-40`), after `resolvePlannedFrame` returns. This covers every pipeline, lets rebalance predicates see real text, keeps promoted sections' bodies, and also strips the sections rebalance demotes into avoid. 2. **Do not exclude avoid from the rebalance pools.** That leaves demoted avoid sections shipping with content and removes rebalance's ability to rescue a mis-bucketed candidate. 3. **Fix the tests.** - Move the pinned assertion from `tests/frame/finalize.test.ts` to a planner-level test. - Add one shared assertion helper for the frames-*.test.ts results: every avoid section has null content, every primary or supporting section has non-empty content, and no id appears in two buckets. - Add one fixture that reproduces a promotion, for example the waiver-application frame where 441.304 is an avoid candidate. Without it the invariant is vacuous: existing fixtures have zero content-less surfaced sections at HEAD. 4. **Drop the "carries an avoid reason" assertion.** `reason` is a free string with no bucket marker. If the stale label matters, overwrite `reason` when rebalance moves a section between buckets.
- **How it was verified:** The claim is true: I could not find a guard that stops it, a probe at HEAD reproduces it, and the saved runs match the stated counts exactly. **What I read** - `finalize.ts`: avoid sections are hydrated and then mapped to `content: null`. - All three rebalance modules: each builds its pool from primary, supporting and avoid together. All three pipelines call rebalance immediately after `finalizeFrame` and return the result. - `service.ts` diff since the eval commit: the only change is an early-return coverage-scope path. Nothing re-hydrates or drops content-less primary or supporting sections after rebalance. - Agent side: `listSections` (used for the "Cover first" line) does not filter on content, and the verifier's `extractFrameCorpusSections` skips sections without content. **Probe at HEAD (tsx, synthetic frame)** - Primary held 441.301 with text; avoid held "441.304 Duration, extens…
- **Why this severity:** The finding stands at medium. It is not an accepted trade-off, it is not tracked, and it contradicts the intent written in the code itself. What I opened: finalize.ts, the three rebalance modules, coverage-sections/selection/assembly.ts, the agent's answer-frame tool, the planner (answer-frame-planning.ts), the verifier evidence builder (verifier/evidence.ts), the non-archive docs, the GitHub issues, git history for the stripping commit, and the last five saved answer runs. Why it is not dismissible: - **Not a decision.** The pooling of avoid sections into the rebalance pool dates from April 2026 (cc6cae1 / 6b42ad1). The content stripping was added later, on 2026-06-11 in 216dab4, which tou…

### r2-critic-stored-run-incidence-05 — On the release benchmark, 9 of 50 frames in every September run ship a primary or supporting section with no text, three times the incidence round one measured on the held-out set

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/rebalance/coverage.ts:21`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** In each of the three local September runs on q-001 to q-050 (baseline, initial gate, final gate; local legacy graph), the same 9 of 50 frames carry at least one primary or supporting section with no text. Two mechanisms produce this: - **Avoid-strip (round one's corpus-frames-08):** 7 instances in 6 questions (q-002, q-005, q-009, q-017, q-036, q-039). An avoid candidate has its content set to null in `finalizeFrame` and is then promoted by rebalance. The text exists in the graph; for q-017 the first primary section, § 4.2.1.4 Other Non-covered Ambulance Services, has 713 characters. This is 6 of 50 against round one's 3 of 50 on the July held-out set: double, not triple. - **Empty-body sections (not in round one):** 4 instances in 4 questions (q-009, q-010, q-033, q-034). Frame selection picks a section whose `content_text` is empty in the graph, with an ordinary reason. The sections are MA-2180 "NOTE: Refer to Job Aid…", Policy 3K-2 § 5.8, Policy 2B-1 Attachment E (a primary) and an MA-3540 manual heading. No tool call can recover a body for these. Across all stored evidence, 118 of 1,697 answers (7.0%) have a content-less primary or supporting section and 75 (4.4%, 7 questions) have a content-less primary. Of 169 instances, 106 carry avoid-derived reasons and 63 carry ordinary ones. The 4 ordinary-reason instances in each September run are empty graph sections; the remaining ordinary-reason instances in older runs were not traced. No score harm is visible. q-017 scored 3 in all three runs because the agent fetched § 4.2.1.4 itself.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/rebalance/coverage.ts:20-21 `const surfacedSections = uniqueBy(` / `[...frame.primarySections, ...frame.supportingSections, ...frame.avoidSections],`. packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/finalize.ts:84 `(candidate) => ({ ...candidate.section, content: null }),`. milestones/2026-09-22-verified-release/final-gate-full.json, q-017 routing entry: `Primary sections:` / `ID: sec:doc:clinical_policy:15:4.2.1.4` / `Title: 4.2.1.4 Other Non-covered Ambulance Services` / `Reason: Tangential or process-focused section to avoid unless explicitly requested.`, and no `[corpusSection]` entry exists for that id in the same evidence set. Stored lengths for other stripped sections: Policy 3G-1 § 4.2.2 2,221 characters; Policy 5B § 5.5 1,250; Policy 1H _s27 607. Script: s4_authority.mjs, out_authority_rows.json.
- **Impact:** On nearly one frame in five of the gating benchmark the plan tells the model that a section is primary evidence while giving it, and the verifier, no text for it. The model has to spend a tool call to recover the body, and a claim written from the frame label alone has nothing to be checked against.
- **Fix:** 1. **Apply round one's corpus-frames-08 fix unchanged.** Strip avoid content once in `createEvidencePlanner.resolveAnswerFrame`, after rebalance. This clears the 6 avoid-strip questions. 2. **Handle empty-body sections separately.** In frame selection (`hydrateSectionCandidates` or the primary and supporting candidate builders), do not let a section with empty content take a primary or supporting slot. Skip it, or substitute its first child that has content, as the existing "[Container heading with N children…]" placeholder already does for some containers. Confirm on the current release graph whether such heading-only sections still exist before sizing this; the measurement here is from the legacy July 2026 local build. 3. **Add the frame invariant test only after both fixes.** It should assert that every primary and supporting section has non-empty content and every avoid section has null content. It will fail on step 1 alone. 4. **Report the count in the answer eval output.** Add "frames with a content-less primary or supporting section", split by avoid-derived reason versus other. It is deterministic, costs nothing, and was 9 of 50 (6 avoid-strip, 4 empty-body, one question in both) on the September gate.
- **How it was verified:** **Verdict: not refuted. The counts reproduce exactly, but the 9-of-50 figure combines two mechanisms, and only 6 of the 9 questions are round one's corpus-frames-08 bug.** **(1) Is the claim true? Yes.** - **Code.** `rebalance/coverage.ts:20-21` pools avoid sections back into the candidate set, and `finalize.ts:83-85` sets their content to null. Neither `finalize.ts` nor `rebalance/*` changed between the eval commit 1d93d60 and HEAD, so the mechanism is still live. - **Measurement method is sound.** `extractFrameCorpusSections` (agent `verifier/evidence.ts:333`) emits a `resolveAnswerFrame:section` entry for every primary or supporting section unless its content is empty. `extractSources` applies no cap or cross-tool dedupe. So a missing corpusSection entry does mean empty content. - **September runs.** My own parser over `baseline.json`, `initial-gate.json` and `final-gate-full.json` f…

### r2-critic-coverage-selection-core-02 — The yes/no 'scope' slot is chosen by substring over section bodies: an exclusions container (8C) and EPSDT boilerplate (Policies 9, 1A-2) are served as the scope section, and 2.1.1 never is

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/coverage-sections/selection/groups.ts:146`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** `collectCoverageScopeGroups` (groups.ts:136-158) classifies a section as coverage scope by unbounded substring match over its number, title and hydrated body, using 'scope', 'description', '2.1.1', '1.0' and 'general provisions'. On the local June 2026 parse about 1,834 of 13,228 sections qualify, most through body text alone, and every policy has several. `buildCoverageAnchors` (anchors.ts:116-121) gives the first primary slot to the top-`primaryScore` member of that group. Scores tie often enough that the winner depends on section listing order: Policy 9 yields 1.0 in document order and 2.2.1 EPSDT in reverse. Recorded 2026-09-22 milestone frames: - 8C leads with the 4.2.1 exclusions container, which matches on its hydrated child heading "4.2.1.1". Section 1.0 is still in supporting. - Policy 9 (q-032, q-040) and 1A-2 (q-031) lead with the 2.2.1 EPSDT boilerplate, which matches on "limitations on scope". Section 1.0 is absent from those frames entirely. - 2.1.1 never holds the slot in recorded frames. In the reviewer's in-memory per-policy run it holds it for 37 of 177 policies, so "2.1.1 never is" applies to recorded frames only. The measured answer cost is limited. The agent can fetch 1.0 itself: q-031 cited it, and q-032 scored 3 on every dimension. The one degraded released answer, q-040, shares its cause with corpus-frames-03, which put 3.2.1 in avoid. Policy 9 § 1.0 does state that covered OTC products are in scope, so its absence plausibly contributed. The 8C case is mostly a mislabel, because the hydrated 4.2.1 container is relevant not-covered evidence. The same substring logic labels 10A § 3.2.1.1 and 8C § 4.2.1.1 as "Scope or description section". This corrects corpus-frames-03's statement that the scope slot is 2.1.1.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/coverage-sections/selection/groups.ts:144-153 `sectionNumber === '1.0' ||` `sectionNumber === '2.1.1' ||` `includesPhrase(text, [` `'scope',` `'description',` `'2.1.1',` `'1.0',` packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/coverage-sections/selection/anchors.ts:118-120 `.filter((entry) => groups.coverageScopeSections.some((section) => section.id === entry.section.id))` `.sort((left, right) => right.primaryScore - left.primaryScore)` `.slice(0, 1)` Why two real sections qualify (<review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/critic-coverage-selection-core/r_scope_why.out): 8C § 4.2.1 'Specific Criteria Not Covered by Medicaid' has no text of its own; getSection synthesises `[container heading with 3 child sections:] ### 4.2.1.1 outpatient behavioral health …`, which contains '2.1.1'. Policy 9 § 2.2.1 matches on `service limitations on scope, amount, duration, frequency`. Recorded frame, 2026-09-22 final gate, q-040 'Does NC Medicaid cover over-the-counter medications without a prescription?': `Primary sections: ID: sec:doc:clinical_policy:9:2.2.1 Title: 2.2.1 EPSDT Special Provision: Exception to Policy Limitations for a Medicaid Beneficiary under 21 Years of Age` … `Avoid sections: … ID: sec:doc:clinical_policy:9:3.2.1 Title: 3.2.1 Specific criteria covered by Medicaid … Reason: Matched billing.`
- **Impact:** Recorded frames, 16 distinct yes/no questions on clinical policies (<review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/critic-coverage-selection-core/saved_yesno_byq.out): the first primary section is the 8C exclusions container for q-028 and its paraphrase h-028 (every one of 39 runs), and the EPSDT boilerplate for q-031, q-032 and q-040 (every one of 9 runs). In those three the real description, section 1.0, is not in the frame at all. For q-034 (adult dental whitening, routed to the DHHS manual MA-3540) the first primary is 'A. EPSDT for Recipients under 21 years of Age'; I did not trace which rule put it there. q-040 shows the cost. Its frame led with EPSDT and 4.1, omitted 1.0, and listed 3.2.1 as an avoid section with its text stripped. Section 3.2.1 is where Policy 9 says selected OTC products are covered under Policy 9A. The agent answered from the frame alone. The released, verifier-passed answer was scored 'partially misleading' for missing exactly that. The avoid nomination is corpus-frames-03's defect; the missing description is this one. In-memory run, one out-of-scope yes/no question per policy (<review scratch directory, not kept>
- **Fix:** Replace the predicate with one exported structural function, for example `isPolicyDescriptionSection`: - It is true when the normalised section number is exactly '1.0', or the title, after stripping the number, starts with "description of the procedure, product". - It uses no body text, no '2.1.1', and no bare 'scope' or 'description'. Use that function in `collectCoverageScopeGroups` and in the 'coverage-scope-primary' title profile (COVERAGE_SCOPE_SECTION_TERMS), so '2.1.1' can no longer match inside '3.2.1.1'. Make the slot deterministic: - Pick by structure, not by `primaryScore`: 1.0 first, then the unnumbered Description heading. - When 1.0 has no text of its own (about 16 to 18 policies on the local parse), fall back to its first non-empty 1.x child. - If nothing structural has text, leave the slot empty. Do not fill it with another section, and never anchor a section with no content. Keep EPSDT 2.2.1 where assembly.ts:90-94 already puts it, in supporting. Keep the limitation anchor as the second primary. Land this together with corpus-frames-03 (avoid nomination, third primary slot) and rag-design-06, because the following still name "2.1.x" as the scope section and should be changed to 1.0 in the same change: - the prompt (packages/agent/src/prompt.ts:21); - the verifier hint text (packages/agent/src/processors/verifier/verify.ts:66); - docs/development/gotchas.md:114; - the golden primary labels for q-026 to q-032. Add one frame test built from a real policy's section list (Policy 9 or 8C) that asserts the first primary is 1.0 under both document and reversed listing order. Validate against the saved milestone questions offline before any paid evaluation run.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Is the claim true? Yes for the mechanism and for the recorded frames; the corpus-wide counts I only partly rechecked. - Code: I read groups.ts, anchors.ts, assembly.ts, initial-selection.ts, scoring.ts and internal/base.ts. `includesPhrase` is a plain `text.includes`, with no word boundary. `collectCoverageScopeGroups` runs it over number + title + hydrated content with the bare terms 'scope', 'description', '2.1.1' and '1.0'. The only guard is `!isCoverageProcessTitle(title)`. `buildCoverageAnchors` takes the single top-`primaryScore` member of that group, and assembly puts it ahead of the limitation anchor and the initial primaries. No other guard exists. - Recorded frames: my own script over the eval results confirms that every 2026-09-22 milestone frame for q-028 leads with 8C § 4.2.1 then 4.1, and every one for q-032 and q-040 leads with Policy 9 § 2.2.1 then 4.1, with no 1.0 a…
