### r2-critic-coverage-selection-core-05 — On template policies a yes/no frame spends all eight text-bearing slots on title-matched template sections; with the right section as the top search hit it arrives with text in 4-11% of probes

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/coverage.ts:220`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The mechanism and the numbers stand. On template policies a plain yes/no coverage frame fills both primary slots and all four supporting slots from title-matched template sections before any search hit. Given the right section as the top hit, the real pipeline returned it with text in 9 to 25 of 218 synthetic probes (4-11%) and as a bare title in 192 to 208 (88-95%). Two corrections: 1. This is a corpus-wide measurement of corpus-frames-03 (primary crowding, avoid nomination) and corpus-frames-08 (stripped sections promoted by rebalance), not an independent defect. The new parts are the rate, the supporting-side crowding, and the low-value sections that fill supporting (4.0 banner, 'None Apply.', container aggregates). 2. 'corpus-frames-03's third primary slot is not enough' is not shown. With corpus-frames-03's fix applied (the reviewer's src_fixA), the same oracle puts the target in primary with text in 182 to 185 of 218 and in supporting with text in the rest. That oracle is a best case for the fix, because each question contains the section title; paraphrased questions were not measured. The probes are synthetic, some are unnatural, and the effect on final answers was not observed.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/coverage.ts:220-233 `const primaryCandidates = uniqueBy(` `[` `...context.toSectionCandidates(documentPrimaryHydrated),` `...promotedLimitationCandidates,` `...buildSectionCandidates(` `primarySeedHits,` … `).slice(0, FRAME_PRIMARY_LIMIT * 2);` packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/finalize.ts:73 `const primarySections = (await hydrateSectionCandidates(primaryCandidates, FRAME_PRIMARY_LIMIT, seenSectionIds)).map(` Real pipeline on parsed Policy 5B, golden q-026 wording (<review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/critic-coverage-selection-core/r_epsdt_5b.out): `supportingSections: 5B § 4.0 | … | 129ch`, `5B § 4.2 | 4.2 Specific Criteria Not Covered | 643ch`, `5B § 4.2.1 | … | 481ch`, `5B § 4.2.2 | 4.2.2 Medicaid Additional Criteria Not Covered | 11ch`; `FULL TEXT § 4.2.2: "None Apply."`; `avoidSections: … 5B § 3.2.1 | 3.2.1 Specific criteria covered by Medicaid | content:null`. The September recorded frame for q-026 has the same primary and supporting sections. <review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/critic-coverage-selection-core/e1_oracle.json, document order, target-only hits: `"plainYesNoProbes": 218`, `"supporting, content null": 121`, `"avoid (content null)": 85`, `"supporting, with text": 8`, `"primary, with text": 3`
- **Impact:** Oracle test: for 223 service-specific sections in 90 policies I asked 'Does NC Medicaid cover <section title>?' and gave the pipeline that section as its top hit (218 took the plain yes/no path; two hit sets, two listing orders). The section was a primary section with text in 1 to 3 probes, had text anywhere in 9 to 25 (4-11%), and arrived as a bare title in 192 to 208 (88-95%). The benchmark cannot see this. Its yes/no questions ask about services that are not in the policy, so the best hits are the template sections already selected; the one positive case, q-005, has its own congregate branch. For an ordinary 'is X covered' where X is covered, the frame tells the model to 'answer yes or no first' from 1.0 and 4.1 and holds no text about X. Section 3.2.1, where a policy lists what it covers, was an avoid entry with no text in 150 of 177 frames of the per-policy run, and in q-040's recorded frame; in other recorded frames it did reach supporting. What the agent then answers was not observed.
- **Fix:** Fold this into corpus-frames-03 and corpus-frames-08 rather than tracking it separately. 1. Implement corpus-frames-03's fix first (third yes/no primary slot, top seed hit hydrated ahead of the template picks, avoid nomination filtered by title) together with corpus-frames-08's fix (strip avoid content once, after rebalance). Use e1_oracle as the acceptance check; it moves from about 1% to about 84% primary with text under the first fix alone. 2. Add an oracle variant with paraphrased questions, where the title does not match a focus term. Reserve a supporting slot for the top scoped search hit only if that variant still fails. 3. Clean up supporting as a small independent change: skip a container whose children are already selected, skip sections of about 15 characters or fewer ('None Apply.'), and do not select the 3.0/4.0/5.0/7.0 banner sections. Before dropping the 4.0 banner, check the planner's EPSDT checklist line, which currently keys off it, and point it at 2.2.1 instead. 4. Treat removing 'cover', 'covered', 'will' and 'benefit' from service focus terms as a separate, tested change. It alters primaryLimit and focus scoring for every coverage question; rerun the coverage frame tests and r_saved_compare first. 5. Add positive yes/no questions with no dedicated branch to the benchmark, and add the shared frame-test assertion from corpus-frames-08 that every primary and supporting section has non-empty content.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Factual claim: true. I read pipelines/coverage.ts, context/finalize.ts, context/hydration.ts, rebalance/coverage.ts, the yes/no profile rules in coverage-sections/candidates.ts, initial-selection.ts and internal/search.ts. The mechanism is as described: - Template sections picked by title are listed ahead of search hits in both the primary and supporting candidate lists, and finalizeFrame hydrates only the first four of each. - Leftover hits become avoid entries with content set to null, and rebalance pools those back in without their text. - 'cover' and 'covered' are not service-focus stopwords, so primaryLimit is 2 for every such question. I copied the reviewer's harness and oracle script to verify2 and reran it against the current source. It runs the real resolveCoverageFrame over in-memory parsed sections, with no network. The output was identical to the reviewer's JSON. Of 218 …

## RETR-4 · Substring intent and care-setting rules misroute common questions and are unstable under paraphrase

Severity high · effort M

### corpus-frames-05 — Keyword router misroutes common questions and gives the same type for only 76% and 50% of paraphrases

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:508`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** True as stated, with two clarifications. (1) "What does EPSDT require of states?" does miss the anchored EPSDT overview special case, but it routes to coverage (via the `epsdt` substring in `isCoverageQuestion`), not general; only the exact phrase "What does EPSDT require?" reaches federal_authority. (2) The keyword cascade is bypassed whenever the model passes the optional `questionType` hint to the resolve-answer-frame tool (`detectAnswerQuestionType` returns `requestedType` first, except for the EPSDT overview override), so the measured misroutes apply to calls where the hint is omitted, which the tool description presents as the default.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:508 `queryLower.includes('federal') ||`; :523 `queryLower.includes('amendment') ||`; :512 `(queryLower.includes('waiver') && queryLower.includes('application'))`; :301 `return explicitPolicies.length >= 2 || queryLower.includes('difference between') || queryLower.includes('vs ');`
- **Impact:** Measured at HEAD with analyzeQuery: - An income-limit question that mentions the federal poverty level goes to federal_authority. In a probe the frame held only 42 CFR 435.116; the NC policy section, the top default hit, was absent. - "What does the state plan amendment say about telehealth coverage?" and golden q-047 (a federal SPA-timeliness question) go to amendment. The SPA rebalance hint written for q-047 is unreachable without a model hint; its lines 91-93 are the only uncovered lines when frames-federal.test.ts runs. - "How do I submit an application for the CAP/C waiver for my child?" goes to federal_authority. - "What is the difference between Medicaid and Medicare?" goes to policy_variant. - "Will NC Medicaid pay for dental implants?" goes to general. - "What does EPSDT require of states?" misses the exact-phrase EPSDT special case. The router assigns the same type as the golden original for 38 of 50 held-out paraphrases and 25 of 50 fresh paraphrases. The yes/no coverage path fires for 13 of 17 golden negative-answer questions but 2 of 15 and 1 of 15 of their paraphrases. Recorded example: for h-026, a paraphrase of the chiropractic question, the frame led with "Attachment E: Requesting Unlisted Orthotics...", which frames-coverage.test.ts forbids for the golden wordi…
- **Fix:** Use narrow exclusions rather than allowlists, and do not add a classifier. 1. In the shared `isFederalAuthorityQuery` predicate, ignore "federal poverty" and FPL before testing for "federal". It must be the shared predicate, because retrieval-planning.ts:33 and :79 and rerank.ts:169 use it too; that is what dropped the NC section from the poverty-level frame. 2. In the same predicate, require a state or CMS cue (or a CFR/1915 reference) for the waiver-plus-application clause. 3. In `isChangeNoticeQuery`, strip "(state) plan amendment(s)" and the token SPA before testing "amendment"/"amended". This alone sends q-047 to federal_authority and makes the SPA hint reachable. Do not reorder federal ahead of amendment for any CFR cue; that flips golden q-045. 4. In `isPolicyVariantQuery`, honour "difference between" and "vs " only when at least one explicit or aliased policy number is present. 5. Add "pay(s) for" and "eligible for" to coverage vocabulary, and widen the yes/no opener test to match. Do not add "get" or "paid". 6. Widen the EPSDT overview regex to accept a trailing "of states". 7. Add a free deterministic test that runs `analyzeQuery` over the held-out and fresh paraphrase files and asserts a consistency floor, ratcheted upward. This reuses the calculation already in reports.py:124 and needs no inference. 8. Validate with frame tests for the poverty-level, SPA and q-045 wordings, since the predicates drive retrieval as well as type. q-035 moving to coverage looks like an improvement but is unverified. Keyword edits will not fix paraphrase instability; the simulation shows that. If stability still matters after the above, use the existing optional `questionType` hint on the resolve-answer-frame tool (the agent model already runs per request) and measure it with the existing offline routing campaign, rather than adding a new request-time classifier.
- **How it was verified:** The finding holds: every quoted line is present verbatim at the cited line, and every routing count I re-ran reproduces exactly. **What I opened.** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts` (predicates and `detectAnswerQuestionType`), `packages/policy-corpus/src/query/analysis.ts` (`analyzeQuery`), `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/rebalance/federal-authority.ts` (`SPA_HINT`), and `packages/agent/src/tools/answer-frame.ts` (tool schema). **Precedence.** `detectAnswerQuestionType` (lines 426-441) checks amendment, then federal authority, then prior authorization, then policy variant, then coverage, then general. That matches the claim, including amendment being checked before federal. **Re-run.** I ran a throwaway tsx script calling `analyzeQuery` over the three datasets and the hand-written questions. - Paraphrase stability: 38 of 50 held-out and 25 of 50 fresh paraphrases g…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted. I looked for an accepted trade-off, existing tracking, a conflicting invariant, or contrived scenarios, and found none that dismiss it. What I opened: packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts (the cascade and predicates), packages/policy-corpus/src/query/analysis.ts, the callers in retrieval/hybrid.ts and frame/evidence-plan.ts, packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-prefetch.ts, packages/agent/src/prompt.ts, packages/agent/src/tools/answer-frame.ts, docs/development/jev.md, docs/validation/jev-readiness-2026-10-04/README.md, docs/development/gotchas.md, the open issue list, and packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/jev/reports.py. ### corpus-retrieval-07 — Substring intent rules misroute common phrasings, and routing is fitted to the golden wording

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:508`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Query intent is decided by bare substring checks and exact-sentence regexes. Retrieval never receives a caller-supplied question type (hybrid.ts:25), so these flags always drive rerank adjustments. They also choose the document-type groups whenever the caller passes no documentTypes: that covers the general, coverage, prior-auth, amendment and policy-variant frame pipelines, and the search-sections tool when the model omits the filter. The federal-authority frame passes explicit documentTypes and bypasses group selection. The flags decide the frame type whenever the caller omits questionType. All listed misfires reproduce. The 'child'-in-childless and 'hearing'-in-"hearing aids" cases only set rerank flags (pediatric, public-notice) and do not change question type or profile. Over the 50 held-out paraphrases, 12 change questionType and 33 change at least one flag, but only 2 change the retrieval profile name. 19 of 55 golden questions match one of the eight alias rules; 17 keep the alias after federal-authority suppression. The waiver-plus-application rule is intentional but over-broad.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:508 `queryLower.includes('federal') ||`; :78 `'form',`; :523 `queryLower.includes('amendment') ||`; :512 `(queryLower.includes('waiver') && queryLower.includes('application'))`; :360 `return /^(?:what does epsdt require|what is epsdt|what are (?:the )?epsdt requirements|(?:explain|summarize|describe) epsdt(?: requirements)?)[?.!\s]*$/.test(queryLower.trim());`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/rerank.ts:295 `if (!flags.coverageQuestion || flags.operationalRoutingQuery || flags.federalAuthorityQuery || flags.changeNoticeQuery) {`
- **Impact:** Probes with the real analyzeQuery and selectRetrievalProfile: "What is the income limit as a percentage of the federal poverty level for pregnant women?" becomes federal_authority and federal_hybrid, so only federal_regulation, federal_statute and one implementation group are searched (no bulletins or guidance), with non-federal hits at -0.08 and federal hits at +0.08. "What information must be documented for personal care services?" becomes operational_hybrid with 8 groups, while "What documentation is required for personal care services?" stays baseline; the authoritative-tier boost is skipped in the first case, shrinking the margin between an authoritative clinical-policy hit and a fee-schedule hit from 0.26 to 0.08. "What does the state plan amendment require for PCS rates?" and "What is the current contract amendment for Healthy Blue?" become amendment questions. "How do I apply for the CAP/DA waiver? What is the application process?" becomes federal_authority with the CAP/DA alias dropped. "What does EPSDT require?" is federal_authority, but "What does EPSDT cover?", "What is the EPSDT benefit?" and "Tell me about EPSDT" are coverage. Over the repository's 50 held-out paraphrases, 12 change questionType relative to their golden original and 33 change at least one flag. Mea…
- **Fix:** Keep the router deterministic and make a wrong intent guess cheap, instead of adding a classifier. In order: 1. **Add a free regression gate first.** Write a vitest in policy-corpus that loads `dataset.json` and `heldout-paraphrase.json` and, for each paraphrase/original pair, asserts the same `questionType`, retrieval profile and type groups from `analyzeQuery` and `selectRetrievalProfile`. Start with an explicit allow-list of known divergences and shrink it. Add the reviewer's probe sentences (FPL, "information must be documented", state plan amendment, CAP/DA application, hearing aids, the EPSDT variants) as cases. Treat the held-out set as a regression set, since it has already been inspected. 2. **Make type groups additive.** In `defaultTypeGroupsForQuery`, return `BASE_TYPE_GROUPS` plus the federal implementation group for federal-authority queries instead of replacing the base groups. Keep the `federal_hybrid` profile name, the federal boosts and the frame's statute/regulation pairing (gotcha 152). Stop dropping aliased policy numbers on the federal flag in `collectPolicyNumbersForQuery`. In `scoreCoverageAuthorityTier`, stop zeroing the authoritative-tier boost on a lexical flag alone; reduce it, or require a stronger signal such as an explicit CFR/USC citation. Measure whether always including the operational groups is worth three extra per-group queries before making it the default. 3. **Token-bound the single-word triggers** with the existing `matchesToken` (form, forms, child, contract, claim and similar). Tighten federal authority to authority-seeking phrasing: a CFR/USC/SSA citation, "federal regulation|law|statute|requirement|authority", "statutory", or the existing EPSDT overview test loosened from an exact-sentence list. Bare "federal", and waiver plus application, should no longer qualify. 4. **Do not build a runtime typed or Jev router now.** The 2026-10-04 readiness note records "Keep offline" and "no paid routing work" for routing, and the benchmark lacks independent labels. If more is wanted later, strengthen the `questionType` description on resolve-answer-frame so the agent model supplies it more often, and let the already-collected Jev disagreement list guide inspection of the deterministic rules, as the note suggests. 5. **Defer the graph-data move.** Moving the alias rules to `PolicyConcept` data and deleting the single-question scorers should wait until gate 1 exists and the corpus qualification work (#187, #188) allows a reseed. Delete each scorer only when the gate shows the golden and held-out rows it serves still retrieve…
- **How it was verified:** I read query-intent.ts in full (536 lines), base.ts, query/analysis.ts, internal/retrieval-planning.ts, profiles/retrieval.ts, profiles/retrieval-signals.ts, retrieval/hybrid.ts, the relevant parts of rerank.ts and scoring-constants.ts, and the frame pipelines' search calls. I then ran one throwaway tsx script against the real analyzeQuery, selectRetrievalProfile and query-intent predicates, over hand-written probes plus dataset.json and heldout-paraphrase.json.
