- **Why this severity:** The finding survives at medium, with one recommendation item and one sentence of the claim needing correction. **What I checked** - Read `query-intent.ts`, `base.ts`, `retrieval-planning.ts`, `scoring-constants.ts`, `retrieval-signals.ts`, the agent's `search.ts` and `answer-frame.ts` tools, `gotchas.md`, `docs/development/jev.md`, and `docs/validation/jev-readiness-2026-10-04`. - Checked open issues and the git history of `query-intent.ts`. - Ran a throwaway probe against the built `analyzeQuery` (dist, so possibly behind source, but the cited source lines match). **Not an accepted trade-off** - `includesPhrase` is a raw `text.includes`, and `isFederalAuthorityQuery` still uses bare `inclu…

### r2-domain-facts-02 — The adult-care-home abbreviation 'ach' matches inside ordinary words and forces the congregate PCS policy; the in-home title terms match no policy

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/term-lists.ts:397`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** True in substance. The congregate setting entry contains 'ach' and is matched by plain substring, so any query containing those letters ('each', 'reach', 'attach', 'machine') is treated as a congregate-setting question at three call sites. On the local (July 2026) graph with embeddings disabled, paired controls show that this alone removes Policy 3L from the authoritative documents, leaving only 3L-1, and emits 'Preferred because the query asks about a congregate or facility-based setting.' even for a question that says 'in their own home'. The in-home entry's title terms do not occur in Policy 3L's title, so an explicit in-home question gets no preference for 3L. Correction: in the same probes the frame's primary and supporting sections were already all from 3L-1 without the 'ach' match (the round-one 3L/3L-1 swap). The 'ach' match therefore does not by itself change which section text is supplied in keyword-only mode; it removes the only remaining pointer to 3L and adds a false assertion about the user's question. Behaviour in hybrid mode on the deployed corpus is unmeasured.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/term-lists.ts:397 `terms: ['congregate', 'adult care home', 'group home', 'ach', 'assisted living', 'assisted living facility'],`; :402-403 `terms: ['in-home', 'in home', 'home-based', 'home setting', 'at home'],` / `titleTerms: ['state plan pcs', 'in-home', 'in home'],`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/base.ts:35-37 `return phrases.some((phrase) => text.includes(phrase));`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/document-selection.ts:196-202 `for (const variant of VARIANT_SETTING_TERMS) {` / `if (!includesPhrase(queryLower, variant.terms)) continue;` ... `return { documents: matching, reason: variant.reason };`. Probe, 'How many hours of personal care services does NC Medicaid cover each month for someone living in their own home?': `authoritative: 3L-1 "State Plan Personal Care Services (PCS) Provided in Congrega` (only document); `variantReasoning: Preferred because the query asks about a congregate or facility-based setting.`; `PRIMARY: 3L-1 § 5.3.1 | 5.3.1 Monthly Service Hour Limits`. Same result for '... a beneficiary who needs help reaching the bathroom?'. Probe, 'Does NC Medicaid cover in-home personal care services for adults?': all six frame sections come from 3L-1 and `variantReasoning: Relevant policy variant returned by policy lookup.` Local graph: 3L section 5.3.1 has `A beneficiary under 21 years of age may be authorized to receive up to 60 hours of service per month`; 3L-1 section 5.3.1 has no such sentence. Four document…
- **Impact:** A daughter asks how many hours of personal care her mother can get each month at home. The frame contains only the congregate-settings policy and states that she asked about a facility. The answer is grounded and cited, but to the wrong policy: the hour-limit section omits the under-21 limit, and the exclusions and provider rules are those for adult care homes. A groundedness verifier cannot detect this, because every claim matches the supplied text. The benchmark cannot detect it either: only one golden question contains the letters, and it concerns a different policy.
- **Fix:** Step 1 (small, one PR, all in packages/policy-corpus): 1. In both consumers (document-selection.ts:118 and :196), match the query-side `terms` with the existing `matchesToken` instead of `includesPhrase`. Apply it to every term, not only 'ach'; otherwise 'at home' keeps matching "what home health ...". 2. Because token matching drops plurals, add them to the lists: 'adult care homes', 'group homes', 'assisted living facilities', 'nursing homes', 'residential facilities', 'residential treatment facilities'. Keep 'ach' (now token-bounded) or drop it; either is safe. 3. Add an optional `policyNumbers` field to the VARIANT_SETTING_TERMS entries: ['3L-1'] for congregate, ['3L'] for in-home. Match it by exact equality on the upper-cased document.policyNumber, never by substring on the combined title text ('3l' is a substring of '3l-1'). Keep `titleTerms` as the fallback for documents outside this pair, and delete the dead 'state plan pcs' title term. 4. Replace the invented fixture titles in tests/internal/document-selection.test.ts with the real ones ("State Plan Personal Care Services (PCS)" and "... Provided in Congregate Settings") and add cases: 'each month ... in their own home' gives no congregate preference; 'in-home personal care' prefers 3L only; 'adult care homes' (plural) prefers 3L-1; 'what home health services' gives no in-home preference. 5. Skip the proposed "title terms match a current document" data test: there is no checked-in title catalogue, so it would need a live graph. A one-off read-only Cypher check of the nursing-facility and residential-treatment title terms is enough. Step 2 (separate change, needs an evaluation run): when the query names no setting and no policy number, and the routing documents are the 3L/3L-1 sibling pair, do not let coverage.ts:171 narrow to one sibling. Keep both in the frame with a variantReasoning that says which policy covers which setting, as gotchas.md:102 already instructs. Check golden q-005 and q-014 before and after, with repeated runs, since q-014 names no setting and expects 3L-1.
- **How it was verified:** I read term-lists.ts:395-404, base.ts:35-37, document-selection.ts (scoreVariantDocument 103-131, selectSettingPreferredDocuments 192-206), routing.ts:150-170 and coverage.ts:137-180/296, then ran a read-only frame probe against the local (older, July 2026) graph with embeddings disabled, using paired control questions. Code facts, all confirmed as quoted: the congregate entry contains 'ach'; includesPhrase is a plain String.includes over the lower-cased query; there is no token-boundary guard on any of the three call sites (scoreVariantDocument, routing.ts collectCoverageRoutingDocuments, coverage.ts settingPreference). No test covers a false 'ach' match; 'ach' appears nowhere else in package source. Behaviour confirmed with controls. "...cover each month for someone living in their own home?" and "...needs help reaching the bathroom?" both return a single authoritative document, 3L-1 …
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted. It is not a repeat of round one, not an accepted trade-off, not tracked, and the scenario is realistic. What I opened: term-lists.ts, base.ts, document-selection.ts, frame/context/routing.ts, frame/pipelines/coverage.ts, the document-selection test, gotchas.md, the round-one digest (index plus corpus-retrieval-04 and corpus-frames-05 in full), issues #188 and #161, the open issue list, the golden dataset, and the 3L / 3L-1 document rows in the local graph (read-only). 1. **Not a duplicate.** A grep of every digest file for `VARIANT_SETTING`, `selectSettingPreferred` and `'ach'` returns nothing. corpus-retrieval-04 is about the `lookupPolicy` prefix match and explicitly judged t…

## RETR-5 · Section text beyond the first 6,000 characters cannot be read by any tool

Severity high · effort M

### agent-processors-tools-09 — getSection promises the complete text but returns only the first 6,000 characters, with no way to read the rest of a long leaf section

- **Location:** `packages/agent/src/tools/read.ts:12`
- **Severity:** high (reviewer proposed medium) · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim is accurate as written. The one gap it left open ("how many answer-ready sections exceed 6,000 characters was not measured") can now be filled from the local graph: 5,393 of 60,347 non-empty sections (about 9%) exceed 6,000 characters, and 5,222 of those are leaf sections with no sub-sections to explore. By answer-ready document type: fee_schedule 2,722 of 3,322 (82%), federal_statute 119 of 327 (36%), managed_care_contract 670 of 4,012 (17%), federal_regulation 500 of 3,404 (15%), clinical_policy 213 of 10,848 (2%), dhhs_policy 129 of 13,221 (1%). The largest section is 38.5 MB. The same 6,000-character cap (MAX_COMBINED_CHARS) also applies when getSection combines the children of an empty container heading.
- **Evidence:** tools/read.ts:12-13 `'Retrieve the full content and metadata of a specific policy section by its ID. ' +` / `'Use this when you need to read the complete text of a section found via searchSections.',` policy-corpus internal/record-mapping.ts:143-144 `if (!content || content.length <= MAX_CONTENT_CHARS) return content;` / `return \`${content.slice(0, MAX_CONTENT_CHARS)}\\n\\n[Content truncated at 6,000 chars. Use getSectionContext to explore sub-sections.]\`;` :59 `contentSnippet: hit.content ? hit.content.slice(0, SEARCH_SNIPPET_CHARS) : null,`
- **Impact:** A fee-schedule sheet, drug-criteria attachment or long table whose relevant row sits past character 6,000 can be ranked first, since retrieval scores on the full text (exact procedure-code boosts, for instance), and is then read as its head only. docs/development/gotchas.md:226 records local sections above 38 MB. The agent answers 'could not verify', or treats a truncated list as complete ('X is not listed'), and the verifier can only check against the same truncated text. Neither the agent nor the policy-corpus tests cover the truncation path (no match for 'Content truncated' or MAX_CONTENT_CHARS in either test tree). How many answer-ready sections exceed 6,000 characters was not measured.
- **Fix:** Do the small part now and defer the chunk-based parts. 1. **Add `offset` (default 0) and `maxChars` (default 6000, capped at about 12k) to `PolicyCorpus.getSection` and the tool.** Cut the window in Cypher with `substring(s.content_text, $offset, $maxChars)` and return `s.content_length AS totalChars` plus `nextOffset` (null at the end). This also stops shipping whole 38 MB bodies to Node. Defaults leave the frame hydration callers unchanged. 2. **Fix the wording.** Replace the truncation note with "Showing chars X-Y of N; call getSection with offset=Y to continue", built from the constant. Change the tool description and prompt.ts lines 8 and 74 to say the read is windowed. Tell the model not to conclude "not listed" from a section where nextOffset is not null. 3. **Label verifier evidence with the window.** In `extractGetSection`, add the range (for example "Chars 6000-12000 of 48210") so the verifier sees that a source is partial. No keying change is needed; sources are not deduped. Keep in mind the 120,000-char verifier budget shared across all sources. 4. **Add tests.** Cover the default window, continuation, the last window, and the container-children path, which has the same 6,000 cap (MAX_COMBINED_CHARS). 5. **Do not build the HAS_CHUNK passage reader or the chunk-text snippet yet.** Revisit only after planner-v3 chunks with body_start/body_end are confirmed in the release graph (the #188 qualification work). At that point the cheap version is to have chunk-vector.ts also return `c.body_start`, so the search hit carries a `matchOffset` the model can pass straight to `getSection(offset)`. That needs no new tool and no extra embedding call. 6. **Treat the multi-megabyte fee-schedule sections as a pipeline problem, not a read-tool problem.** Paging cannot reach a row deep in a 38 MB sheet. Bound spreadsheet section size at load time (row-group sections with the header row repeated) so retrieval lands on a readable unit. Until then, have getSection report totalChars so the agent can say the sheet is too large to read in full, rather than implying completeness.
- **How it was verified:** Every factual element of the claim holds in the current code, and the local graph shows the scenario is common rather than hypothetical. What I opened and found: - packages/agent/src/tools/read.ts: the getSection description quote is verbatim at lines 12-13. The input schema accepts only sectionId, so there is no offset or length parameter. execute calls policyCorpus.getSection and only strips sectionRoles. - packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/sections.ts: a section with non-empty content goes straight to truncateContent(content) at line 134. The child-combining path runs only when content is empty, and it is also capped (MAX_COMBINED_CHARS = 6000). - packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/record-mapping.ts: truncateContent (lines 142-145) and the snippet line (59) are verbatim as quoted. MAX_CONTENT_CHARS = 6000 and SEARCH_SNIPPET_CHARS = 300 are in scoring-constants.ts. - getSectionCo…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted, and I would raise it from medium to high. **What I checked** - `packages/agent/src/tools/read.ts`, `packages/agent/src/prompt.ts` and `deep-research/agents.ts`: the description and prompts all say "full"/"complete" text. A grep of `packages/agent/src` for "truncated" finds no guidance on what to do with the truncation marker. - `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/sections.ts` and `internal/record-mapping.ts`: the truncation is as reported, and no other reader bypasses it. - `frame/context/hydration.ts` calls `getSection` four times, so `resolveAnswerFrame` evidence is cut the same way. `processors/verifier/evidence.ts` builds verifier evidence from `getSection` output, so th…

### corpus-retrieval-02 — Section text beyond the first 6,000 characters cannot be read by any primitive, and the truncation note points at sub-sections that do not exist

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/record-mapping.ts:144`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim is true in substance; three details need tightening. - **Truncation hint:** it is appended whenever content exceeds 6,000 characters, with no check for children. It is not appended on every `getSection` call. - **Container sections:** empty-content sections take a separate branch that concatenates child text up to `MAX_COMBINED_CHARS = 6000`. The unreachable-remainder problem applies to sections with their own content over 6,000 characters. In the local graph nearly all of these are leaves, including all 500 over-length `federal_regulation` sections. - **Scale:** the local Neo4j graph shows the problem is wider than the eCFR-cache figure of 106 sections. It has 500 `federal_regulation` leaf sections over 6,000 characters, 2,700 `fee_schedule`, 663 `managed_care_contract` and 102 `federal_statute`. The SSA split therefore has not eliminated the problem for statutes either.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/record-mapping.ts:144 `return `${content.slice(0, MAX_CONTENT_CHARS)}\n\n[Content truncated at 6,000 chars. Use getSectionContext to explore sub-sections.]`;`. packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/sections.ts:134 `return buildSectionView(record, truncateContent(content));`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/record-mapping.ts:59 `contentSnippet: hit.content ? hit.content.slice(0, SEARCH_SNIPPET_CHARS) : null,`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/chunk-vector.ts:26 `s.content_text AS content,`. packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/federal/parser.py:130 `"level": 1, # CFR sections are flat within a part`
- **Impact:** Reproducing the loader's text for 42 CFR 438.8 (medical loss ratio, about 18,400 characters) from the local eCFR cache: the 6,000-character window ends mid-sentence inside paragraph (e)(2); (f) Denominator starts near character 7,600, (h) Credibility adjustment near 12,100, (j) Remittance near 15,100 and (k) Reporting near 15,400. A question about the MLR denominator, remittance or reporting duties retrieves the right section, the model reads the first third, and getSectionContext returns no children because CFR sections are loaded flat. Across 42 CFR parts 430-459 in the cache, 106 of 1,250 sections exceed 6,000 characters, including 438.6 (47.7k), 441.301 (26.7k), 441.302 (19.9k) and 435.4 (20.1k). Fee schedules are worse: median one section per document, 203 local documents averaging over 1 MB per section. Outcomes are a withheld or partial answer, or a negative claim grounded in a truncated read. The pipeline already acknowledged this for statutes (ssa_parser.py splits large SSA sections because an agent "never reaches" 1905(r)); nothing equivalent exists for CFR, contracts or spreadsheets.
- **Fix:** Fix reachability in the read path only, using the chunk offsets already stored; no pipeline or embedding change. 1. **Window read.** Change `getSection(sectionId, { offset = 0, length = MAX_CONTENT_CHARS })` to slice with Cypher `substring(s.content_text, $offset, $length)`, so multi-megabyte fee-schedule bodies never leave Neo4j. Return `contentLength` (already selected at `sections.ts:33`), `contentOffset` and `truncated`. Add optional `offset` to the get-section tool schema. Do not add a separate `getSectionPassages`. 2. **Honest truncation note.** Replace the note in `truncateContent` with a factual one, for example `[Showing chars 0-6000 of 18400. Call getSection with offset=6000 to continue.]`. Drop the getSectionContext hint, or gate it on `EXISTS { (s)-[:PARENT_OF]->() }` added to the main query. 3. **Carry the matched passage as new fields.** In `chunk-vector.ts`, additionally return `c.content_text AS matchedPassage, c.body_start AS matchedOffset` and leave `content` as the section text, because rerank, focus-ranking and document-selection score on it. In `mergeMax`, copy `matchedPassage`/`matchedOffset` from the losing hit when the winner lacks them. In `retrievalHitToSearchHit`, build the snippet from `matchedPassage` when present and expose `matchedOffset`, so the model can call `getSection(id, { offset })` directly. 4. **Frames.** In frame hydration, pass the candidate's `matchedOffset` (when present) to `getSection`, so answer frames read the window that matched instead of always the first 6,000 characters. 5. **Verifier.** Include the window range in the `extractGetSection` text (for example `Chars: 6000-12000 of 18400`). Add a test that two windows of the same section both reach `buildSourceText`. 6. **Tests.** A fact at character 10,000 of a leaf section is reachable through an offset read. A `chunk_vector` hit's snippet contains the matched chunk text even when another backend wins the merge. Run agent tests plus an agent build for the schema change. Drop load-time CFR paragraph splitting from this fix. Revisit it only as separate work for citable paragraph identity, and then without duplicating parent text into the embedding plan.
- **How it was verified:** I tried to refute this and could not; the claim holds in the code and in the local graph. **Quoted evidence.** All five quotes are verbatim at the cited lines. **Truncation behaviour.** `getSection` takes only `sectionId`. For any section with non-empty content it returns `truncateContent(content)`, which cuts at `MAX_CONTENT_CHARS = 6000` and appends the `getSectionContext` hint unconditionally, with no check for children. The query fetches `s.content_length` but never puts it in the view, so there is no length or truncated field. **No other route to section text.** I looked for a guard or alternative path and found none: - `LegalPrimitiveService` has seven methods; only `getSection` returns section body text. `getDocument` returns section ids, titles and char counts only. - The agent's `get-section` tool takes only `sectionId`. - `search-sections` returns `contentSnippet`, which is `h…
- **Why this severity:** I tried to dismiss this as an accepted trade-off, an already-tracked item, or a contrived scenario, and none of those hold. Not documented or accepted. I searched docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/decisions (only 0001-repository-knowledge.md), docs/architecture, docs/operations, docs/roadmap.md and the agent prompts for the 6,000-character cap, truncation or passage/offset reads. The only truncation discussion concerns embedding inputs and Jev classifier inputs, where the project's stated stance is the opposite: never truncate, because truncation "can hide lost evidence". The read path contradicts that stance silently. The getSection tool description also promises "the full content" and "th…

## RETR-6 · Amendment recency is unreliable: synthetic dates tie and markers are parsed from unrelated titles

Severity high · effort M

### corpus-retrieval-05 — Amendment recency is arbitrary: same-year change notices tie on a synthetic date, the newest amending record is labelled 'authoritative', and the marker regex accepts any NN-NN

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/amendments.ts:30`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** All four parts of the claim hold. Corrections to detail only: the local graph holds 204 change-notice documents (the 212 figure is from the crawler registry and was not re-counted), every one dated YYYY-01-01. The concrete live cases are MA-3550 (notice 02-26 returned as authoritativeDocument ahead of 04-26), MA-2270 (08-25 ahead of 11-25) and MA-3322 (02-25 ahead of 07-25), rather than the hypothetical 01-26/02-26/04-26 trio. The ranking scores 1.530 vs 1.460 and the specific ABD/F&C twin filenames were not independently reproduced.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/amendments.ts:30 `ORDER BY amend.effective_date IS NULL, amend.effective_date DESC, amend.id ASC`; :46 `LIMIT 1`; :51 `const authoritativeDocument = amendments[0]`. packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/loader.py:291 `return f"{year}-01-01" # approximate: year + sequence only`. packages/agent/src/prompt.ts:93 `- Use authoritativeDocument from getAmendments as the default current document when it differs from the one you started with.`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/change-markers.ts:16 `normalized.match(/\b(\d{1,2})-(\d{2,4})\b/);`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/collector.ts:201-202 `const latestChangeMarkerValue = analysis.flags.isLatestChangeNoticeQuery` / `? hits.reduce<number | null>((latest, hit) => {`
- **Impact:** The local registry holds 212 DHHS change notices, up to 35 in one year. For a manual amended by notices 01-26, 02-26 and 04-26, all three carry 2026-01-01, so authoritativeDocument is whichever has the smallest hashed id. "What is the latest amendment to MA-xxxx?" can be answered with an older notice dated January 1, 2026 (a date no source states), fully grounded in tool output and therefore able to pass the verifier. gotchas.md says the manual's revision header is the authoritative recency signal and that some notices parse with blank bodies. Notices exist as ABD and Family and Children's twins with identical titles (for example CN-02-26-abd.pdf and CN-02-26-fc.pdf), so baseDocument returns one manual arbitrarily. In ranking, probes gave 'Individuals Ages 19-64' = 206419 and 'SPA 25-0026 ...' = 202625 against 202604 for the real header 'REVISED 03/16/2026 -CHANGE NO. 04-26'; for "What is the latest change notice for MA-2240?" the age-band section ranks first (1.530) above the real revision header (1.460).
- **Fix:** Keep the direction; change the specifics as follows. 1. Loader (loader.py `_extract_effective_date`, DHHS branch): write `effective_date = None` for DHHS change notices and add two integer Document properties, `change_year` and `change_sequence`, parsed from the same `_CHANGE_NOTICE_YEAR_RE`. Do not take the "approximate flag" option: a flag still lets `Effective: YYYY-01-01` reach verifier evidence (evidence.ts:108, :184) and tool output. 2. Ordering: in both primitives/amendments.ts:30 and retrieval/backends/amendment-context.ts:40, order by `coalesce(amend.change_year, toInteger(substring(amend.effective_date, 0, 4))) DESC, amend.change_sequence DESC, amend.effective_date DESC, amend.id ASC`. This keeps clinical synthetic revision nodes ordered by their real ISO dates. Nulling the date also moves notices to the end of the `coalesce(d.effective_date, '') DESC` tie-breaks in keyword.ts:105, document-expansion.ts:112 and routing.ts:92; confirm with the existing retrieval-ranking tests and one eval run before merging. Document that (year, sequence) is ordered only within one manual's series; ABD and F&C twins tie and fall to id. 3. Return shape: `{ document, amendments, amendedDocuments, totalAmendments, truncated }`, with `document` being the queried document and `amendedDocuments` returned `ORDER BY base.policy_number, base.id` with no LIMIT 1. Drop `authoritativeDocument` and `baseDocument` rather than keeping aliases. Update together: types.ts, navigate.ts schema and description, prompt.ts:93 (cite the queried manual as current text and its revision header for recency; amendments are history), evidence.ts extractGetAmendments (key and link from `document`), web message-sources.ts:266-272, and the three tests. 4. change-markers.ts: do not just delete the fallback. Replace both regexes with one explicit pattern that covers the real titles, for example `/\bchange\s+(?:notice\s+(?:for\s+manual\s+)?)?(?:no\.?\s*)?(\d{1,2})-(\d{2})\b/`, and reject years outside 1990 to current year + 1. Bare titles such as "02-26" then yield no marker, which is what gotchas.md:99-101 wants. Re-run tests/amendments.test.ts, because finalize.ts selectLatestRevision uses the same function. 5. collector.ts: do not compute the latest marker per document, since every standalone notice would become its own maximum and all would be boosted. Compute the maximum only over hits that are revision headers of non-notice documents (`isRevisionHeaderTitle(sectionTitle)` and the document title is not a change notice), restricted to `analysis.explicitPolicyNumbers` when the query names a po…
- **How it was verified:** The claim is true, and the local graph shows the wrong-answer case occurring today. I read amendments.ts in full, change-markers.ts, the loader date derivation, the AMENDS construction, prompt.ts, navigate.ts, collector.ts and the test file, then ran bounded read-only queries against local Neo4j. (1) Confirmed. The ORDER BY at amendments.ts:30 is verbatim, and loader.py stamps DHHS change notices with `{year}-01-01`. In the local graph all 204 change-notice documents have an effective_date ending in -01-01. Running the primitive's exact ORDER BY against live data returns the older notice first for three manuals: MA-3550 gets 02-26 ahead of 04-26, MA-2270 gets 08-25 ahead of 11-25, and MA-3322 gets 02-25 ahead of 07-25. MA-3550 is the case gotchas.md:101 names, where the manual header says CHANGE NO. 04-26. No guard intervenes between this query and `authoritativeDocument = amendments[0]…
- **Why this severity:** I tried to dismiss this as a documented trade-off, an already-tracked item, or a contrived scenario. None of those hold; the core of it (parts 1 and 2) is reproducible in the local graph on the project's own flagship example. What I opened: amendments.ts, loader.py (_extract_effective_date), change-markers.ts, prompt.ts, navigate.ts (getAmendments tool), frame/pipelines/amendment.ts, answer-routing.ts, gotchas.md, the 2026-06-11 faithfulness review and remediation plan, tests/amendments.test.ts, the open issue list, and the local Neo4j graph (read-only). 1. Not contrived: it happens in the live local graph. 56 of the 209 documents amended by DHHS notices have two or more notices tied on the…

### neo4j-live-03 — getAmendments names an arbitrary or wrong notice as authoritativeDocument when amendment dates tie

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/amendments.ts:30`
- **Severity:** medium (reviewer proposed high) · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim stands as written, with one clarification: the "12" cases where the pick is not the highest-numbered notice of the year are 12 base document rows covering 7 distinct policy numbers (MA-3550, MA-3322, MA-2270 x3, MA-2311, MA-3236, DHB-5046 x4, MA-2507), because some policies exist as several base documents (URL variants).
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/amendments.ts:30: "ORDER BY amend.effective_date IS NULL, amend.effective_date DESC, amend.id ASC" | :51: "const authoritativeDocument = amendments[0]" | packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/loader.py:291: "return f\"{year}-01-01\" # approximate: year + sequence only" | packages/agent/src/prompt.ts:93: "- Use authoritativeDocument from getAmendments as the default current document when it differs from the one you started with." | packages/policy-corpus/tests/amendments.test.ts:17: "it('returns the latest amendment as authoritative', async () => {"
- **Impact:** Real primitive on the local graph: getAmendments(MA-2270 Long Term Care Need and Budgeting) returns Change Notice 08-25 as authoritative although 11-25 and 04-25 share the date 2025-01-01. All 209 amended DHHS base documents have a year-only newest date; 56 have a tie at that date; in 12 the pick is not the highest-numbered notice of the year (MA-3550 NEMT picks 02-26 while 04-26 exists; MA-3322 picks 02-25 over 07-25; MA-2311 picks 03-25 over 11-25). With hash-derived ids the choice is equally arbitrary. The amendment frame pushes this document as an authoritative candidate (frame/pipelines/amendment.ts:78-83) and the prompt tells the model to treat it as the current document. For clinical policies the pick is a zero-section row such as "Policy 3K-2 Revision - 2025-09-01 (All sections and attachments)"; for 11A-10 it is permanently the typo row dated 2105-09-01.
- **Fix:** 1. **Stop promoting a notice to `authoritativeDocument` for in-place-revised types.** In `primitives/amendments.ts`, when the queried document is a `dhhs_policy` manual section or a `clinical_policy`, return the queried (base) document as `authoritativeDocument`. If the queried document is itself an amendment, return its `baseDocument`. Add a separate `latestAmendment` field for the delta. Keep amendment-as-authoritative only for types that genuinely supersede (revised SPAs). Update `prompt.ts:93` and `amendments.test.ts:17` to match, and say in the PR that this is a deliberate semantic change consistent with `gotchas.md:101`. 2. **Fix the dates at the source instead of adding a sequence property.** In `loader.py` `_extract_effective_date`, for change notices parse the labelled header block ("Effective: April 1, 2011", else "DATE: MARCH 28, 2011"), anchored to the "Change No:" block so prose dates are not picked up. Fall back to `YYYY-01-01` only when neither label is present. Do not persist an integer sequence or `amendment_order`: the numbering is per manual and collides on the same base. Keep `amend.id ASC` as the final stable key. 3. **Use the manual's own header where it exists.** For DHHS base manuals, parse "REVISED m/d/yyyy - CHANGE NO. NN-YY" (present on 117 of 209 amended bases) into the base's `effective_date` and a `latest_change_no` property. That gives a "latest" signal that does not depend on AMENDS edge coverage. Record the MA-2311 case (header 03-26, no linked 03-26 notice) as a separate edge-coverage defect. 4. **Reject implausible revision years.** In `_parse_revision_rows`, skip rows whose year is later than the current year plus one, which removes the 11A-10 row dated 2105-09-01. 5. **Tests.** Add a pipeline unit test for the header-block date parser with three same-year notices and one with no labelled date. Add a policy-corpus test asserting the base is authoritative for a DHHS manual and that `amendments[]` is ordered by effective date. Confirm against the local graph with a read-only query for MA-2270, MA-2311 and 11A-10, since mocked records cannot catch ordering errors.
- **How it was verified:** I read packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/amendments.ts in full, the loader's _extract_effective_date, the amendment frame pipeline, the prompt line and the cited test, then ran read-only Cypher against the local Neo4j using the primitive's exact ORDER BY. All quoted evidence is present verbatim at the cited lines. No guard intervenes between the query and the result: getAmendments promotes amendments[0] straight to authoritativeDocument, with no secondary sort on notice sequence and no filter on section-less rows. The amendment frame pushes that document as an authoritative candidate (score 1.3) and the prompt tells the model to treat it as the default current document. The live data matches the claim: - 209 amended dhhs_policy bases, all 209 with a year-only (-01-01) newest date, 56 with a tie at that date. - MA-2270 picks notice 08-25 ahead of 11-25 and 04-25, all dated 2025-01-01…
- **Why this severity:** The finding stands as a real defect, but I would rate it medium, not high. **What I checked** - Read `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/amendments.ts`, the loader date code, `frame/pipelines/amendment.ts`, the agent prompt, the verifier evidence extractor and `amendments.test.ts`. - Searched `docs/development/gotchas.md`, the amendment-context spec, the product-scope review and the open issue list for an accepted trade-off or existing tracking. - Ran one read-only Cypher query for MA-2270 on the local graph. It reproduced the tie: 08-25, 11-25 and 04-25 all carry 2025-01-01, and 08-25 sorts first by id. - I did not re-run the reviewer's 209-document comparison (56 ties, 12 wrong picks);…

### corpus-frames-09 — latestRevision is unreliable: it falls back to the document title, takes the highest marker from any document, and reads any N-NN token as a change number

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/finalize.ts:52`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** selectLatestRevision runs for every frame built through finalizeFrame (the general, coverage, prior-auth, federal-authority and amendment pipelines), not literally every frame: the policy-variant pipeline and the empty-scope frame in service.ts hardcode latestRevision to null. It runs over all primary and supporting sections regardless of document, picks the largest value parsed from a section title (the fallback pattern accepts any bare "N-NN" token), and when nothing parses reports the first authoritative document's title as the marker. The "Lead with the latest revision marker" plan directive is emitted only for the amendment family; the verifier evidence line "Latest revision marker:" is emitted for any frame with a non-null latestRevision.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/finalize.ts:52 `const document = documents.find((candidate) => candidate.id === section?.documentId) ?? documents[0] ?? null;` and :56 `marker: describeLatestRevision(section, document),` packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/change-markers.ts:16 `normalized.match(/\b(\d{1,2})-(\d{2,4})\b/);` and :37 `return section?.title ?? document?.title ?? null;`
- **Impact:** Probe at HEAD, shaped like recorded question h-043: "What is the date of the newest change notice issued for the 8C outpatient behavioral health policy?", with a hit from an unrelated eligibility-manual notice. latestRevision.marker became "CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUAL NO. 09-21, TRIBAL MEMBERSHIP AND AMERICAN INDIAN HEALTH SERVICES (IHS)". The agent plan's first family directive was "Lead with the latest revision marker:" followed by that title. The saved real frame for h-043 shows the same marker. A second probe with a section titled "Ages 18-64 Service Limits" made that title the marker (parsed as change 18 of 2064), and ordinary sections were labelled "Latest revision header or marker section." Every non-amendment frame with a document reports the document title as the latest revision; saved frames show "Latest revision marker: Nursing Facilities" and "Lock-in Program". Recorded answers for h-043 were still right because the agent used getAmendments.
- **Fix:** Keep the direction, but implement it through the seam that already exists and gate the parser instead of tightening the regex. 1. **change-markers.ts:** make `extractChangeMarkerValue` return null unless the normalized text contains "change no", "change notice" or "revised"; factor that test out of `isRevisionHeaderTitle`. Keep both regexes, including the loose N-NN fallback. Do not delete line 16 or require the keyword to sit directly before the digits: 248 real titles of the form "CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUAL NO. 01-22, ..." parse only through the fallback. The gate removes the 826 keyword-less false positives ("Ages 21-64", "SPA 22-0004", "Table 4-21") for finalize, rerank.ts:218 and collector.ts:203 together. Add table tests for "CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUAL NO. 09-21, ..." (202109), "DMA CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUAL 01-14" (201401), "REVISED 03/16/2026 - CHANGE NO. 04-26" (202604), "Ages 18-64 Service Limits" (null) and "PUBLIC NOTICE (SPA 22-0004)" (null). 2. **finalize.ts:** compute latestRevision only from the existing `latestRevisionSections` input. Stop merging in primarySections and supportingSections, and drop the `documents[0]` fallback. Return null when that input is empty or nothing parses. Set the marker to the chosen section's title and the document to that section's own document. Delete `describeLatestRevision`. Non-amendment frames then get latestRevision null with no questionType branch. 3. **amendment.ts:** pass `latestRevisionSections` as the hydrated sections for `revisionHeaderHits` (already gated by `isRevisionHeaderTitle`). When `lookupDocuments` is non-empty, first filter those hits to document ids in lookupDocuments plus the ids already returned in `amendmentViews` (amendments, baseDocument, authoritativeDocument). That is an id allow-list over data already fetched, with no new query. When the named policy has no parseable header (clinical coverage policies use "Revision Information:"), latestRevision is null and the agent relies on getAmendments, which is what produced the correct h-043 answers. 4. **Optional cosmetic:** at amendment.ts:120, use the "Latest revision header or marker section." reason only for hits where `isRevisionHeaderTitle` is true. 5. **Tests:** add one amendment-frame regression where the query names policy A and the top hit is a change notice from unrelated document B; expect latestRevision null or a section from A. The existing MA-3550 test should pass unchanged.
- **How it was verified:** The claim is true in substance; I could not refute it, and one detail ("every frame") is slightly overstated. What I opened: finalize.ts, change-markers.ts, pipelines/amendment.ts, the callers of finalizeFrame, the amendment rules in answer-frame-planning.ts, the verifier's evidence.ts, and saved eval results. I did not re-run the reviewer's two vitest probes; the verdict rests on reading the code and the saved frames. - **Quoted evidence:** all four quotes are present verbatim at the cited lines. - **Runs over all sections regardless of document:** finalizeFrame calls selectLatestRevision unconditionally over the union of latestRevisionSections, primarySections and supportingSections. There is no questionType guard and no document filter. - **Highest marker wins:** the reduce keeps the section whose title parses to the largest value. The document is then looked up from that section, so…
- **Why this severity:** I tried to dismiss this as a harmless or known trade-off and could not; the finding stands at medium. What I opened: finalize.ts, change-markers.ts, the amendment rules in the agent's answer-frame-planning.ts, the verifier's evidence.ts, docs/development/gotchas.md, the open issue list, and the saved 2026-09-22 verified-release gate (final-gate-full.json). Why it is not already handled or accepted: - Nothing in gotchas.md, docs/decisions or the open issues (#144-#188) covers latestRevision selection. Gotchas lines 99-101 point the other way: they say the governing manual's own revision header is the authoritative recency signal. Picking the highest marker from any document contradicts that …

### r2-critic-amendment-date-design-06 — Marker patterns on 70,634 real section titles: the explicit design misses the 'Current Change Notice: NN-YY' header, the gated design accepts revised-SPA numbers (one as year 2033), and the document-title fallback is the larger source of false markers

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/change-markers.ts:15`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Measured on the legacy local graph (29,351 distinct section titles, 70,634 titled section occurrences, 7,589 documents of which 4,267 are synthetic clinical revision rows), with the regex mechanism present in current HEAD. True change markers: 479 titles, 1,182 section occurrences, 204 document titles. - Current parser (change-markers.ts:15-16): finds all of them, plus 836 false section occurrences, false values on 658 real document titles (414 DHHS, 171 SPA) and on all 4,267 synthetic revision titles. On the synthetic titles it reads the MM-DD of the ISO date as number-year, for example "Policy 8C Revision — 2025-04-15" gives 201504. - Explicit pattern in corpus-retrieval-05 step 4: no false accept, but 1,139 of 1,182. It misses all 38 "Current Change Notice: NN-YY" manual headers and 5 notice titles punctuated "NO:", "MANUAL:", "MANUAL, NO:" or "MANUALS NO.". - Keyword gate in corpus-frames-09 step 1: 1,178 of 1,182 (misses "(RE)ISSUED ... - CHANGE NN-YY" without "NO."), and still accepts 10 section titles and 5 SPA document titles that contain "revised" next to an SPA or RFA number. "REVISED PUBLIC NOTICE (SPA #22-0033)" evaluates to 203322, which would be the maximum in collector.ts:201 if it is in the hit set. This finding corrects the fix steps of those two round-one findings; it is not a separate defect in current code.
- **Evidence:** change-markers.ts:15-16 `normalized.match(/\bchange(?:\s+notice)?(?:\s+no\.?)?\s*(\d{1,2})-(\d{2,4})\b/) ??` / `normalized.match(/\b(\d{1,2})-(\d{2,4})\b/);` and :28 `return extractChangeMarkerValue(hit.sectionTitle) ?? extractChangeMarkerValue(hit.documentTitle);`. True-marker families, occurrences (explicit / gated): `REVISED 05/01/11 - CHANGE NO. 04-11` and REISSUED/ISSUED forms 912 (912 / 912); notice titles `CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUAL ...` 200 (195 / 200); `Current Change Notice: 03-23` 38 (0 / 38); other header variants such as `REVISED EFFECTIVE 04/01/10 - CHANGE NO. 04-10` 12 (12 / 12); `REVISED 04/01/10 - CHANGE NOTICE 04-10` 8 (8 / 8); `Change No. 02-24:` 7 (7 / 7); `ISSUED 01/01/11-CHANGE 24-10` 5 (5 / 1). Gated false accepts include `SPA 25-0006 Revised 1-Day Public Notice (Targeted Case Management Services)` as 200625 and `First Revised and Restated RFA 30-2020-052-DHB` as 202030.
- **Impact:** On a 'latest change notice' question collector.ts:201 takes the maximum marker over all hits. Under the gated design a revised SPA public notice in the hit set becomes the global maximum (2033) and is the hit boosted as latest. Under the explicit design manuals that carry the 'Current Change Notice' header have no marker at all, so the amendment frame cannot select their header; the MA-3420 marker 'Current Change Notice: 06-24' cited in corpus-frames-09 belongs to this family. Today every section of an Administrative Letter or SPA inherits a marker from its document title through the fallback at line 28.
- **Fix:** Fold this into corpus-retrieval-05 and corpus-frames-09 as the replacement for their change-markers.ts step. Do not implement either the round-one explicit pattern or the keyword gate. 1. Replace both regexes in extractChangeMarkerValue with the single pattern `/\bchange\s+(?:no\b|notice\b(?:\s+for\s+manuals?)?)?[\s.,:]*(?:no\b[\s.:]*)?(\d{1,2})-(\d{2})(?!\d)/`, with year = 2000 + YY, returning null when the year is later than the current year plus one. On the legacy graph this gives 1,182 of 1,182 section markers and 204 of 204 notice titles with no false accept. That result is in-sample, so re-measure on the next corpus build. 2. Pin table tests, one per family: - "REVISED 05/01/11 - CHANGE NO. 04-11" - "REVISED 04/01/10 - CHANGE NOTICE 04-10" - "CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUAL NO. 09-21, ..." - "CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUAL, NO: 07-15, ..." - "CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUALS NO. 22-08, ..." - "DMA CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUAL 01-14" - "Current Change Notice: 06-24" - "Change No. 02-24:" - "ISSUED 01/01/11-CHANGE 24-10" And negatives that must return null: - "Ages 18-64 Service Limits" - "SPA 25-0026" - "REVISED PUBLIC NOTICE (SPA #22-0033)" - "First Revised and Restated RFA 30-2020-052-DHB" - "DHB ADMINISTRATIVE LETTER NO: 01-24" - "Policy 8C Revision — 2025-04-15" - "ATTACHMENT II (REVISED 09-07)" - the two known misprints "CHANGE NO. 0111" and "CHANGE NO. 17X-11", documented as accepted misses. 3. With the explicit pattern the document-title fallback at line 28 can stay: it produced no false accept on the real document titles. 4. Apply the remaining steps unchanged: corpus-frames-09 steps 2 and 3 (finalize.ts and amendment.ts), and corpus-retrieval-05 step 5 (compute the maximum only over the governing manual's revision headers).
- **How it was verified:** (1) Factual claim: true. I opened packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/change-markers.ts (lines 15-16 and 28 are as quoted; normalizeSearchText in base.ts:23 only lowercases), collector.ts:201-207 (maximum marker over all hits when isLatestChangeNoticeQuery) and rerank.ts:476-494 (the boost for the hit equal to that maximum). I exported every Section title with its count and every Document title read-only from the local legacy Neo4j graph, then ran five parsers over them in a throwaway Node script: the current parser, the corpus-retrieval-05 explicit pattern copied from its fix text, the corpus-frames-09 gated design, the reviewer's label rule and the reviewer's recommended pattern. Every headline number reproduced exactly: - True markers: 479 titles, 1,182 section occurrences, 204 document titles. - Current parser: 1,182 of 1,182, plus 836 false section occurrences; false values on 658 …

### r2-critic-amendment-date-design-07 — Document-keyword tie-break gives all six document slots to change notices ahead of the governing manuals, because only notices carry a date; replacing fabricated dates with real ones keeps this

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/keyword.ts:105`
- **Severity:** low · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** documentKeywordBackend keeps six documents per type group, ordered by title score and then effective_date descending. In the DHHS group only change notices carry a date (204 dated notices, 1,033 undated current documents in the legacy graph, and the current loader still dates only notices), so when notices and manuals tie on title score the notices take the slots. On the legacy graph, for the NEMT-to-dialysis question nine notices and the two NEMT manuals tie at the top score and all six selected are notices; the backend then returns 14 sections, all from notices, scored 0.885 to 1.185. A citizenship and alien requirements question also selects six notices and no manual. This follows from the sort key, so real notice dates preserve it, and nulling notice dates (corpus-retrieval-05) makes the outcome depend on document id. The manuals are not lost from the candidate pool: section_keyword returns MA-2910 and MA-3550 sections for the same question (best 0.875), below every notice hit from this backend. The effect on final ranking and answers was not measured because the hybrid search needs a paid embedding call.
- **Evidence:** keyword.ts:105 `ORDER BY docScore DESC, coalesce(d.effective_date, '') DESC, d.id ASC`, :139 `docLimit: Math.min(DOCUMENT_KEYWORD_DOC_LIMIT, profile.perGroupK),`, scoring-constants.ts:50 `export const DOCUMENT_KEYWORD_DOC_LIMIT = 6;`. The document-selection part of the statement, run read-only with the question's real search terms and docTypes ['dhhs_policy'], returned six rows, all `status: amendment`, docScore 14: notices 02-26, 04-26, 04-26, 02-26 with `eff: 2026-01-01`, then 04-22 and 05-22 with `eff: 2022-01-01`.
- **Impact:** For a topical DHHS question that names no policy number, this backend contributes sections from change notices and none from the manual that states the current rule. Any design that dates notices and not manuals preserves it, so it belongs in the date decision instead of surfacing later as an unexplained evaluation shift.
- **Fix:** Fold this into the notice-date decision from corpus-retrieval-05. In keyword.ts:105, put status before date: `ORDER BY docScore DESC, CASE WHEN coalesce(d.status,'') = 'amendment' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END, coalesce(d.effective_date,'') DESC, d.id ASC`, matching policy-lookup.ts:76-78. Change-notice questions are unaffected because amendment-context.ts serves them separately. Do not add a status key at document-expansion.ts:112. That query is the change-notice header expansion, run only for change-notice queries, where newest-notice-first is intended; it needs the (change_year, change_sequence) ordering from corpus-retrieval-05 instead. At routing.ts:92 add only a final `, s.id ASC`; the query is already limited to status 'amendment'. Add a retrieval-ranking test with a tied notice and manual asserting the manual is selected (tests/search-ranking.test.ts already has a documentKeyword fixture), then run one retrieval evaluation, since this changes which documents feed the candidate pool.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Factual claim: true on the legacy local graph, and the mechanism is in current code. I opened packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/keyword.ts and the ORDER BY at :105 and docLimit at :139 are as quoted; DOCUMENT_KEYWORD_DOC_LIMIT is 6. The loader (packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/loader.py, _extract_effective_date) still returns a date for dhhs_policy only when the title matches the change-notice pattern, so in any build only notices are dated in that group. The graph confirms it: 204 dhhs_policy documents with status 'amendment' are all dated, and 1,033 with status 'current' are all undated. I generated real search terms with the built analyzeQuery and ran the document-selection Cypher read-only. NEMT question: 11 documents tie at docScore 14, nine notices first, MA-2910 and MA-3550 in positions 10 and 11, so all six slots go to notices. Citizenship question: six notic…

## RETR-7 · Retrieval and frame heuristics are tuned to the same questions that gate releases

Severity high · effort L

### architecture-01 — Retrieval and frame heuristics are fitted to the 55-question benchmark that also gates corpus releases

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:359`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** L · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The hand-written retrieval layer (about 40 intent predicates in query-intent.ts plus topic scorers and alias-to-policy rules) contains several branches whose trigger vocabulary matches one or two golden questions: HCWD to MA-2180 (q-009), ambulance not-covered (q-017), hospice "terminally ill"/certification (q-029), waiver application/"demonstrate" (q-010), and home modifications (q-011). It also has one fully anchored regex, isEpsdtOverviewQuery, that matches the production smoke question 'What does EPSDT require?' verbatim. release-validate scores only the 55 original golden phrasings: run_eval is called with no dataset, and require_complete_results in packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/evaluation_evidence.py:80-90 pins the id set to load_dataset(). The non-overridable thresholds are answerScore 2.75, retrievalRecall 0.9 and primaryDocumentHitRate 0.95. The only other question sets are paraphrases of the same golden topics (50 in heldout-paraphrase.json, plus 50 in data/jev/fresh-paraphrases.json used for the Jev routing benchmark). Neither is part of the release gate and neither can detect topic-level fitting. The exact counts (52 phrase lists, 81 constants, 57 planner rules, "at least a dozen" fitted branches) were not independently reproduced. Hospice should be dropped from the impact's list of topics no predicate names, since a hospice-specific predicate exists.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:359-360 `export function isEpsdtOverviewQuery(queryLower: string): boolean {` / `return /^(?:what does epsdt require|what is epsdt|what are (?:the )?epsdt requirements|(?:explain|summarize|describe) epsdt(?: requirements)?)` (anchored at both ends); scripts/ops/epsdt-smoke-batch.mjs:12 `question: 'What does EPSDT require?', startedAt: new Date().toISOString(), attempts: [], passed: false };`; packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/cli/release.py:283 `answer_result = run_eval(level="answer")` with packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/eval/dataset.py:13 `path = path or GOLDEN_DATASET_PATH` and evaluation_evidence.py:83 `expected = {row["id"] for row in load_dataset()}`; packages/pipeline/data/golden/CHANGELOG.md:16-18 `honest re-baseline after the 2026-06-11 de-contamination — the old 2.45 baseline partly measured retrieval code that contained golden-answer text` (wrapped over three lines); packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts:61 `const collectHcwdIncomeHistoryHits = async (): Promise<CorpusRetrievalHit[]> => {` and query-intent.ts:491-492 `if (isHcwdQuery(queryLower)) {` / `policyNumbers.push('MA-2180');` versus golden q-009 `Is there a maximum earned income limit for people on HCWD (Health Coverage for Workers with Disabilities)`. Full heuristic-to-question map in the appendix.
- **Impact:** A user asks about a policy no predicate names (dental, vision, hospice billing, estate recovery, or any of the roughly 4,400 documents outside the ~25 policies the benchmark touches). No topic branch fires, retrieval falls back to the generic path whose quality has never been measured separately, and the release manifest still reports answerScore >= 2.75. A change that degrades the generic path passes the gate as long as the 55 tuned questions pass. The verifier bounds hallucination, not retrieval misses, so the symptom is a confident 'the corpus does not address this' or an answer grounded in the wrong policy.
- **Fix:** Do this in order and stop when the evidence says to. 0. **Free step, no labelling.** Have release-validate also run the answer eval with `dataset=data/golden/heldout-paraphrase.json` (the `--dataset` plumbing exists) and record golden and paraphrase aggregates separately in the manifest. Parameterise require_complete_results by dataset path so each result is checked against its own id set. Report the paraphrase score at first rather than gating on it. 1. **Make the ablation possible.** Add one env flag in policy-corpus (for example POLICY_CORPUS_DISABLE_TOPIC_HEURISTICS=1) that bypasses only the single-policy branches: scoreWaiverApplication, scoreHcwdIncomePremium, scoreModificationReview, the anchored isEpsdtOverviewQuery, collectHcwdIncomeHistoryHits, and the agent planner's family rules. Leave extractAliasedPolicyNumbers out of the arm: program-name to policy-number aliases are domain knowledge and belong in ingest-time alias data regardless of the result. Add a `heuristics` mode to scripts/eval-ab.sh that uses the existing answer_arm with that env var, RUNS=3, and eval-answer-stability. 2. **Small disjoint seed, retrieval-level first.** Write 20-30 questions on policies no predicate, alias or golden question names (dental, vision, hospice, estate recovery and similar). Score them at retrieval level first: it is deterministic, needs no judge, and only requires verified evidence section ids. Compute document ids with pipeline.identity.build_document_id and inspect the parsed bodies (gotcha 127). Add a new `source` value to dataset.schema.json and keep the file under data/golden so the existing .dockerignore exceptions cover it. Write down the rule that this set is report-only: a miss may motivate a generic fix but never a predicate, alias or planner rule naming that topic. That is how the paraphrase set was spent. Grow it to about 100, with answer-level scoring, only if it proves informative. 3. **Decide from the ablation.** If the full stack does not beat the ablated arm beyond run-to-run variance on the paraphrase and disjoint sets, delete the bypassed branches and the flag, and adopt "no predicate that names one policy". For any planner family rule that does earn its keep, move it into PolicyCorpus per AGENTS.md rather than leaving it in the agent processor. 4. **Ingest-time roles only if step 2 shows generic retrieval is weak.** Do not start a new design. Promote the existing deterministic roles in packages/policy-corpus/src/jev/heuristics.ts, plus clinical-policy template numbering, to section properties computed at load time. Qualify them with …
- **How it was verified:** I opened every cited location and all the structural claims hold. I did not reproduce the heuristic counts or measure generalisation, which is why confidence is medium. Confirmed: - isEpsdtOverviewQuery at query-intent.ts:359-360 is a regex anchored at both ends whose first alternative is "what does epsdt require". The production smoke script asks exactly 'What does EPSDT require?'. No golden question has that phrasing (the EPSDT golden questions are q-004 and q-049), so the branch serves the smoke question, not the benchmark. - release-validate calls run_eval(level="retrieval") and run_eval(level="answer") with no dataset argument (cli/release.py:264, 283; again at 588-589 for the baseline). run_eval passes dataset_path=None to load_dataset, which defaults to GOLDEN_DATASET_PATH. require_complete_results pins the id set to load_dataset(). - The gates are non-overridable thresholds from…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted. I looked for a reason a maintainer could dismiss this (accepted trade-off, tracked issue, conflict with an invariant, contrived impact) and found the project's own documents support it instead. What I opened: the golden dataset and its CHANGELOG, the 2026-06-11 RAG faithfulness review and remediation plan, gotchas.md, jev.md, the release CLI, scripts/eval-ab.sh, query-intent.ts, rerank.ts, frame/context/routing.ts, git history for the cited predicate, and issue #188 plus the open issue list. 1. **Known problem, not an accepted trade-off.** The June review rated the same class of problem "high" and said the baseline "is not evidence the system works on unseen questions". The rem…

### rag-design-03 — Routing still encodes individual golden questions, and the held-out set is a paraphrase of the same questions

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/term-lists.ts:100`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Query predicates, term lists, federal hints and the planning checklist carry the section numbers of specific golden evidence labels (q-005: 3.2.1, 3.3, 4.2.1.2) and the components of specific reference answers (q-015), and source comments cite q-045 and q-046 as the reason for rules. The default dataset of `scripts/eval-ab.sh` (`packages/pipeline/data/golden/heldout-paraphrase.json`, overridable via `DATASET`) is a one-to-one paraphrase of golden q-001 to q-050 with byte-identical answers and the same evidence sections (49 of 50 identical section-number sets), and its evidence ids are still in the pre-migration scheme (0 of 50 questions share a section id with dataset.json). The remediation plan shows three further tuning rounds were measured on that same slice. Golden evidence covers 33 documents: 21 of 177 clinical policies, and no fee schedule, SPA, contract, bulletin, waiver or provider manual. The "Answer directly from the current frame before widening further" directive is keyed on lane `baseline_hybrid` (which the general route uses, with widening policy `none`), not on the widening policy itself. The exact firing counts (28 of 55, 27 of 50, 2 of 25) depend on the reviewer's unlisted predicate set and control questions and were not reproduced; an independent choice of 15 topic predicates gave 15 of 55 golden, 14 of 50 held-out and 1 of 25 fresh, which is the same direction at a lower magnitude.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/term-lists.ts:100-111 `export const CONGREGATE_PCS_CRITERIA_SECTION_TERMS = [ '3.2.1', 'specific criteria covered by medicaid', ];` ... `'3.3',` ... `'4.2.1.2',` (golden q-005 evidence is exactly 3.2.1, 3.3 and 4.2.1.2) | packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts:528-538 `function federalCoreConditions(allText: string): string[] {` ... `conditions.push('ordering practitioner requirements');` ... `conditions.push('written plan of care');` ... `conditions.push('60-day review cycle');` (the three components of the q-015 reference answer) | packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/rebalance/federal-authority.ts:123 `// application-process hint wins on questions like q-046.` | scripts/eval-ab.sh:37 `DATASET="${DATASET:-data/golden/heldout-paraphrase.json}"`
- **Impact:** Measured with a throwaway script that imports the pure predicate functions (no network): at least one of 15 topic-specific predicates fires on 28 of 55 golden questions (16 of the original 25), on 27 of 50 held-out paraphrases (only 5 paraphrases lose any predicate their golden twin fired) and on 2 of 25 fresh questions about other common NC Medicaid topics (dentures, wheelchairs, estate recovery, spend-down, a CPT rate). 14 of those 25 fresh questions route to the 'general' family, whose route carries widening_policy 'none' and the directive 'Answer directly from the current frame before widening further.' The 55 golden questions label evidence in 33 documents: 21 of 177 clinical policies, and no fee schedule, state plan amendment, contract, bulletin, waiver or provider manual. The held-out file's evidence ids are still in the pre-migration scheme (0 of 50 match dataset.json). So the 2.7 to 2.8 scores describe tuned topics; behaviour on the other 88% of clinical policies and on whole source families is unmeasured, and model or embedding swaps are approved on the paraphrase set.
- **Fix:** Keep the direction; change the specifics. 1. **Fix the paraphrase file first (mechanical, no paid calls).** For each h-NNN, copy the `evidence` block from its `paraphrase_of` twin in dataset.json, then run eval-validate. Refresh the stale defaults in scripts/eval-ab.sh (voyage-law-2, gpt-5.4, gpt-5.4-mini). Relabel the file in the script header and CHANGELOG as a phrasing-robustness check, not a held-out set. 2. **Author a smaller disjoint set: about 40 questions, not 60 to 100.** - Put it in a separate file (for example data/golden/disjoint.json) and run it with --dataset. Leave dataset.json untouched so the frozen 55-question benchmark in #188 is unaffected. - Stratify over families absent from the golden evidence (fee schedules, SPAs, bulletins, waivers, manuals, contracts) and clinical policies outside the 21 already covered. The by-document, by-family sampler in pipeline/jev/benchmarks.py `prepare_sections` can pick the source sections. - Write each question from the document, with section-level evidence (the schema's unit). Build ids with `pipeline.identity.build_document_id` and verify them with eval-validate (gotcha 127). - Build a matching identity catalog, or run this set without EVAL_IDENTITY_CATALOG; the normalizer raises on evidence missing from the frozen catalog. - Freeze it and never tune on it. 3. **Add an `ablation` mode to eval-ab.sh instead of reusing `agent`.** - `agent` swaps AGENT_MODEL; the new mode should call the existing `answer_arm` with a flag assignment per arm. - Add a paired per-question delta table from the result files; the current verdict compares only mean `overall`. - Two eval-only flags: planning checklist off, and frame off. Frame off must skip AnswerFramePrefetchProcessor and also drop `resolveAnswerFrame` from the tool map. - Reject both flags in production in runtime-config.ts, next to the VERIFIER_ENABLED guard. - Add both to `modelEnvironment` in evaluation_evidence.py, and make `evaluation_fingerprint()` raise when either is set, so an ablated run cannot become release evidence. - Start with one run per arm on the disjoint set. Go to three runs only if the paired delta is within about 0.1 of zero. 4. **Replace "keep only heuristics that help on the disjoint set" with a two-part rule.** - General machinery (prefetch, planning checklist, the 'general' route with widening 'none'): judge it on the disjoint set with the ablations above. - Each topic-specific predicate or term list: it cannot fire off-topic, so test it by deletion. Remove it and run its golden twin plus two or three new same-topic, different-facet …
- **How it was verified:** The claim is true in substance. I confirmed every structural fact from the code and data, but could not reproduce the exact predicate-firing counts, and a few details need correcting. **Confirmed** - **Quoted evidence is verbatim.** All four quotes are at the cited locations. The golden q-005 evidence is exactly sections 3.2.1, 3.3 and 4.2.1.2 of one document, matching the three CONGREGATE_PCS term lists, which are live in coverage-section candidate selection and grouping. - **q-015 match.** The reference answer contains the ordering practitioners (nurse practitioner, clinical nurse specialist, physician assistant), "written plan of care" and "reviewed every 60 days", which are the three things `federalCoreConditions` emits. - **Golden ids in non-test source.** Comments in `federal-authority.ts` (q-046) and `section-predicates.ts` (q-045) name specific golden questions as the reason for…
- **Why this severity:** The finding survives this lens: it is known in outline, but the project treats it as closed, and the gap it describes is not tracked anywhere. What I opened: the gotchas, the 2026-06-11 review and remediation plan, the golden CHANGELOG, scripts/eval-ab.sh, docs/development/jev.md, the 2026-10-04 Jev readiness README, the 2026-09-22 verified-release doc, issue #188, the cited term-list lines, and both golden files (compared with a short script). I did not re-run the reviewer's predicate-firing counts or check the 'general' route's widening policy, since my lens assumes the facts. Grounds for dismissal that I tested and rejected: 1. **Already known and tracked.** The 2026-06-11 review raised …

## RETR-8 · Hit-inferred coverage routing keeps one policy, dropping a second service or the top-ranked document

Severity medium · effort S

### r2-triage-corpus-06 — A question about two policies gets a frame and plan for one: the second policy's sections are removed even when they are top hits

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/document-selection.ts:237`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** When a coverage question names no policy number, documents are inferred from hits and narrowInferredCoverageDocuments (document-selection.ts:237) keeps exactly one unless two share a policy family. This happens whatever the score margin, and the winner is chosen by summed hit score. The coverage pipeline then keeps only that policy's hits, so a question joining two services gets authoritative documents, primary sections and supporting sections from one policy. The plan names one preferred source, tells the model to answer from the current frame before widening, and gives the yes/no directive. Nothing in the frame or plan says a second service was dropped. The only exception is the care-setting preference, which was added for one earlier instance of the same problem. This was reproduced through resolveAnswerFrame and the planning processor on synthetic fixtures with hand-set hit scores, not live retrieval, and the agent was not run. The model may still widen with searchSections and the verifier still gates the answer, so the expected result is a partial or withheld answer, with a wrong verdict for the second service possible but not demonstrated.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/document-selection.ts:233 `if (preferredFamily && (countsByFamily.get(preferredFamily) ?? 0) > 1) {` and :237 `return documents.slice(0, 1);` | packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/routing.ts:160 `const inferredDocuments = await collectPolicyDocumentsFromHits(query, hits, FRAME_DOCUMENT_LIMIT + 1);` and :169 `return narrowInferredCoverageDocuments(settingScopedDocuments);` | packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/coverage.ts:71-72 `const scoped = hits.filter(` `(hit) => hit.policyNumber && preferredPolicyNumbers.has(hit.policyNumber.toUpperCase()),` | packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts:711 `render: () => 'Answer directly from the current frame before widening further.',` and :789 `lines.push(`Preferred sources: ${listDocuments(frame.authoritativeDocuments)}.`);` | Probe p3/twopolicy.out: `===== Does NC Medicaid cover hearing aids and eyeglasses for adults?` `authoritative=7(Hearing Aid Services)` `PRIMARY: 7 §1.0 "1.0 Description of the Procedure, Product, or Service" | 7 §4.1 "4.1 General Criteria Not Covered"` `SUPPORTING: 7 §3.3 "3.3 Hearing Aids for Adults" | 7 §3.1 "3.1 General Criteria Covered" | 7 §2.1.1 "2.1.1 General"` `Preferred sources: 7 (Hearing Aid Services).` `Cover first: § 1.0; § 4.1.`; `===== Are chiropractic services and podiatry services covered for adults?` `authoritative=1C-1(Podiatry Services)` `Preferred sources: 1C-1 (Podiatry Services).`
- **Impact:** "Does NC Medicaid cover hearing aids and eyeglasses for adults?" In a probe on the repo's fixtures the hearing-aid hit scored 0.97 and the eyeglasses hit 0.96. The fixture's eyeglasses section said "not covered for adults". The frame held only Policy 7, Hearing Aid Services. The plan named that one source, gave the yes/no directive and added "Answer directly from the current frame before widening further". "Are chiropractic services and podiatry services covered for adults?" kept only podiatry, although the chiropractic hit ranked first. The likely outcome is a confident yes or no that is right for one service and silent or wrong for the other. The widening policy is one_step, so a careful model can still search for the second service. The agent was not run.
- **Fix:** 1. In collectCoverageRoutingDocuments, keep a second (up to FRAME_DOCUMENT_LIMIT) inferred document when it matches a question focus term that the top document does not. collectPolicyDocumentsFromHits already computes titleMatches and documentMatches per hit against the focus terms; return those per policy and reuse them instead of adding new scoring. Do not gate on score closeness: the current cut ignores margin, and fused scores are not comparable (corpus-retrieval-08). 2. When documents are dropped, record them on the frame and have the planner emit a line naming the service the frame does not cover and telling the model to search for it before answering. This is the cheapest part and is worth doing even if step 1 is deferred. 3. Suppress the two yes/no directives (answer-frame-planning.ts:711-715) when the frame holds more than one non-sibling document or records a dropped one. 4. Add the two probe questions as frame tests and update the unit test at document-selection.test.ts:178, which currently pins the single-document fallback. 5. Before building steps 1 and 3, run one two-service question through the agent to see whether the model widens on its own. Count the result in the corpus-frames-11 measurement of a simpler frame without single-document narrowing.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Is the claim true? Yes. I opened document-selection.ts, frame/context/routing.ts, frame/pipelines/coverage.ts and the agent's answer-frame-planning.ts. narrowInferredCoverageDocuments returns documents.slice(0, 1) for any set of inferred documents in which no policy family repeats, and a unit test pins that behaviour. The only other document path in collectCoverageRoutingDocuments is collectLookupDocuments, which runs first and needs a policy number named in the question. The setting-preference step before narrowing only fires on care-setting terms. coverage.ts then scopes candidate hits and focus hits to the surviving policy number. The planner emits one preferred source, "Answer directly from the current frame before widening further" and the yes/no directive; nothing in the plan flags a dropped service. I did not rerun the reviewer's probe: its output was written on Oct 5, after …

### r2-critic-policy-number-bakeoff-03 — narrowInferredCoverageDocuments lets any two-member family displace the top-ranked document

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/document-selection.ts:232`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** narrowInferredCoverageDocuments (document-selection.ts:222-238) picks the policy family with the most members and ignores rank. On the hit-inferred coverage path (routing.ts:160-169, reached only when no policy number or alias lookup resolves), any two documents sharing a family key displace the top-ranked document. This happens for genuine clinical siblings ([1H, 1T-1, 1M-5, 1M-4] returns [1M-5, 1M-4]) as well as for the coarse 'MA' key that corpus-retrieval-04 reported. Fix 7 of corpus-retrieval-04 (tighten policyFamilyKey) would not fix the clinical case. It would also stop MA-2910/MA-3550 (the NEMT twins, both authoritative for q-037 in the saved 2026-09-22 gate) from being treated as siblings. The pure-function behaviour is reproduced on HEAD. The q-036 frame-level example comes from a lexical-degraded replay on the older local graph; in the saved hybrid gate q-036 resolved 1H through the lookup path, so production frequency is unmeasured.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/document-selection.ts:232-234 `const preferredFamily = Array.from(countsByFamily.entries()).sort((left, right) => right[1] - left[1])[0]?.[0] ?? null;` / `if (preferredFamily && (countsByFamily.get(preferredFamily) ?? 0) > 1) {` / `return documents.filter((document) => policyFamilyKey(document.policyNumber) === preferredFamily);`. Replay with unmodified code, local graph, lexical-degraded mode, golden q-036 "Does Policy 1-H authorize routine general-wellness telehealth visits without a specific clinical indication?": inferred lookups in rank order [1H, 1T-1, 1M-5, 1M-4]; frame authoritative = [1M-5 Home Visit for Postnatal Assessment and Follow-up Care, 1M-4]; primary = 1M-5 2.1.1 and 1M-5 "4.1". Pure call on HEAD: narrowInferredCoverageDocuments([1H, 1T-1, 1M-5, 1M-4]) returns the third and fourth. With the extraction fix only, "My father has stage 3b kidney disease; is dialysis education covered?" infers [1A-34 Dialysis Services, 11B-4, MA-3540, MA-2905] and the frame becomes [MA-3540, MA-2905] with no primary section; with the top-document rule it is [1A-34] with primary 1A-34 1.1.3 and 3.2.1. Saved hybrid gate (2026-09-22 final-gate-full.json): q-037 frame authoritative = [MA-2910, MA-3550], the ABD and Family-and-Children NEMT twins, golden evidence in MA-3550; a tightened key would drop the twin.
- **Impact:** A question that names no number, or whose number is not extracted, can get a frame built on a sibling pair that ranked below the right policy. In the saved hybrid run q-036 routed to 1H, so the frequency in hybrid mode is unmeasured; the mechanism does not depend on the retrieval mode.
- **Fix:** Adopt the reviewer's rule in place of fix 7 of corpus-retrieval-04: - In narrowInferredCoverageDocuments, compute the family of documents[0]. Return that family's members when there is more than one; otherwise return documents.slice(0, 1). - Leave policyFamilyKey and the tests that pin 'MA' unchanged. Two additions: - Record the residual in a test or comment: with the 'MA' key, a top-ranked MA manual still keeps any unrelated MA manual in the set (for example [MA-2910, 15, MA-3540]). If that matters, handle it with a title or program-twin check for MA manuals, not by tightening the key. - Before merging, run the full hybrid golden set with isolated observability storage and compare routed authoritative documents per question. The new rule trusts rank 1, so look for any question where the correct sibling pair previously won over a top-ranked distractor. Fold this into the corpus-retrieval-04 work item rather than tracking it separately.
- **How it was verified:** Not refuted. The mechanism is real and reproduced on HEAD; how often it fires in hybrid mode is unmeasured, and I did not re-run the reviewer's frame replays. (1) Is the claim true? Yes for the code; the end-to-end examples are only partly confirmed. - I read document-selection.ts:208-238. narrowInferredCoverageDocuments counts documents per policyFamilyKey, takes the highest count, and returns that family if it has more than one member. Rank is never consulted, so a lower-ranked pair beats the top document. - I ran the real function with tsx from the verify2 directory. [1H, 1T-1, 1M-5, 1M-4] returns [1M-5, 1M-4]. [1A-34, 11B-4, MA-3540, MA-2905] returns [MA-3540, MA-2905]. [3K-2, MA-2180, MA-3550] returns the two MA manuals. - No guard prevents this. routing.ts:160-169 is the only caller. It is reached whenever collectLookupDocuments is empty, and it passes hit-inferred documents in sc…

## RETR-9 · Prior-authorisation frames fill with tied template headings and demote the Prior Approval section

Severity medium · effort S

### r2-critic-coverage-selection-core-04 — Prior-authorisation frames are filled by tied template headings because 'criteria' is a title term; the policy's own Prior Approval section is left out or stripped

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/term-lists.ts:192`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** In resolvePriorAuthFrame, title-matched sections of the routing document are placed ahead of retrieval hits, and the title terms include the bare word 'criteria'. Every template heading containing it (3.1, 3.2, 3.2.x, 4.1, 4.2, 4.2.x) ties at the same score with '5.1 Prior Approval' and '5.2 Prior Approval Requirements'. The tie is broken by the section listing order, which comes from a Cypher collect() with no ORDER BY, so which four sections become primary depends on an unspecified graph ordering; with a median of 11 ties per policy filling the 8 document slots, retrieval hits normally cannot reach primary at all. The Jev role ranker that could reorder them is off by default. Hits that lose the slots are nominated as avoid sections with no match requirement and no scoping to the routing document, and their text is stripped. Rebalance additionally demotes any section whose body (not only title) contains 'phone', 'fax', 'web portal' or 'requesting prior authorization'. Observed on recorded September gate frames: q-014's golden section 3L-1 5.6 shipped as an avoid section with no text, alongside the sibling policy 3L's 5.6, while two 'Not Covered' headings were primary; q-033 put 'Attachment K: Nursing Facility Quality Initiatives' in the frame through the Policy 9 'attachment k' term.
