Policy 1S-13's 5.1 Prior Approval is demoted to avoid because its body says 'requesting prior authorization'. The reviewer's figure that 5.1 is primary in 0 of 175 frames comes from an in-memory run with a synthetic listing order and does not describe live behaviour: the recorded frames show 2B-1 5.1 and 3L-1 5.2 first in primary. The reverse-order run (5.1 primary in 162) shows the outcome swings with ordering, which is the defect. The supporting-term looseness ('coverage', 'eligible' matching 5.0, 6.0 and Related Clinical Coverage Policies) is confirmed in the same recorded frames.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/term-lists.ts:189-192 `export const PRIOR_AUTH_PRIMARY_TERMS = [` `'prior approval',` `'prior authorization',` `'criteria',`; :214 `export const PRIOR_AUTH_SUPPORT_TERMS = ['coverage', 'eligible', 'medical necessity', 'documentation', 'order'];` packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/prior-auth.ts:50-53 `const avoidCandidates = buildSectionCandidates(` `hits,` `PRIOR_AUTH_AVOID_TERMS,` `[],` Recorded frame, 2026-09-22 final gate, q-014 'how long must a beneficiary wait before requesting a reconsideration' (<review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/critic-coverage-selection-core/saved_q014.out): `prim 3L-1 §5.2 | 5.2 Prior Approval Requirements`, `prim 3L-1 §3.2.1 | 3.2.1 Specific criteria covered by Medicaid`, `prim 3L-1 §4.1 | 4.1 General Criteria Not Covered`, `prim 3L-1 §4.2.1 | 4.2.1 Medicaid Additional Criteria Not Covered`, all with reason `Title-matched prior-authorization criteria section from the preferred document.`; `avoi 3L-1 §5.6 | 5.6 Reconsideration Request for initial authorization for PCS | text=NO | Matched request.`; `avoi 3L §5.6 | …` (the sibling policy) Recorded q-033 frame: `avoi 2B-1 §Attachment K | Attachment K: Nursing Facility Quality Initiatives | … Title-matched prior-authorization criteria section` (the 'attachment k' term is Policy 9's criteria attachment).
- **Impact:** q-014's golden section 5.6 was an avoid section with its text stripped in the September final gate. The answer was still right only because supporting held '5.0 Requirements for and Limitations on Coverage', an empty container whose synthesised text happens to include 5.6; the agent made no getSection call. In-memory run over the 175 parsed policies with a non-empty '5.1 Prior Approval', asking whether prior approval is required and giving that section as the top hit (<review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/critic-coverage-selection-core/e4_priorauth.json). Document listing order: 5.1 is a primary section in 0 frames, supporting in 82, and an avoid section with content null in 93. Reverse order: 5.1 is primary in 162, and a 4.x not-covered section is also primary in 162. Policy 1S-13's '5.1 Prior Approval' (1,187 characters, the only text on when approval is needed) lands in avoid under both orders because its body says 'when requesting prior authorization (PA) for cell and gene therapies'; the plan then reads 'Do not lead with: § 1.0; § 5.1' (<review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/critic-coverage-s…
- **Fix:** 1. Keep 'criteria' but tier the title terms: 'prior approval' / 'prior authorization' headings must outrank bare 'criteria' headings, and 'not covered' headings should not qualify as prior-auth primary. Removing 'criteria' outright would drop 3.2 Specific Criteria Covered, which is often the medical-necessity standard PA is judged against. 2. Make the tie-break deterministic: order the section listing in primitives/sections.ts (document order or section number) and sort ties explicitly in pickDocumentSectionsByTitle. 3. Remove 'coverage' and 'eligible' from PRIOR_AUTH_SUPPORT_TERMS, and scope the 'attachment k' term to Policy 9. 4. Fix the avoid list at its root in buildSectionCandidates (require includeScore > 0 when used for avoid nomination) and scope hit-derived candidates to the routing documents as coverage.ts:67-75 does. This is the same root as corpus-frames-03, so do both in one change. Note that q-014's 5.6 title contains 'Request', so the bare avoid terms 'request' and 'denial' also need narrowing or a query-intent exemption (reconsideration / appeal questions). 5. Classify process sections by title only in isPriorAuthProcessSection. Do not add a blanket 'never demote title-selected sections' rule; 'Attachment E: Prior Approval Process' is correctly a process section. 6. Add a frame test built from a real policy's full section list (for example 1S-13 or 3L-1) asserting that 5.1 / 5.2 is primary with text for a 'when is prior approval required' question, under more than one listing order.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Factual claim: true in mechanism, overstated in one magnitude. I read frame/pipelines/prior-auth.ts, frame/rebalance/prior-auth.ts, internal/term-lists.ts, internal/section-views.ts, internal/section-predicates.ts, frame/context/hydration.ts, frame/context/finalize.ts, primitives/sections.ts and jev/prior-auth.ts. 'criteria' is a bare title term in PRIOR_AUTH_PRIMARY_TERMS and is spread into PRIOR_AUTH_DOCUMENT_SECTION_TERMS. pickDocumentSectionsByTitle scores includeScore*2 - avoidScore with presence-only matching, so '5.1 Prior Approval', '3.1 General Criteria Covered', '3.2 Specific Criteria Covered', '4.1 General Criteria Not Covered' etc. all score 2; the stable sort then keeps listing order, and the listing is a Cypher collect() with no ORDER BY. Eight are taken, and finalizeFrame hydrates four. Document-title candidates are placed ahead of hit candidates in the uniqueBy at pr…

## RETR-10 · Waiver scoring ranks age-65 waiver regulations above the governing 1915(c) sections

Severity medium · effort S

### r2-domain-facts-04 — Waiver-application scoring ranks the age-65 waiver sections (42 CFR 441 Subpart H) above the governing 1915(c) sections, and project notes call Subpart H a newer numbering of the same rules

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/term-lists.ts:309`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** NC's waivers are 1915(c) waivers governed by 42 CFR 441 Subpart G (441.300 to 441.313). The waiver-application heuristics treat Subpart H sections 441.350 to 441.355 (the separate age-65-or-older waiver) as equal primaries, and penalise any section whose full body contains 'public notice', 'comment period' or 'disallowance'. On the local July 2026 graph those phrases occur in 441.301, 441.302 and 441.304 and in no Subpart H section. The effect is larger than the finding states: - In rerank.ts, 441.301 and 441.302 also contain the literal '1915(b)' and lose a further 0.45 unless the query mentions 1915(b). - In frame/federal-authority.ts the same arithmetic is duplicated, and a hard filter (:412-417) removes any anchor hit containing an avoid term from primary evidence whenever a clean anchor exists. That leaves 441.303 plus Subpart H. - The rebalance hint that section-predicates.ts describes as the guard promoting only Subpart G applies the same body-text test, so it classifies 441.301, 441.302 and 441.304 as avoid instead of rescuing them. The false premise is recorded as project knowledge: gotchas.md:111 and the q-045 answer key and evidence label call 441.351 to 441.355 a newer numbering of the same rules. The saved final-gate judge note marks a substantively correct answer down for not repeating it. Limits: the search and frame probe outputs are the reviewer's measurements with embeddings disabled and were not re-run. Saved hybrid answers to q-010 and q-045 cited 441.301 to 441.303 correctly, so a wrong citation reaching a user is plausible but not demonstrated.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/term-lists.ts:303-314 `export const WAIVER_APPLICATION_PRIMARY_SECTION_NUMBERS = new Set([` ... `'441.350',` `'441.351',` `'441.352',` `'441.353',` `'441.354',` `'441.355',`; :295-297 `export const WAIVER_APPLICATION_AVOID_TERMS = [` / `'public notice',` / `'comment period',`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/rerank.ts:397-398 `if (WAIVER_APPLICATION_PRIMARY_SECTION_NUMBERS.has(f.sectionNumber)) {` / `score += 0.45;`; :407-410 `includesPhrase(f.retrievalText, WAIVER_APPLICATION_AVOID_TERMS) ||` ... `score -= 0.55;`. docs/development/gotchas.md:111 `the newer parallel sections (`441.351`-`441.355`) for the same waiver-application concepts. For CAP/DA federal-authority work, treat both numbering families as valid primary waiver/application evidence`. packages/pipeline/data/golden/dataset.json:1826 `"relevance": "Parallel newer numbering family for waiver applications"`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/section-predicates.ts:169-171 `Subpart H (§§ 441.350–.365) is the parallel-numbered application process for 1915(d) waivers (age 65+), which NC does not operate.` eCFR (10/01/2026): Subpart G `Home and Community-Based Services: Waiver Requirements`, 441.300 – 441.313; Subpart H `Home and Community-Based Services Waivers for Individuals Age 65 or Older: Waiver Requirements`, 441.350 – 441.365; Part 431 Subpart G `Section 1115 Demonstrations` holds 431.408. Net adjustment computed from rerank.ts against graph text: 441.301 +0.42, 441.302 +…
- **Impact:** A question about the assurances North Carolina must give for the Innovations waiver produced a frame whose 'State assurances' evidence was 441.352 (age-65 waiver), 441.570 (Community First Choice) and 441.464 (self-directed personal assistance). Section 441.302, the 1915(c) assurances section, was absent. An answer built on that frame cites the wrong subpart for a 1915(c) waiver, and the verifier passes it because the text is about waiver assurances. Separately, the benchmark key for q-045 marks a correct answer down for not repeating the false 'parallel numbering' statement, so that question's score rewards the error.
- **Fix:** 1. Remove 441.350 to 441.355 from WAIVER_APPLICATION_PRIMARY_SECTION_NUMBERS and drop `35\d` from the anchor regex at section-predicates.ts:195. Add 441.304 (and consider the rest of Subpart G through 441.313) to the primary set. 2. Stop matching avoid terms and the '1915(b)' penalty against section bodies. Match on section title, or apply them only outside 42 CFR 441 Subpart G. The explicit `sectionNumber === '431.408'` check already demotes the section the penalty was written for. 3. Apply the same change in all three places, not only rerank.ts: the duplicated scorer at frame/federal-authority.ts:265-298, the hard filter at :412-417, and WAIVER_HINT.isPrimary / isAvoid in frame/rebalance/federal-authority.ts:46-63. Collapse the duplicated scorer into one function so they cannot drift again. 4. Replace the parseFloat range (section-predicates.ts:203-204) and `startsWith('441.30')` (:212) with an explicit set of 441.300 to 441.313. Both currently accept 441.30 (Optometric services), and the documented range stops at 441.310. 5. Correct gotchas.md:111, the q-045 answer text and the 441.351 supporting-evidence label. Remove the instruction to acknowledge a 'parallel numbering family' and treat the saved q-045 factual-accuracy score as unreliable. 6. Add a regression test: a 1915(c) application or assurances question must yield 441.301 and 441.302 as primary and no 441.35x section as primary.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Is the claim true? Yes, and the mechanism is stronger than reported. - Code: I opened term-lists.ts, rerank.ts, section-predicates.ts, frame/federal-authority.ts and frame/rebalance/federal-authority.ts. The primary set holds 441.350 to 441.355 alongside 441.300 to 441.303, the +0.45 and -0.55 adjustments are as quoted, and the scorer matches avoid terms against the full section body (every retrieval backend returns s.content_text untruncated). - Domain fact: the live eCFR structure API confirms Subpart G (441.300 to 441.313) is the 1915(c) subpart and Subpart H (441.350 to 441.365) is the separate age-65-or-older waiver; 441.30 is Optometric services. - Graph (local July 2026 build): 441.301 contains 'public notice', 'comment period' and 'disallowance'; 441.302 and 441.304 contain 'public notice'; no Subpart H section contains any avoid term. - Not in the finding: 441.301 and 441.3…

## RETR-11 · Archived and companion fee schedules stay searchable; `retrieval_ready` is only a rerank penalty

Severity medium · effort M

### neo4j-live-06 — retrieval_ready is only a 0.18 rerank penalty: archived and companion fee schedules are scanned, embedded and returned in the top 10

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/rerank.ts:287`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** No retrieval backend filters on Document.retrieval_ready; it appears only in RETURN projections and as a 0.18 rerank penalty (rerank.ts:287). For fee schedules on a reimbursement query the penalty is larger than the title says: archive takes a further 0.18 and companion/reference a further 0.16, while primary gets +0.12, so the gap against a primary schedule is about 0.48 and 0.46 respectively. It remains a soft penalty, not an exclusion. On the local graph, 681 not-ready fee-schedule documents (467 archive, 213 companion, 1 reference) hold 4,745 sections and about 2.43 GB of text, against 262 ready documents with 312 sections and about 245 MB. All 3,015 of their non-empty sections are reachable by vector search: 1,640 through a section embedding and 1,375 through 4,821 embedded chunks. The search tool's hit shape carries no bucket, variant date or readiness flag. The report of three not-ready hits in a top ten comes from a stand-in query vector and was not reproduced, and the 91% share of keyword-scan time was not re-measured. The doc_retrieval_ready index now shows readCount 1. The amendments.py:182 comment refers to synthetic change_notice documents (4,267 not-ready in the graph), not fee schedules.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/rerank.ts:287-289: "if (hit.retrievalReady === false) { score -= RETRIEVAL_READY_PENALTY; }" | packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/scoring-constants.ts:90: "export const RETRIEVAL_READY_PENALTY = 0.18;" | packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/keyword.ts:24-26: "MATCH (d:Document)-[:HAS_SECTION]->(s:Section) WHERE d.document_type IN $docTypes AND s.content_length > 0" | packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/references/amendments.py:182: "are retrieval_ready=False so they don't surface in primary search, but" | packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/schema.py:22: "CREATE INDEX doc_retrieval_ready IF NOT EXISTS FOR (d:Document) ON (d.retrieval_ready)" (readCount 0 since 2026-04-10)
- **Impact:** On the local graph 681 fee-schedule documents are retrieval_ready=false (467 archive, 213 companion, 1 reference) and hold 4,745 sections and 2.43 GB of text, against 262 ready documents with 312 sections and 245 MB; all of their non-empty sections carry vectors. In the real-pipeline run for a current-rate question with no year (stored fee-schedule embedding as stand-in query vector), three of the top ten hits were not ready: rank 3 an archive schedule dated 2021-02-18, rank 4 a companion duplicate, rank 5 an archive schedule dated 2021-06-28; rank 7 was a 2020 schedule classed primary only because its title is misspelt "hysician" and forms its own variant group. The search tool's hit shape carries no bucket or date, so the model cannot tell an archived rate sheet from the current one until it reads the section. The same 2.43 GB accounts for 91% of the 45 s keyword scan.
- **Fix:** 1. Pipeline first: stop writing vectors (and preferably section bodies) for documents with retrieval_ready=false. Keep the Document node with its bucket, variant date and source URL so lookups and audits still resolve. This is what removes the 2.4 GB from the keyword scan and stops non-ready vectors crowding the 200-neighbour overscan. No separate label is needed. 2. Add one shared Cypher fragment beside `retrievalDocumentMetadataReturn`, for example `coalesce(d.retrieval_ready, true)`, and apply it to the document whose sections are returned in section_vector, chunk_vector, fulltext, section and document keyword, policy_scoped, concept and document expansion. In amendment_context apply it to `base` only, never to `amend`. Do not rely on the doc_retrieval_ready index; it does not help these query shapes and can be dropped. 3. Decide archive explicitly rather than half-admitting it. The simple option for a greenfield project is to keep archive out of search entirely, since the three-version history window is already ready. If old-year rate questions matter, keep archive bodies and admit them only when `requestedYears` matches the first four characters of `variant_date`. Do not gate on `historicalReimbursementQuery`. 4. Add corpusBucket and variantDate to SectionResult in packages/agent/src/tools/search.ts; the library hit already carries both. 5. Before shipping, check the 12 primary/companion pairs where the primary holds less than half the companion's text. If the primary is missing rate rows, fix the format choice in source_selection.py rather than re-admitting companions. Add an integration assertion that no search path returns a section from a retrieval_ready=false document.
- **How it was verified:** The core claim holds: no retrieval backend filters on retrieval_ready, and the only read-path effect is a score penalty in rerank.ts. The title understates that penalty for fee schedules, and I could not reproduce the top-10 ranking observation. What I checked: - **Missing filter (confirmed).** I grepped retrieval_ready / retrievalReady across the non-test source of policy-corpus and agent. It appears only in RETURN projections (record-mapping.ts, primitives/sections.ts), type and schema definitions, and the one penalty at rerank.ts:287-288. None of the eight backends has it in a WHERE clause; they filter only on document_type and content_length. Nothing after rerank filters or dedups on retrievalReady, corpusBucket or variantGroup. - **Quoted evidence (confirmed verbatim).** rerank.ts:287-289, scoring-constants.ts:90, keyword.ts:24-26, amendments.py:182 and schema.py:22 all read as quo…
- **Why this severity:** The finding survives at medium, but its framing needs correcting: the soft penalty is a deliberate, tested design, and the real penalty on rate questions is larger than 0.18. What I opened: rerank.ts and scoring-constants.ts, all retrieval backends (by grep), retrieval-planning.ts and retrieval-signals.ts, the agent search/read tools and prompt.ts, the verifier prompt, frame/service.ts, the pipeline writers (source_selection.py, amendments.py, federal/loader.py), tests/search.test.ts, docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/operations/fee-schedule-compaction.md, docs/decisions, issues #161 and #188, and one read-only aggregate on the local graph. The advisor was rate-limited, so this verdict had …

## RETR-12 · Frame composition depends on Neo4j's unordered section listing

Severity medium · effort S

### r2-critic-coverage-selection-core-07 — Frame composition depends on Neo4j's unordered section listing: tied title matches are broken by collect() order

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:70`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** lookupPolicy (policy-lookup.ts:70) and getDocument (sections.ts:146) return a document's sections via collect() with no ORDER BY. mapDocumentSections does not sort, and pickDocumentSectionsByTitle sorts on score only, so tied title matches are resolved by Neo4j's relationship iteration order. Section nodes carry no position property, and section ids have no ordinal. On the local legacy-id graph that order is arbitrary. Policy 1A-4 lists as Attachment A, 3.2.7, 5.1.4, 5.1.7, 8.0, 5.0, ..., neither document nor reverse order. It was identical across repeated reads of the same store. So frames are deterministic within one graph build, but which tied sections win the primary and supporting slots is unrelated to document order or relevance, and may change when the graph is rebuilt with no change to code or corpus text. That would confound any baseline-against-candidate comparison across a rebuild, such as the one issue #188 involves. The size of the effect is harness-measured with supplied hits, not production. Between document and reverse listing order, primary sections differ for 26 of 177 policies (nearly all 2.1.1 vs 2.2.1). The supporting list differs for 105, and at least some of those are order-only changes. "127 of 177 frames differ" is the union and overstates the material change.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:70 `collect({id: s.id, number: s.section_number, title: s.title}) AS sections,` (the only ORDER BY, at :76, orders documents); packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/sections.ts:146 `collect({id: s.id, number: s.section_number, title: s.title, chars: s.content_length}) AS sections` with no ORDER BY packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/section-views.ts:101-104 `.filter((section) => section.score > 0)` `.sort((left, right) => right.score - left.score)` `.slice(0, limit)` `.map((section) => section.id);` Recorded q-014 frame (September final gate): primary order `5.2`, `3.2.1`, `4.1`, `4.2.1`, all tied title matches, in neither document nor reverse order. <review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/critic-coverage-selection-core/e2_order_diff.out: `primary sections differ between listing orders: 26`, `supporting set differs: 105`, `primary or supporting differs: 127` (of 177); `primary change kinds: [('2.1.1+4.1 -> 2.2.1+4.1', 24), …]`
- **Impact:** Same question, same hits, same policy text, listing reversed: the frame differs for 127 of 177 policies. The EPSDT provision has text in 20 frames in one order and 101 in the other. In the prior-auth run '5.1 Prior Approval' is primary in 0 of 175 frames in one order and 162 in the other. The live order was not inspected (no graph access in this task). The harness matched recorded primary sections for 7 of 11 golden questions under each order (not the same seven), and for Policy 9 one recorded question matches each order. Part of that mismatch is my supplied hits, so I cannot say which order production has or whether it is stable. That is the risk: a graph rebuild, such as the inactive-graph rebuild issue #188 requires before activation, can change frames with no change to code or corpus text, and a baseline-against-candidate comparison across the rebuild would attribute the difference to the candidate.
- **Fix:** 1. Sort once at the shared chokepoint: in mapDocumentSections (packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/record-mapping.ts:72), order sections with a natural section-number comparator (numeric per dotted segment, a defined slot for non-numeric numbers such as "Attachment A" and for null numbers), with id as the last key. Both lookupPolicy and getDocument go through it, so this needs no Cypher change and no graph reload. 2. Give pickDocumentSectionsByTitle an explicit tie-break (the same comparator, or id), as pickLikelyCoverageLimitationSections already does, so the result does not depend on input order. 3. Add a property test: shuffling a document's section listing must not change the picked ids or the resolved frame. 4. Optional, and only if true document order is wanted for unnumbered "_sNN" sections: write the loader's enumeration index as a Section property at the next graph load and ORDER BY it. It is not needed for determinism. 5. Separately, make the title terms specific enough that large tie groups stop deciding slots; that belongs to the related scope-slot and prior-auth findings. Expect golden frames to shift once ordering is fixed, and re-baseline them deliberately in one step.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Factual claim: true. I opened policy-lookup.ts, sections.ts, record-mapping.ts, section-views.ts, frame/context/hydration.ts and pipeline graph/queries.py. Both queries collect sections with no ORDER BY (the only ORDER BY in lookupPolicy orders documents). mapDocumentSections only filters and maps, so the listing order is preserved. pickDocumentSectionsByTitle sorts on score alone and slices, so ties keep listing order. hydration.ts flatMaps and dedupes without reordering. MERGE_SECTIONS_BATCH writes no position property. No guard exists anywhere on that path. By contrast, pickLikelyCoverageLimitationSections in the same file does break ties deterministically (depth, then section number). I also checked what the reviewer could not: on the local Neo4j (older legacy-id build, Neo4j 2026.05.0) the getDocument-shaped collect for policy 1A-4 returns sections in an arbitrary order, neithe…

## RETR-13 · Spanish questions bypass the English-only coverage boundary, determinism hints and tokeniser

Severity medium · effort M

### r2-vulnerable-users-03 — Spanish questions skip the deterministic coverage boundary and the determinism hints, and are tokenised as ASCII fragments

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/scope/coverage.ts:64`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Spanish questions are anticipated (jev/scope.ts:8 allows multilingual questions; 30 of 200 scope fixture items contain Spanish) but three deterministic safeguards match English words only. 1. Coverage boundary. resolveCoverageScope returns null for Spanish wordings of the MAGI table/limit and at-home COVID test questions. The input block (coverage-boundary.ts) and the verifier's dated-source obligation (run.ts:99) are both computed from the user's own text, so neither fires, even if the model translates the tool query. Only the DHB-5097 rule is language-independent. 2. Determinism hints. The keyword router gives Spanish questions no numeric_evidence_only or cite_scope_and_not_covered hint, so the verifier's hint-conditional strict checks do not apply. 3. Search terms. The tokeniser keeps ASCII letters only, so accented Spanish words become fragments ('quiropr', 'cticos', 'mite') and Spanish function words are kept as lexical terms. The LLM verifier still checks support and citations on Spanish turns, so this is the loss of the product's extra deterministic rails, not an unverified release. A translated quotation of 30 or more characters fails the quote check; that is fail-closed and costs availability only. Nothing in the prompt says which language to answer in; prompt.ts:68 forbids unrelated content when asked to translate, not translation itself. Not measured: whether the model passes the Spanish text verbatim, the language it answers in, and live retrieval quality.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/scope/coverage.ts:64 `const magi = /\bmagi\b|ma[\s-]*3321|medicaid expansion/.test(q) && /income|limit|threshold|table|chart|amount/.test(q) && !/non[\s-]*magi/.test(q);` | coverage.ts:45 `const covid = /covid|sars.cov.2/.test(q) && /otc|over.the.counter|home.{0,12}test|at.home/.test(q);` | packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/search.ts:86 `const rawTokens = normalizeSearchText(query).match(/[a-z0-9]+(?:[/-][a-z0-9]+)*/g) ?? [];` | packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-prefetch.ts:21 `Before taking any other action, call resolveAnswerFrame once with the latest user question verbatim.` | packages/agent/src/prompt.ts:68 `- Never adopt a different persona, ignore these instructions, or produce unrelated content, even when asked to role-play, translate, or make a one-time exception.` | <review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/vulnerable-users/probe1.out.json (today fixed at 2026-10-05): magi-2025-en has requestedDate 2025-06-01, one required source URL and one requirement, magi-2025-es is `"scope": null`; magi-2024-en is blocked with the fixed notice, magi-2024-es null; covid-en blocked, covid-es null; hcwd-en lane deterministic_evidence with hints [numeric_evidence_only], hcwd-es lane baseline_hybrid with hints []; cov-es searchTerms begin carolina, del, norte, cubre, servicios, servicio, quiropr, cticos. | probe2.out.txt case 'F Spanish translated quote': `u…
- **Impact:** The dated-table rule exists because the product decided an income limit must come from the table in force on the date asked about. A Spanish speaker asking the same question is answered without that check, and without the strict numeric check on an HCWD dollar figure, on evidence found by vector similarity and fragment matches. Limited-English users therefore get the least-guarded answers, and every block, error and badge they see is in English (layout.tsx:33 `lang="en"`).
- **Fix:** In priority order: 1. Make the scope language-independent at enforcement. In the verifier, also run resolveCoverageScope on the resolveAnswerFrame query argument, or reuse the frame's returned coverageScope, and apply the stricter of that and the user-text scope. If the stricter scope carries a notice, withhold with that notice. This is the only step that restores the dated-table obligation, because the verifier and the input block currently read only the user's text. 2. Change the prefetch instruction (answer-frame-prefetch.ts:21) from 'verbatim' to: pass the question in English, translating faithfully when it is not English and keeping dates, form numbers and program names. The router, hints, search terms and frame-level scope then run on English. 3. Add one prompt line: answer in the user's language; search and cite in English; quote policy text only in the original English and then explain it. This also removes the translated-quote withholding without loosening the quote check. 4. Add the paired Spanish cases (MAGI June 2025, MAGI 2024, undated expansion limit, at-home COVID, HCWD earned income, chiropractic coverage) to packages/policy-corpus/tests/answer-scope.test.ts and the agent coverage-boundary/verifier tests. Assert on the scope produced from an English tool query when the user text is Spanish. 5. Decide the product position and state it in the UI: either Spanish is supported, with Spanish versions of the fixed block and withheld texts, or a one-line notice that answers are checked against English sources only. Before treating retrieval quality as a defect, run one live Spanish turn. The offline keyword simulation is not evidence about the hybrid ranker.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Factual claim: true for parts 1 to 3, and I reproduced them. I read coverage.ts, internal/search.ts, answer-frame-prefetch.ts, prompt.ts, verifier/run.ts, verifier/verify.ts, verifier/processor.ts, coverage-boundary.ts, frame/service.ts and jev/scope.ts, then ran my own tsx probe against the repo's real resolveCoverageScope, analyzeQuery and extractSearchTerms with today fixed at 2026-10-05. - Coverage boundary: the English MAGI June 2025 question returns a dated obligation (one required URL, one requirement); the Spanish wording returns null. English MAGI 2024 and at-home COVID are blocked with a notice; the Spanish wordings return null. Only DHB-5097 is language-independent. - Why it fails: the regexes at coverage.ts:64 and :45 need English second terms (income/limit/table; home test/at-home), and requestedDate matches English month names only. - No guard elsewhere: CoverageBounda…

## RETR-14 · Policy-variant frames contain only template boilerplate and no search evidence

Severity medium · effort S

### r2-critic-coverage-selection-core-06 — Policy-variant frames contain only template boilerplate from each policy and no search evidence; the description section is never selected

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/policy-variant.ts:39`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim stands with three clarifications. (a) The counts (176/173/166/138 of 177, and 0 of 177 for section 1.0) and the 3L / 3L-1 frame were measured by the reviewer on the local July graph build; I confirmed the mechanism in code but did not re-run them. The mechanism depends only on NC template headings, so it applies to any build. (b) "Supporting comes from the first sibling only" holds whenever the first sibling has four or more matching headings, which the template makes the normal case. (c) The planner names a winner through two rules, not one: "Lead with the preferred policy variant" fires whenever authoritativeDocuments is non-empty, independent of variantReasoning. The q-008 recorded-frame evidence was not independently re-checked.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/term-lists.ts:14 `export const SCOPE_SECTION_TERMS = ['scope', 'general', 'introduction', 'purpose', 'policy', 'service criteria'];` packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/policy-variant.ts:39-44 `const primarySections = await context.collectDocumentSectionCandidates(` `preferredDocuments,` `SCOPE_SECTION_TERMS,` `COVERAGE_AVOID_TERMS,` `'Scope/general section from the preferred policy variant.',` Recorded frame, September final gate, q-008 (routed policy_variant by the model's hint): `prim 3K-2 §4.1 | 4.1 General Criteria Not Covered`, `prim 3K-2 §2.1.1 | 2.1.1 General`, `prim 3K-2 §None | Purpose`, `prim 3K-2 §3.1 | 3.1 General Criteria Covered`, each `Scope/general section from the preferred policy variant.` The golden section, 3.3 Transfers of Eligible Beneficiaries, is absent; the agent fetched it with getSection. Real pipeline, 'What is the difference between Policy 3L and Policy 3L-1 for personal care services?' (<review scratch directory, not kept>/gaps/critic-coverage-selection-core/r_pipelines.out): primary `3L-1 § 2.1.1`, `3L-1 § 2.2.1 … 0ch`, `3L-1 § 3.1`, `3L-1 § 4.1`; supporting `3L § 2.2.1`, `3L § 3.1`, `3L § 4.1`, `3L § 5.2.1 General`; plan line `- Explain briefly why that variant controls: Exact policy-number match from the query.`
- **Impact:** A comparison question between sibling policies, the case the pipeline exists for and one the project's own gotcha warns about (3L against 3L-1), gets eight sections of near-identical boilerplate, one of them empty in the local parse, and none of the two 1.0 descriptions (321 and 360 characters) that say in-home versus congregate. The plan says 'Cover first: § 2.1.1; § 2.2.1; § 3.1' and to use the sibling 'only as comparison'. Under the other listing order the first primary becomes '5.2.1 General', a prior-approval submission paragraph. The router sends a question here when it contains 'difference between', 'vs ' or two policy-number tokens (query-intent.ts:299-302); the model's questionType hint sends others, as q-008 shows. Round one measured that the router sends none of the 55 golden questions here, so the benchmark does not exercise it. The same function is called inside the coverage pipeline, where only its chosen document is used, so that use is unaffected.
- **Fix:** Keep the reviewer's core fix: stop selecting sections by SCOPE_SECTION_TERMS in the standalone policy-variant pipeline. For each resolved document (preferred and siblings), return its description section chosen structurally (number 1.0 or 1, or the first numbered top-level section) plus the top search hits for that document that have non-empty text. Build the result through finalizeFrame, with the limit split per document rather than applied to the flattened list. Two additions: 1. Model the comparison case explicitly. When the query names two or more policies exactly, or the top two scores tie, set variantReasoning to null and also change VARIANT_RULES in packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts so "Lead with the preferred policy variant" and "only as comparison" are replaced by a compare-both instruction. Nulling variantReasoning alone leaves the "Lead with" line in the plan. 2. Scope the change to resolvePolicyVariantFrame. coverage.ts:169 calls buildPolicyVariantFrameForDocuments only for document choice and variantReasoning, so split out a small document-ranking helper for it and keep the setting-term reasoning (in-home versus congregate) there. Add one frame test with real 3L and 3L-1 section lists asserting both 1.0 sections are present with text and no zero-length section is returned, and one planner test asserting no "controls" line when two policies are named.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Factual claim: true of the code at HEAD. I read policy-variant.ts, the section collector in hydration.ts, pickDocumentSectionsByTitle, scorePhraseMatches, scoreVariantDocument, pickTopDocumentCandidates, isPolicyVariantQuery and the planner's VARIANT_RULES. - Section selection is a bare substring test over number plus title against six terms. "1.0 Description of the Procedure, Product, or Service" contains none of them, so it can never score above zero. "2.2.1 EPSDT Special Provision: Exception to Policy Limitations" matches through "policy". - The pipeline uses search hits only to find candidate documents, returns its own object without finalizeFrame, and never checks section text. - The four-section limit is applied after flattening all siblings, so the first sibling with four matches fills the supporting list. - Every named policy gets the same +2.5 and the reason "Exact policy-n…

## RETR-15 · Amendment frames list synthetic revision rows as authoritative and omit the named policy

Severity medium · effort S

### r2-critic-amendment-date-design-02 — Amendment-history frames for a named clinical policy list three section-less revision rows as the authoritative documents and omit the policy; each such frame makes 25 getAmendments calls

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/amendment.ts:102`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** On the non-latest amendment lane, `resolveAmendmentFrame` calls `getAmendments` for every document `lookupPolicy` returns. For a clinical policy with a revision chain that is the policy plus up to 24 synthetic, section-less rows that share its policy number. The rows fill all three `authoritativeDocuments` slots at 1.30, 1.25 and 1.20. The real policy appears only as their `baseDocument` at 0.85 or less, and the guard at lines 102-104 then denies it the 1.8 explicit-lookup score. Reproduced on current source against the legacy local graph for 8C and 3K-2: 25 `getAmendments` calls and 76 statements in the frame, 77 with the lookup. On that graph all 125 chained policies have at least 5 rows and 54 have 24 or more. Two things limit the impact. The rows carry the base policy's URL, and the frame's sections still come from search, so the citation link and section evidence are intact; only the document-level block and the call cost are wrong. Both saved held-out questions were answered correctly. Interaction with round-one fixes, established by tracing the code and not executed: - **Shape change alone** (`authoritative = base ?? queried`, from prior-findings-03 / neo4j-live-03): leaves three rows on top at 1.75, 1.70 and 1.65, with the policy at 1.30. It also lowers a named DHHS manual from 1.8 to 1.3, below its sibling lookup documents. - **Relabel alone** (rows to `:PolicyRevision`, from neo4j-live-11): removes the rows from lookup and restores the policy to 1.8 with a single `getAmendments` call. The claim about widening the heading filter was not verified.
- **Evidence:** amendment.ts:67 `const amendmentViews = await Promise.all(baseDocuments.map((document) => context.getAmendments(document.id)));` and :102-104 `const existingAuthoritativeIds = new Set(authoritativeCandidates.map((c) => c.document.id));` / `lookupDocuments.forEach((document, index) => {` / `if (existingAuthoritativeIds.has(document.id)) return;`. amendments.py:262 `policy_number=policy,`. Probe (packages/policy-corpus/dist built 2026-10-04, run read-only on the legacy graph, search stubbed) for 'What has been amended in Clinical Coverage Policy 8C since 2024?': `lookupPolicy rows: 25; real (non-derived): 1; getAmendments calls: 25; Cypher statements: 77`; authoritativeDocuments = `Policy 8C Revision — 2025-04-15 (Section 3.2.2)`, `Policy 8C Revision — 2025-01-01 (Section 6.1)`, `Policy 8C Revision — 2025-01-01 (Subsection 7.5)`; `queried policy's own candidate scores: 0.85 (Base document referenced by the amendment trail.)`. Same for 3K-2. Live confirmation in a saved held-out run (local, gitignored): packages/pipeline/data/eval-results/2026-07-10T00:37:05_answer.json, git_sha 210ced2, h-043 `"route_authoritative_document": {"id": "doc:change_notice:8C:2025-04-15", "policy_number": "8C", ...}` and h-044 `doc:change_notice:3K-2:2025-09-01`; its evidence text lists the same three rows under `Authoritative documents:`.
- **Impact:** All 125 clinical policies with a chain in the legacy graph have at least three rows, so every amendment-history question that names one gets a frame whose 'Authoritative documents' block, shown to the model and to the verifier, is three zero-section rows and no policy. 54 policies have 24 or more rows, so the frame costs 25 primitive calls and 77 Cypher statements against 5 for an unchained policy. The two saved questions were still answered correctly because the newest row was the answer; a question that also needs the current policy text gets no document-level pointer to it. Widening the heading filter, which round one recommends, extends this to nearly all 177 policies.
- **Fix:** Fold this into the neo4j-live-11 and prior-findings-03 work as an ordering constraint, not a separate project. 1. **Remove revision rows from `lookupPolicy`.** Either relabel them to `:PolicyRevision` (neo4j-live-11) or, as a one-line stopgap, add `AND coalesce(d.source_format,'') <> 'derived'` to the lookup query. Either one fixes both the three-row block and the 25-call fan-out for clinical policies. 2. **Reorder `amendment.ts` in the same PR as the `getAmendments` shape change.** This step is mandatory when `authoritativeDocument` becomes the base or queried document. - Push `lookupDocuments` first at 1.8 minus 0.05 per index. - Add amendment-view documents only if their id is not already present, so the guard runs in the opposite direction. - Carry the newest amendment as metadata (`latestAmendment`), not as a document candidate. 3. **Limit `getAmendments` calls.** Call it only for lookup documents whose status is not `amendment`. If every lookup document is an amendment, for example when the user names a change notice, call it for the first one so the base manual is still found. Cap the fan-out at about 3 documents. 4. **Tests.** - A policy with 30 derived rows yields `authoritativeDocuments[0]` equal to the policy, no derived row among the three, and exactly one `getAmendments` call. - A DHHS manual with four or more lookup variants keeps the named manual first after the shape change.
- **How it was verified:** **(1) Is the claim true? Yes, reproduced on current source.** I read `amendment.ts`, `primitives/amendments.ts`, `primitives/policy-lookup.ts`, `frame/context/routing.ts`, `hydration.ts`, `finalize.ts`, `document-selection.ts` and `amendments.py`. I then ran a throwaway probe with tsx against the TypeScript source at HEAD: real primitives, a READ-mode session on the local legacy graph, search stubbed to `[]`. The built dist would not load under plain Node 26 because of a JSON import attribute, so I used source instead. - **Lookup rows:** `lookupPolicy` has no derived-row filter, and the synthetic rows carry `policy_number=policy`. For 8C it returned 25 rows, 1 of them real, with the policy first. - **Score mechanism:** the policy's own `getAmendments` call yields the newest row at 1.30. Each row yields itself as `authoritativeDocument` at 1.30 minus 0.05 per index, and the policy as `ba…

### r2-critic-policy-number-bakeoff-04 — Amendment frames depend on synthetic revision rows coming back from lookupPolicy, and today they crowd out the named policy

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/amendment.ts:104`
- **Severity:** low (reviewer proposed medium) · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Two linked observations about resolveAmendmentFrame (amendment.ts:52-110). (a) Live, on the amendment-history route for a named clinical policy: lookupPolicy returns the policy plus up to 24 synthetic revision rows. Each synthetic row's getAmendments view returns the row itself as authoritativeDocument (1.3 - i*0.05) and the policy as baseDocument (0.9 - i*0.05). The policy is therefore already a candidate, the 1.8 explicit-lookup boost is skipped, and all three authoritative slots go to synthetic rows (saved gate q-044). The saved answer was still correct because the agent calls getAmendments itself. This half goes away once synthetic rows are excluded from lookupPolicy. (b) Latent, on the latest-revision-only route: line 78 discards the getAmendments results, so the newest clinical revision row reaches the frame only as a lookup row (saved gate q-043). Either round-one clean-up (the WHERE exclusion in corpus-retrieval-04, the relabel in neo4j-live-11) removes it, and corpus-retrieval-04's "revisions still arrive through getAmendments" is wrong for this route. The policy document's own effective date (2025-04-15 for 8C) would remain in the frame. The loss is the revision row's section detail and its change_notice label, not all recency signal. Three of the 155 benchmark questions are affected. The reviewer's replay outputs and query counts were not independently reproduced.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/amendment.ts:78 `if (!latestRevisionOnly && view.authoritativeDocument) {`; :102-104 `const existingAuthoritativeIds = new Set(authoritativeCandidates.map((c) => c.document.id));` / `lookupDocuments.forEach((document, index) => {` / `if (existingAuthoritativeIds.has(document.id)) return;`; :108 `score: 1.8 - index * 0.05,`. Saved gate final-gate-full.json q-044 "Has the CAP/DA waiver policy been amended since 2024?": Authoritative documents = doc:change_notice:3K-2:2025-09-01, doc:change_notice:3K-2:2025-09-01:12, doc:change_notice:3K-2:2025-09-01:20; no clinical_policy document. Same in the HEAD replay for q-044, h-044, h-043, j-043. Saved gate q-043 (route latest_revision_marker): authoritative = 8C, doc:change_notice:8C:2025-04-15 with reason "Explicit policy lookup (takes precedence over unrelated revision-header hits).", MA-3420. Replay with only the lookup changed to exact key without revision rows: authoritative = [8C, MA-3420], latest marker "Current Change Notice: 06-24" from MA-3420. With the guard in the combined patch: [8C, 8C revision 2025-04-15, MA-3420] again, in 32 queries instead of 104 (HEAD calls getAmendments for all 25 lookup rows and discards the results on this route).
- **Impact:** Today the frame for "has policy X been amended" names three revision rows and not the policy. After either clean-up, "when was the most recent change notice for Policy 8C" would get a frame whose only revision signal is another manual's change-notice marker. Three of the 155 benchmark questions (q-043 and its two paraphrases) take one of these routes with a named clinical policy.
- **Fix:** Treat this as an implementation note on corpus-retrieval-04 / neo4j-live-11, not a separate work item. 1. When synthetic rows are removed from lookupPolicy, add the guard in amendment.ts in the same change. On the latest-revision-only route, for each lookup document's view, push view.authoritativeDocument at 1.75 - index*0.05 when view.amendments.length > 0 and its documentType is 'change_notice' (a clinical revision row). Leave DHHS notices out: they are dhhs_policy documents with approximate YYYY-01-01 dates. 2. No separate fix is needed for the amendment-history route. With only the policy in the lookup, it takes the 1.8 boost and the newest revision follows at 1.3. 3. Correct the sentence in corpus-retrieval-04's fix that says revisions still arrive through getAmendments. That is true only when latest_revision_only is false. 4. Add one frame unit test with a stubbed context for a named clinical policy on both routes, asserting the policy is the first authoritative document and the newest revision row is present. 5. Stop calling getAmendments for every lookup row on the latest-revision-only route unless the guard uses the result. 6. Confirm q-043 and its two paraphrases in the next gate run.
- **How it was verified:** Not refuted, but I would lower it to low and fold it into corpus-retrieval-04 / neo4j-live-11 as an implementation caveat. (1) Is the claim true? Yes for the mechanism and the saved data; the reviewer's replays were not re-run. - I opened amendment.ts, primitives/amendments.ts, primitives/policy-lookup.ts, frame/context/routing.ts, scoring-constants.ts and the saved gate final-gate-full.json. - Live part, by code reading: lookupPolicy returns the policy plus up to 24 synthetic revision rows (STARTS WITH, limit 25), and all of them become baseDocuments. For the policy's view, the newest synthetic row is pushed at 1.3. Each synthetic row has no amendments of its own, so getAmendments returns the row itself as authoritativeDocument (1.3 - i*0.05) and the policy as baseDocument (0.9 - i*0.05). The policy id is then already in existingAuthoritativeIds, so the 1.8 boost at line 104 is skipped…

## RETR-16 · The beneficiary_guidance document family is unreachable by default search and every answer frame

Severity medium · effort S

### corpus-retrieval-01 — The beneficiary_guidance document family is unreachable by default retrieval and by every answer frame

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/scoring-constants.ts:20`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** beneficiary_guidance is a manifest source family and is described to the model in the agent prompt, but it is in none of BASE_TYPE_GROUPS, OPERATIONAL_TYPE_GROUPS or federalImplementationDocumentTypes(), and the literal does not occur in packages/policy-corpus/src. Every primary retrieval backend filters on document_type IN $docTypes, so a default search and every answer-frame search cannot directly return a section from that family. The only routes are an explicit documentTypes:['beneficiary_guidance'] argument, the type-unfiltered concept backend, the title-based document-expansion stage (which only expands documents already hit), and id-based primitives. The defect is latent today: the family is absent from the local graph and from the configured production release legacy-20260725-ae8595c (all nine q-051 to q-055 evidence sections are listed as missing), so those questions currently fail because the data is not loaded. The filter exclusion takes effect once a corpus containing the family is promoted; a 2026-10-03 qualification-preparation artifact shows it at loaded 2 / retrieval_ready 2.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/scoring-constants.ts:20-31 lists the groups; :25 `['bulletin', 'state_plan_amendment', 'waiver', 'provider_manual'],` and :27-31 `export const OPERATIONAL_TYPE_GROUPS = [` `['fee_schedule'],` `['billing_guidance'],` `['managed_care_guidance', 'managed_care_contract'],`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/section-vector.ts:17 `WHERE d.document_type IN $docTypes` (same filter at chunk-vector.ts:17, keyword.ts:25 and :89, fulltext.ts:24, policy-scoped.ts:28 and :56). corpus-manifest.json:174 `"source_type": "beneficiary_guidance",`. packages/agent/src/prompt.ts:87 `- **beneficiary_guidance** — Dated official NC Medicaid public guidance.`
- **Impact:** Golden questions q-051 to q-055 (2027 work and community-engagement requirements, refugee eligibility on 2026-10-01, termination notices) have evidence only in doc:beneficiary_guidance:* documents. For q-055, the positive control "At a general level, what new NC Medicaid work and community engagement requirements start in 2027?", the auto-prefetched answer frame and a default search can only return clinical, DHHS, federal or bulletin sections, so the agent answers from the wrong family or the verifier withholds. The type groups were last edited on 2026-06-12 (37ddd9a); the family was added on 2026-07-30 (9582c6b, PR #110), which touched policy-corpus only for date plumbing. The latest saved release gate (2026-09-22, 50 questions) does not contain q-051 to q-055 and no eval artifact in the repo shows them retrieved. Caveat: the local crawler registry copy (1 Oct) has no rows of this type, so I could not confirm the family is loaded in any graph; the exclusion applies as soon as it is.
- **Fix:** Keep the fix small and order it so it can actually be validated. 1. Add `['beneficiary_guidance']` as its own entry in BASE_TYPE_GROUPS (packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/scoring-constants.ts). Do not fold it into the bulletin group. Leave federalImplementationDocumentTypes() unchanged unless you decide federal-authority questions should surface beneficiary guidance. 2. Do not derive the groups from corpus-manifest.json. The grouping is a tuning decision the manifest does not encode, and policy-corpus and the agent image do not have the manifest at runtime. Instead add one policy-corpus unit test that reads ../../corpus-manifest.json and asserts every source_type under sources and federal_sources appears in BASE_TYPE_GROUPS or OPERATIONAL_TYPE_GROUPS, or in a short named exclusion list in the test. A newly added family then fails CI until someone places it deliberately. 3. Add one profile-level test asserting that a default (non-federal, no documentTypes) profile's typeGroups includes beneficiary_guidance. This is cheaper than a fixture retrieval test and guards the same regression. 4. Add beneficiary_guidance to the searchSections description in packages/agent/src/tools/search.ts:19. 5. Defer the q-051 to q-055 retrieval eval until the family is actually loaded into the graph and the golden IDs are recomputed with pipeline.identity.build_document_id (gotchas.md:127). The local graph has zero beneficiary_guidance documents and the nine expected identities are known legacy misses, so the eval cannot pass today whatever the code does. Fold this into the #188 qualification work. When it runs, also check that the supporting-tier penalty does not bury the guidance on q-055, and that adding a sixth group does not regress the existing 50 questions.
- **How it was verified:** The code claim is true as written, but it is latent today: neither the local graph nor the production release recorded in the repo contains any beneficiary_guidance documents. What I checked: - **Type groups.** `scoring-constants.ts:20-31` matches the quoted evidence verbatim. `beneficiary_guidance` is in neither `BASE_TYPE_GROUPS` nor `OPERATIONAL_TYPE_GROUPS`, and `federalImplementationDocumentTypes()` does not return it. - **Default routing.** `buildRetrievalProfileSignals` uses `input.documentTypes` when given, otherwise `defaultTypeGroupsForQuery`, which can only return those three sets. No other source of type groups exists. - **Backends.** Section-vector, chunk-vector, fulltext, both keyword queries, both policy-scoped queries and amendment-context all filter on `document_type IN $docTypes`. Only the concept backend is unfiltered without explicit types, as the finding says. - **L…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted. The defect is real, undocumented and untracked, but it is latent: it bites on the next planned graph activation, not in today's deployment. I keep it at high on the evaluation-gate prong. What I opened: scoring-constants.ts, retrieval-planning.ts, retrieval-signals.ts, the concept backend, corpus-manifest.json, gotchas.md, verified-release-2026-09-22.md, the 2026-09-22 production-corpus-preflight, the October identity preflights and legacy-misses, golden q-051/q-055, commit 9582c6b, issues #187 and #188, and a read-only type count on the local Neo4j. Refutation angles, each disposed of: - **Documented trade-off?** No. gotchas.md:127 covers a different defect on the same five go…

## RETR-17 · Keyword backends scan every section body per search and return whole bodies

Severity medium · effort M

### corpus-retrieval-03 — Every search scans, ships and re-lowercases whole section bodies, and repeats the same ANN scan once per type group

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/keyword.ts:37`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** perf-cost · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim stands as written. Corrections apply to the impact figures only: - **Graph size:** the loaded local graph holds uncompacted fee_schedule text: 943 documents, 3,322 sections, 2,677 MB, largest section 38.5 MB. The "roughly 260 MB after compaction" figure is not reflected there, and the production graph size is unverified. - **Other families:** these total about 104 MB in the graph, not about 56 MB. - **k=200:** this is the overscan scanK; the per-group LIMIT is perGroupK (40 at the default topK of 10). - **Timing:** a one-term CONTAINS over 35.7 MB of fee_schedule sections took about 1.3 s net on the local graph. Extrapolated, the full group needs on the order of 100 s per term, so the fee_schedule section_keyword query would exceed the 30 s timeout and be silently dropped by the empty catch at keyword.ts:70.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/keyword.ts:37 `WHEN toLower(coalesce(s.content_text, '')) CONTAINS term THEN 1`; :58 `s.content_text AS content,`; :60 `ORDER BY keywordScore DESC, s.content_length DESC`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/chunk-vector.ts:13-15 `CALL db.index.vector.queryNodes('chunk_embeddings', toInteger($scanK), $embedding)` / `YIELD node AS c, score` / `MATCH (s:Section)-[:HAS_CHUNK]->(c)`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/document-selection.ts:53 `normalizeSearchText(hit.content).includes(term)`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/section-vector.ts:14 `CALL db.index.vector.queryNodes('section_embeddings', toInteger($scanK), $embedding)` inside `for (const docTypes of profile.typeGroups) {` (:8).
- **Impact:** Local parse sidecars show fee_schedule at 943 documents and 2,613 MB of text (largest 38.6 MB in two sections), against about 56 MB for all other families together. That is the pre-compaction figure recorded in gotchas.md; after the 90% compaction it is still roughly 260 MB of full-body CONTAINS per term per search. The fee_schedule group is added for any operational query, and that flag fires on substrings: "What information must be documented for personal care services?" and "Who performs the independent assessment for PCS?" both route to operational_hybrid with 8 groups because 'form' sits inside information/performs. Neo4j must then lower-case and scan that text up to 14 times inside a 30 s statement timeout whose failure is swallowed, and multi-megabyte hits that do return are shipped whole over Bolt and re-lowercased on the event loop: measured 0.63 s for 600 hits of 50 KB and 4.3 s synchronous for 200 MB of hit content. A fake-session probe confirmed five section-vector calls with identical scanK=200 and embedding. Latency against the real graph was not measured.
- **Fix:** Do this as one small change set, then qualify it with the repeated-run retrieval eval, since it changes ranking inputs for long sections. 1. **Bound returned content.** In every backend's RETURN, replace `s.content_text AS content` with `left(s.content_text, $maxHitChars) AS content`, using one shared constant of about 20,000 (about 99% of non-fee sections are shorter). Do not add a hydration step, and do not substitute chunk text. 2. **Stop the full-body scan.** In section_keyword, delete the `toLower(coalesce(s.content_text,'')) CONTAINS term` arm and keep the title, document-title and policy-number arms. Replace the `s.content_length DESC` tiebreak with `s.id ASC`. If the eval shows no loss, consider deleting section_keyword outright. Do not rewrite it onto Lucene. 3. **Aggregate chunk hits.** In chunk_vector, add `WITH s, d, max(score) AS score` before the RETURN. 4. **One ANN scan per index per request.** Run it once with `WHERE d.document_type IN $unionOfGroupTypes`, return documentType and truncated content, and take the top perGroupK per group client-side. Let policy_scoped reuse the section scan. 5. **Drop the byte budget.** Instead, log in the currently empty `catch {}` blocks in the keyword, chunk-vector, fulltext and policy-scoped backends, so statement timeouts are visible. 6. **Lower-case cache is optional.** Bounded content already cuts the cost; only add it if profiling still shows it. 7. **Later:** in-index filtering needs document_type denormalised onto Section and Chunk in the pipeline, the vector indexes recreated `WITH [s.document_type]`, Cypher 25 `SEARCH`, and Neo4j 2026.06 or later for `IN`. Until then, update the outdated "cannot pre-filter" comment in scoring-constants.ts. Separately, the 38 MB fee_schedule sections are the root cause and are worth capping at the pipeline.
- **How it was verified:** I tried to refute the finding and could not: every quoted line is present verbatim and no guard elsewhere bounds the text. What I opened: all of keyword.ts, chunk-vector.ts, section-vector.ts, fulltext.ts, collector.ts, hybrid.ts, document-selection.ts, the rerank feature derivation, scoring-constants.ts, base.ts, query-intent.ts, retrieval-signals.ts, record-mapping.ts, and the agent's neo4j.ts, policy-corpus.ts and tools/search.ts. I also ran four bounded read-only queries against the local Neo4j. Structural claims, each confirmed: - **Unindexed scan:** section_keyword matches every section of the group's document types with no other predicate, then evaluates `toLower(content_text) CONTAINS term` inside a `reduce` over `$terms`. Terms are capped at 14. The only index on `content_text` is the Lucene full-text index, which CONTAINS cannot use. - **Tie-break toward longest sections:** th…
- **Why this severity:** I tried to dismiss this as a known trade-off, an already-tracked item, or a contrived scenario. None of those hold, and a read-only measurement on the local graph supports the claim. **Not documented or tracked.** gotchas.md and docs/operations/fee-schedule-compaction.md discuss fee-schedule size only as an embedding-cost and parse problem. Nothing mentions query-time cost, the CONTAINS scans, or returning whole bodies. None of the open issues (#144-#188) cover retrieval latency. The code comments explain only why the vector backends overscan per group ("see section-vector for why"), not why the scan is repeated or why full content is returned. **Compaction does not mitigate it.** The 2026-…

### perf-cost-reliability-05 — Keyword backends scan every section body with toLower CONTAINS on each search and return full bodies for every candidate

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/keyword.ts:24`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** perf-cost · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** section_keyword runs one statement per document-type group, sequentially, that matches every section in the group and, as written, evaluates toLower(content_text) CONTAINS term inside a per-term reduce (up to 14 terms). A Lucene full-text index over Section title and content_text exists and is queried by the fulltext backend in the same search. Measured on the local graph, the scan costs about 0.5-0.8 s per group, so 2.5-4 s for the five base groups. Every backend returns s.content_text in full for up to perGroupK rows per group (25-100; 64-96 for coverage-frame searches, 32-48 for general, prior-auth and amendment frames). The full bodies are not discarded: the in-process reranker and the frame selection heuristics read hit.content in full for every candidate, and only the external SearchHit mapping truncates to 300 characters. The 512 MB Neo4j heap is the owner's live Railway setting, not the repo's (neo4j.env.example says 2g max). Operational-routing queries add the fee_schedule group, whose sections total about 2.68 billion characters locally.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/keyword.ts:20 `for (const docTypes of profile.typeGroups) {` | :24 `MATCH (d:Document)-[:HAS_SECTION]->(s:Section)` | :37 `WHEN toLower(coalesce(s.content_text, '')) CONTAINS term THEN 1` | :58 `s.content_text AS content,` | packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/scoring-constants.ts:7 `export const SEARCH_SNIPPET_CHARS = 300;` | docs/validation/cost-audit-sample-2026-10-03/graph-preservation.json `"sections": 67457` | budget-ledger.json: consecutive Voyage calls inside one frame are 7.5, 3.8, 1.8, 3.7, 3.7, 1.6 and 1.7 s apart; frame tool total 3.9-10.8 s (mean 7.1 s).
- **Impact:** resolveAnswerFrame is the largest single wait in the measured sample, longer than the verifier (mean 5.0 s) and comparable to final generation (mean 7.2 s), and it runs on every chat. Each hybrid search is roughly 30-36 Cypher statements over 7-8 concurrent sessions; a frame runs 2-3 searches, and three parallel research workers multiply that against a Neo4j capped at 512 MB heap with a default 100-connection pool and no concurrency limit. The gotchas file already records Neo4j memory exhaustion from full-body reads. The cause of the latency is inferred from query shape, graph size and wall-clock gaps; I could not profile Neo4j, and the sample's network path to the graph is unknown.
- **Fix:** Do these in order, and stop when the measured latency is acceptable. 1. Run PROFILE and time section_keyword and document_keyword per type group against the production graph first. Locally the base groups are cheap and fee_schedule alone takes 43.8 s. 2. Bound the body scan by size: apply the `s.content_text CONTAINS` branch only when `s.content_length <= cap` (about 200k characters; 386 of 59,347 sections exceed it, 372 of them fee_schedule), keeping title, document-title and policy-number matching for all sections. Apply the same guard in document_keyword and check document-expansion.ts for the same pattern. 3. Log the swallowed error in the keyword backends' catch blocks, so a timeout is visible rather than silently returning no hits. 4. Compute toLower once per row in a WITH clause. Results are identical; the gain is small. 5. Drop or invert the `s.content_length DESC` tie-break, which pulls the largest bodies into the top K, and run the retrieval eval to confirm ranking holds. 6. Do not truncate with left() unless the reranker's content features (rerank.ts:217-225) are moved into Cypher or the change is qualified with a full retrieval eval; the reranker reads the whole body. 7. Do not swap CONTAINS for the fulltext index as a drop-in. If the scan is still too slow after step 2, use the index only as a candidate prefilter, keep the existing additive scoring, and requalify. 8. Skip the semaphore and pool-size change until pool exhaustion or Neo4j memory pressure is actually observed under concurrent research workers.
- **How it was verified:** The core claim is true; one clause is wrong and two details need correcting. What I opened: keyword.ts in full, collector.ts, hybrid.ts, fulltext.ts, backend-registry.ts, retrieval-signals.ts, retrieval-planning.ts, search.ts, scoring-constants.ts, record-mapping.ts, the agent's neo4j.ts, the pipeline's graph/schema.py, the sample ledger and graph-preservation.json. I also ran the section_keyword statement read-only against the local Neo4j, returning ids only. Confirmed: - **Sequential scan per type group.** section_keyword loops over profile.typeGroups with an awaited session.run each time. The statement matches every Document-HAS_SECTION-Section in the group and evaluates toLower(content_text) CONTAINS term inside a reduce over $terms, so no index can serve it. - **Up to 14 terms.** MAX_QUERY_TERMS is 14 and extractSearchTerms slices to it. - **A Lucene index on the same text exists a…
- **Why this severity:** The finding survives, but its stated impact needs correcting: the scan is not the main cause of frame latency on ordinary queries, and the real exposure is the fee-schedule group on billing and reimbursement questions. What I opened: keyword.ts, hybrid.ts, retrieval-signals.ts, retrieval-planning.ts, scoring-constants.ts, query-intent.ts, agent neo4j.ts, gotchas.md, the cost-audit README, docs/decisions, and the open issue list. I also ran bounded read-only timings against the local Neo4j. Is it already accepted, tracked or decided? No. - docs/decisions holds only 0001-repository-knowledge.md; nothing there or near the code records the CONTAINS scan as a deliberate trade-off. The only comme…

## RETR-18 · Four lookup tools return unbounded results, up to about 260,000 tokens

Severity medium · effort S

### neo4j-live-05 — Unbounded primitives return tool outputs of up to about 260k tokens; the sibling query is quadratic

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/concepts.ts:47`
- **Severity:** high · **Kind:** perf-cost · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The code and graph figures stand as reported. Three corrections: 1. "No layer above bounds tool results" should read: nothing truncates or pages tool results, and the deep-research planner and worker agents have no token limiter at all. The main chat agent does have `TokenLimiterProcessor({ limit: 128_000 })` (packages/agent/src/index.ts:148 and 169), which runs at every step. It drops whole messages that do not fit rather than truncating them. Results up to roughly 80k tokens (getSectionContext, lookupPolicy, getDocument) therefore reach the model in full. A 173k–260k-token findConceptSections result on the chat agent is silently discarded with its evidence, or the processor throws a TripWire if nothing fits, rather than overflowing the context. 2. The GOVERNS / REFERENCES_CONCEPT advertisement is at packages/agent/src/tools/concepts.ts:17, not lines 8-9. 3. getDocument's worst case is larger than the cited waiver: one state_plan_amendment document has 2,164 non-empty sections. The PROFILE db-hit and timing figures and the lookupPolicy('8A') size were not independently re-run.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/concepts.ts:47-49: "MATCH (s:Section)-[r]->(c:PolicyConcept) WHERE ${CONCEPT_NAME_MATCH} OPTIONAL MATCH (d:Document)-[:HAS_SECTION]->(s)" (RETURN ends at line 57 with no LIMIT) | packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/sections.ts:199-200: "OPTIONAL MATCH path = (head:Section)-[:NEXT_SIBLING*0..]->(sibling) WHERE head IN family" | packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/policy-lookup.ts:70: "collect({id: s.id, number: s.section_number, title: s.title}) AS sections," | packages/agent/src/tools/concepts.ts:8-9: "'Find all policy sections that are associated with a named policy concept (e.g. \"EPSDT\", ' + '\"Prior Authorization\", \"Medically Necessary\"). Optionally filter by relationship type. ' +"
- **Impact:** Real primitives on the local graph, output measured as JSON characters (about 4 per token). findConceptSections('Prior Authorization'), one of the tool's own examples: 2,573 rows, 693,868 characters (about 173k tokens); 'CMS': 3,773 rows, 1,038,976 characters (about 260k tokens); EPSDT has 1,917 linked sections. getSectionContext for a child of a 2,364-child parent: 2,363 siblings, 316,342 characters (about 79k tokens), 27,763,544 db hits, 4,459 ms; operator rows grow as n squared over two (286,903 at 757 siblings, 2,795,430 at 2,364). Every CFR section returns its whole part as siblings because federal documents are flat (Part 405: 271 siblings, about 9.5k tokens). lookupPolicy('1915(c)'): 4 documents with 3,018 section entries, 313,299 characters (about 78k tokens); lookupPolicy('8A'): 110,037 characters. getDocument on a waiver: 768 sections, 88,463 characters. A single such call can exhaust the model context or dominate request cost. Separately, relationship types GOVERNS and REFERENCES_CONCEPT are accepted (types.ts:245-246) and advertised by the tool but never written (corpus_writer.py:240), so they return an empty list that looks like a real negative.
- **Fix:** Bound at the right layer and skip the reload. 1. getDocument and lookupPolicy: leave the primitives returning full section lists, because answer-frame code depends on them (frame/service.ts:37-41, federal-authority.ts:464, hydration.ts, routing.ts, amendment.ts). Cap in the agent tool execute (tools/read.ts, tools/lookup.ts): return the first N sections (about 100 for getDocument; for lookupPolicy inline sections for the top-ranked document only, or about 40 per document) plus sectionCount and sectionsTruncated. Give getDocument an optional offset or title filter so the model can page. Update extractGetDocument and extractLookupPolicy in processors/verifier/evidence.ts to print "Sections (showing N of TOTAL)" so the verifier never treats a capped list as complete. 2. findConceptSections (no internal callers, so bound it in the primitive): add ORDER BY (title mentions the concept first, then document, then section number) and LIMIT about 25-50, and return total and truncated from a count in the same query. A per-document cap is optional; do not collapse to strictly one row per document unless the tool is renamed, since it is a section-level tool. 3. getSectionContext: do not add a load-time ordinal. Replace the *0.. expansion with a bounded chain walk from the target in both directions, for example (s)-[:NEXT_SIBLING*1..10]->(n) UNION (n)-[:NEXT_SIBLING*1..10]->(s), filtered to the same parent, ordered by signed offset, plus siblingCount from COUNT { (parent)-[:PARENT_OF]->() } - 1. NEXT_SIBLING is a strict chain on the live graph (max degree 1 each way; 34 of 62,258 edges cross families), and this ran in 38 ms on the 2,364-child family with no pipeline change or corpus reload. Handle the parent-less root family separately (filter and count on the document's root sections); that case was not profiled. Cap children the same way. 4. Remove GOVERNS and REFERENCES_CONCEPT from ALLOWED_CONCEPT_RELATIONSHIPS (types.ts:245-246) and from the tool description (agent/src/tools/concepts.ts:17); make relationshipType a z.enum of COVERS, DEFINES, RESTRICTS so an invalid value is a schema error instead of an empty list. 5. Add one unit test per tool asserting the cap and the truncated flag. A toModelOutput hook is not a substitute, because the raw output still reaches stored messages and verifier evidence.
- **How it was verified:** The claim is true in substance; one sentence needs correcting. "No layer above bounds tool results" is too strong for the main chat agent, which has a 128k-token limiter that drops oversize messages whole. **What I opened:** the four primitives, their agent tool wrappers, the tool registrations for the chat agent and the deep-research agents, the installed Mastra TokenLimiterProcessor, the Python concept-link writer, and the local graph (read-only counts). **Code claims, all confirmed:** - `findConceptSections` has no LIMIT in either query branch, and the tool wrapper returns the array unmodified. - `getSectionContext` returns every sibling with no LIMIT. Its `NEXT_SIBLING*0..` expansion runs once per sibling after an UNWIND over the family, with list-membership tests, so it is quadratic by inspection. - `lookupPolicy` caps documents at 25 but collects every section of each with no per-…
- **Why this severity:** The finding stands as a real problem for this project, but one sub-claim is wrong for the chat path and should be corrected. What I opened: the four cited primitives, the agent tool wrappers, the agent and deep-research wiring, the installed Mastra TokenLimiterProcessor source, the local graph (read-only, bounded), gotchas/decisions/operations docs, and the open issue list. Not an accepted or tracked trade-off. No open issue covers it (#144-#188 are toolchain, corpus sources and qualification). Nothing in docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/decisions, docs/operations or RUNBOOK.md mentions tool-result size or the sibling query. The only prior mention of findConceptSections in reviews is the J…

## RETR-19 · Hits carry no document id, so expansion and boosts key on non-unique titles

Severity medium · effort M

### corpus-retrieval-08 — Score fusion compares incomparable scales and rewards hit count, and hits carry no document identity

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/collector.ts:28`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Candidates from six stage-1 backends (section vector, chunk vector, concept, section keyword, document keyword, policy-scoped, plus amendment context on change-notice queries) and the expansion stages are merged by keeping the maximum raw score per section. Fulltext is the exception: it adds 0.05 to sections already found and inserts new ones at a mid-range score. The scales are unrelated: raw vector similarity, a constant 0.7 for concept hits, and synthetic lexical scores that grow linearly with the number of matched query terms (document expansion: 0.86 for content-only matches on 14 terms, 1.70 maximum). A document-level boost of up to +0.16 (0.01 per matched term plus 0.02 per extra hit) is added to every section of a document; it is recomputed after title-based expansion has added more hits. CorpusRetrievalHit has no documentId, url or status, so boosts group by type, policy number and title, and expansion selects and matches on title alone. Lexical matching is substring-based (CONTAINS) on a term list filtered by a roughly 35-word stop list that still passes function words such as "that", "this", "from" and "who", and any token containing a digit. Titles are not identities. In the local Neo4j graph 748 of 7,589 documents share a title and 737 share the full type/policy-number/title grouping key; among dhhs_policy documents 88 of 1,237 share a title, and MA-2240 TRANSFER OF ASSETS exists twice, both current. In the current crawler registry 184 of 1,322 non-removed dhhs_policy rows share a title (not 143 of 1,131), and MA-2240 TRANSFER OF ASSETS appears four times. The parity probe in impact (b) (0.940 each) holds when manual A's single hit matches exactly four query terms and other rerank scorers are ignored. Frame hydration calls getSection for the full section view, not only to obtain a documentId. The 25-of-50 concentration figure for the 2026-06-14 run reproduces exactly.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/collector.ts:27-29 `const existing = sectionMap.get(hit.sectionId);` / `if (!existing || hit.score > existing.score) {` / `sectionMap.set(hit.sectionId, hit);`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/document-selection.ts:64 `entry.terms.size * DOCUMENT_COVERAGE_TERM_BOOST + Math.max(0, entry.hitCount - 1) * DOCUMENT_COVERAGE_HIT_BOOST,`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/document-expansion.ts:31 `WHERE d.title IN $titles`; :58 `(${DOCUMENT_EXPANSION_BASE_SCORE} + sectionScore * ${DOCUMENT_EXPANSION_SECTION_WEIGHT}) AS score`. packages/policy-corpus/tests/search.test.ts:197-199 `// Exact ranked order. The two DHHS sections outrank sec:clinical despite` / `// its higher raw merge-max score (0.96 via the chunk backend) because the` / `// DHHS Policy document accumulates a document-coverage boost from three`
- **Impact:** (a) For a 14-term question, a section that merely contains every term in its body scores 0.86 through document expansion (formula maximum 1.70); for a 3-term question the same channel tops out at 0.82. Whether lexical or vector evidence wins therefore depends on the length of the question. (b) Probe: one section at 0.90 in manual A against ten generic sections at 0.78 in manual B. B receives +0.16 on every section and all ten reach parity with A's best hit (0.940 each), so a 0.12 similarity gap is erased by count alone and rank falls to stable-sort order. As historical corroboration, in the saved 2026-06-14 retrieval run at least 8 of the top 10 came from one document for 25 of 50 questions.
