(c) Titles are not identities: in the local registry 143 of 1,131 DHHS documents share a title with another (MA-2240 TRANSFER OF ASSETS appears four times; ABD and F&C change notices are twins), so coverage boosts and title expansion mix distinct documents, and frames need extra getSection calls just to learn a documentId. Neo4j's manual says of this situation: "rank each source independently rather than comparing raw score values directly."
- **Fix:** Do this in stages, and do not start with a rank-fusion rewrite. 1. **Identity (do now).** Add `d.id AS documentId` to `retrievalDocumentMetadataReturn` and to `CorpusRetrievalHit`. `status` is optional. Then key on it: - `documentKeyForHit` returns the id; - `selectDocumentTitlesForExpansion` returns ids, and the expansion queries use `WHERE d.id IN $ids`; - `selectPreferredDocumentTitlesByFocus` groups by id; - drop the extra `getSection` lookups the frames make only to learn a document id. 2. **Bound the lexical channels in place, keeping the 0-1 scale.** In document-expansion, section/document keyword, policy-scoped and amendment-context, divide the term score by its maximum (`size($terms) * per-term max`). The score becomes `BASE + WEIGHT * fraction`, with a fixed ceiling below strong vector hits that no longer depends on question length. 3. **Stop the feedback loop.** Compute `computeDocumentCoverageBoosts` once, from the pre-expansion map, and reuse it in `finalizeHybridHits` so expansion hits cannot raise their own document's boost. Then drop the `hitCount` term (keep distinct-term coverage only) or cap it at one or two extra hits. Rewrite the assertion at tests/search.test.ts:197 deliberately to state the new intent. 4. **Diversity only if measured.** If one document still dominates after step 3, add a per-document cap in the `finalizeHybridHits` top-k slice (a few lines), not MMR. 5. **Simplify lexical retrieval.** Replace the `toLower(...) CONTAINS term` scan backends with the existing `section_fulltext` index, converting its unbounded BM25 score by rank as `mergeFulltextHits` already does. Extend `SEARCH_STOPWORDS` and stop admitting bare single digits. 6. **Gate every step.** Run the chunked retrieval eval on golden plus `heldout-paraphrase.json`, repeated; it needs Voyage query embeddings. Per gotcha 95, also trace whether the changed order reaches frame evidence selection. Consider full weighted reciprocal rank fusion only if steps 1-5 leave a measured recall gap. If so, rescale the fused score back to 0-1, because rerank.ts, the focus-ranking windows and the frame offsets all assume that scale.

By type: change notices 223 titles / 517 documents, bulletins 19 / 90, DHHS policies 44 / 88, fee schedules 14 / 28, state plan amendments 3 / 10. "here" expands into 8 unrelated bulletins (529 sections); "Bulletin Digest – January" (en dash) expands into the 2018 to 2022 digests (191 sections); "CHANGE NOTICE FOR MANUAL NO. 01-19, Enumeration" matches both the ABD and FC manual notices. The db-hit figures and scan operator from the reviewer's PROFILE were not re-verified, though the absence of a title index rules out an index seek.

After step 3 this is only a citation-display issue, and it needs a recrawl and reload.

## RETR-20 · Vector search per group draws from one global top-200 pool, starving federal groups

Severity medium · effort M

### neo4j-live-07 — Per-group vector coverage is capped by a global top-200 pool, so federal groups get almost no vector hits; the 'cannot pre-filter' premise is outdated for the pinned server

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/scoring-constants.ts:10`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim stands, with three precisions. (1) The same global top-200 cap applies to `chunk-vector.ts` (the `chunk_embeddings` index) as well as `section-vector.ts` and `policy-scoped.ts`. (2) The recall figures come from stored section embeddings used as query vectors, an in-distribution proxy that probably overstates same-type clustering for real user questions; the structural cap is certain, the effect size on real queries is an estimate. (3) The 01N01 deprecation notice for `db.index.vector.queryNodes` was not independently reproduced; the availability of `SEARCH` with in-index `WHERE` on 2026.05 (equality now, `IN` from 2026.06) was confirmed on the live server and in the Neo4j manual.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/scoring-constants.ts:10-15: "// Neo4j's vector index cannot pre-filter: queryNodes(k) returns the GLOBAL // top-k, and a WHERE afterwards only filters that same pool. Per-type-group // (or per-policy) coverage requires overscanning the index and filtering the // deeper pool down to the group's top-k. export const VECTOR_OVERSCAN_FACTOR = 8; export const VECTOR_OVERSCAN_MAX = 200;" | packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/section-vector.ts:14-17: "CALL db.index.vector.queryNodes('section_embeddings', toInteger($scanK), $embedding) YIELD node AS s, score MATCH (d:Document)-[:HAS_SECTION]->(s) WHERE d.document_type IN $docTypes"
- **Impact:** Using 20 stored section embeddings as query vectors and comparing with the exact per-group top 40 by brute-force cosine: clinical-policy-like queries get 0.0 of 40 federal_statute hits (5 of 5 queries with none) and 0.6 federal_regulation hits (recall 0.02); DHHS-like queries get 0.8 regulation and 0.2 statute hits; bulletin-like queries get zero clinical, regulation and statute hits; even regulation-like queries get 1.8 statute hits (recall 0.04). The four real pipeline runs agree (baseline question: clinical 40, DHHS 24, regulation 1, statute 1). Federal authority therefore reaches the candidate set almost only through keyword scans, which is consistent with the recorded EPSDT miss of SSA 1905(r) that was patched in routing. policy_scoped has the same limit: a named policy's sections surface only if they are already in the global top 200. The live server confirms the alternative: EXPLAIN of the current statement returns 01N01 (queryNodes deprecated, replaced by SEARCH); a SEARCH statement plans; a filtered SEARCH fails only with 22ND3 because the property is not an additional index property; IN inside the filter needs 2026.06.
- **Fix:** Do it in two phases, and promote the "interim step" to the primary fix. **Phase 1: code only, no schema change, no reload** 1. In section-vector.ts and chunk-vector.ts, score any type group whose vector count is under a measured threshold with an exact scan instead of the index. Start the threshold at about 4k vectors; that covers federal_statute (233), federal_regulation (3,106), provider_manual, billing_guidance and fee_schedule at 50 ms or less each. The query shape is: `MATCH (d:Document)-[:HAS_SECTION]->(s:Section) WHERE d.document_type IN $docTypes AND s.embedding IS NOT NULL WITH d, s, vector.similarity.cosine(s.embedding, $embedding) AS score ORDER BY score DESC LIMIT toInteger($k)`. It handles multi-type groups natively. 2. Rewrite the vector half of policy-scoped.ts as the same exact scan restricted to the named policy's documents (`toUpper(coalesce(d.policy_number,'')) IN $policyNumbers`). This makes the named-policy pass exact and removes any need to put policy_number in the index. 3. Keep queryNodes with overscan for the large groups for now, and correct the outdated "cannot pre-filter" comment in scoring-constants.ts. 4. Update the test matchers that key on the literal queryNodes string: graph-fixtures.ts:277 and :284, policy-corpus search.test.ts:92, :223, :235 and :246, and six sites in agent search.test.ts. 5. Re-run the retrieval evaluation before and after. Each query will now carry up to perGroupK low-similarity federal candidates (about 0.73 to 0.79, against 0.88 and above for in-domain hits), so check they do not displace better evidence in answer-frame selection. **Phase 2: ship as a blue-green corpus release, not an in-place change** 1. Set `s.document_type` on Section in the loader (queries.py, next to `s.document_id`) and on Chunk. 2. Drop and recreate both vector indexes with `WITH [s.document_type]`. `IF NOT EXISTS` will not alter the existing indexes. Change indexer.py and packages/agent/tests/helpers/integration-indexes.ts together. 3. Add a release check that no embedded Section or Chunk lacks document_type, because the index treats a missing property as null and silently excludes the node. 4. Move the large groups to `MATCH (s:Section) SEARCH s IN (VECTOR INDEX section_embeddings FOR $embedding WHERE s.document_type = $type LIMIT $k) SCORE AS score`, then join to Document. Use one statement per type on 2026.05, or bump the pinned image to 2026.06 or later and use IN. 5. Delete VECTOR_OVERSCAN_FACTOR, VECTOR_OVERSCAN_MAX and vectorScanK, and retire the deprecated procedure.
- **How it was verified:** The claim holds in substance; I could not refute it. **Code.** The quoted comment and constants are verbatim at the cited lines. `vectorScanK` caps the scan at 200, and the default `perGroupK` is 40 (topK 10 times 4), so the scan is 200 for every group. `section-vector.ts`, `chunk-vector.ts` and `policy-scoped.ts` all call `queryNodes` with that scan size and filter by document type (and policy number) afterwards. No other guard widens the pool, so each group gets only its share of the global top 200. **Live graph.** The local server is Neo4j Kernel 2026.05.0, matching the pinned image. Section vectors per document type sum to exactly 56,102, with `federal_statute` at 233. Neither vector index has additional filter properties. **Recall experiment.** I reran a smaller version of the reviewer's test: 5 stored section embeddings per source type as query vectors, counting document types in …
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted: this is a real, untracked weakness and medium is the right level. What I opened: scoring-constants.ts, section-vector.ts, policy-scoped.ts, docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/reviews/2026-06-11-findings-high-critical.txt, commit 101aa83, the compose files, the open-issue list, the local graph (read-only) and the Neo4j Cypher Manual SEARCH page. 1. Not an accepted trade-off. The 200 cap exists only as a code comment and the 101aa83 commit message. Neither gotchas.md nor any non-archive doc records it as a deliberate limit, and no issue tracks it. It is not on the previously-decided list; the reranker decision (c48a9d4) is about ordering hits, not candidate recall. 2. The earlier …

## RETR-21 · Full-text recovery favours the first type group and passes operators through to Lucene

Severity medium · effort S

### corpus-retrieval-06 — Full-text recovery is effectively limited to the first document-type group, is not a phrase search, and passes AND/OR/NOT through to Lucene

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/collector.ts:184`
- **Severity:** medium (reviewer proposed high) · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim is true in substance. Two precision points: (1) later-group fulltext-only hits land exactly on the 0.01 floor only when midScore is at most 0.02 x perGroupK + 0.01 (0.81 at topK 10), which is the usual case; with a higher mid they sit slightly above the floor but still at least 0.80 below the first group. (2) finalizeHybridHits applies rerankRetrievalHit boosts after the merge, so a floored hit is heavily disadvantaged rather than strictly unrecoverable. The collector comment describing the old flooring behaviour is at collector.ts:163-166 and the test is at tests/retrieval/collector.test.ts:122-135.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/collector.ts:181-184 `for (let index = 0; index < fulltextHits.length; index += 1) {` / `const hit = fulltextHits[index];` / `if (!sectionMap.has(hit.sectionId)) {` / `hit.score = Math.max(midScore - 0.02 * index, 0.01);`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/fulltext.ts:17 `for (const docTypes of profile.typeGroups) {`; :40 `fulltextHits.push(...result.records.map(recordToRetrievalHit));`; :42-43 `} catch {` / `return [];`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/collector.ts:160 `// Fulltext is the exact-phrase recovery path, so it doesn't merge-max:`. packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/base.ts:1 `export const LUCENE_SPECIAL = /[+\-&|!(){}[\]^"~*?:\\/]/g;`
- **Impact:** Probe with the real collector and fulltext backend over a fake session, default five groups and perGroupK=40: the best fulltext-only hit in clinical_policy (raw BM25 6) is inserted at 0.790, while the best in dhhs_policy (raw BM25 30) is inserted at 0.010, as is every hit from federal_regulation, federal_statute and the bulletin/SPA/waiver group. An exact wording match in a DHHS eligibility manual or a CFR section that the other channels missed therefore cannot be recovered, which is the failure the comment at collector.ts:163-166 says was fixed. Separately, "Is PCS NOT covered in adult care homes?" is sent with NOT intact, which excludes sections containing "covered", and a question ending in AND or OR is a parse error that returns [] silently. The only unit test of this merge (tests/retrieval/collector.test.ts:123) uses a single two-hit list.
- **Fix:** Keep the per-group queries and make four small changes; do not add an index-level limit. 1. **Ranking (collector.ts `mergeFulltextHits`):** before the positional loop, rank a copy of the hits by raw BM25, e.g. `const ranked = [...fulltextHits].sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score)`, and iterate `ranked`. The score is already on each hit and is comparable across groups. No single all-types query and no rank-fusion rewrite. 2. **Operators (fulltext.ts):** build the query from `input.query.toLowerCase()` before `escapeLucene`. This neutralises AND/OR/NOT and removes the trailing-operator parse error. 3. **Phrases:** append each `analysis.quotedPhrases` entry as an escaped, double-quoted clause so user-quoted wording is a real phrase match. Then correct the "exact-phrase" comments at collector.ts:45 and :160 to say what the stage does. Treat stop-word dropping and bigram clauses as an optional follow-up: reuse `analysis.searchTerms` rather than new lists, and gate it on an eval run. 4. **Errors:** move the try/catch inside the `typeGroups` loop, as keyword.ts does, and call `debugSearch` with the error so one failing group no longer discards the others. 5. **Do not pass `{limit}` to `queryNodes`** while the document-type filter runs after the index call. It would truncate before filtering and starve small type groups. 6. **Tests:** add a collector case where the fulltext mock returns a later-positioned hit with a higher raw score and assert it lands above an earlier, lower-scored fulltext-only hit. Add a fulltext backend test with a fake session covering: one group throwing while others return rows; "Is PCS NOT covered" sending no uppercase operator; a quoted phrase producing a quoted clause.
- **How it was verified:** I read collector.ts, backends/fulltext.ts, internal/base.ts, the profile and scoring constants, and the only fulltext merge test, then ran read-only probes against the local Neo4j index. Every quoted line is present verbatim at the cited location, and every sub-claim holds. 1. Positional scoring across concatenated groups: confirmed. fulltext.ts loops over profile.typeGroups sequentially and pushes each group's rows (LIMIT perGroupK) onto one array. mergeFulltextHits scores fulltext-only hits by index in that array. With topK 10, perGroupK is 40, so group 2 starts at index 40 and is penalised by at least 0.80. The other backends score on roughly a 0 to 1 scale, so midScore is typically below 0.81 and groups 2 to 5 land on the 0.01 floor regardless of BM25. Nothing between the backend and the merge re-sorts by score. I did not re-run the reviewer's fake-session probe; the arithmetic foll…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted, but I would defend medium rather than high. What I opened: collector.ts, backends/fulltext.ts, backends/keyword.ts, internal/base.ts, scoring-constants.ts, retrieval-planning.ts, the retrieval profile files, docs/development/gotchas.md, the two 2026-06-11 review docs, the open issue list, and the live index definition (read-only). Why it is a real problem here: - **Not an accepted trade-off.** Nothing in gotchas, decisions or operations docs covers the fulltext merge. The only prior record is the June remediation plan, which treated "fulltext can never contribute" as a defect to fix. The collector comment claims it was fixed. - **The fix only works for the first type group.** T…

## RETR-22 · Ten retrieval catch blocks turn any Neo4j error into an empty result without logging

Severity medium · effort S

### corpus-retrieval-10 — A failing or slow backend is invisible: ten catch blocks discard errors without telemetry and there is no search-level deadline

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/keyword.ts:70`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Ten catch blocks across seven retrieval backend files (keyword.ts x2, chunk-vector.ts, fulltext.ts, concept.ts, policy-scoped.ts, amendment-context.ts, document-expansion.ts x3) discard every error with no log, counter or flag on the result. The only instrumentation is duration logging behind POLICY_CORPUS_SEARCH_DEBUG=1, which is set nowhere in the repo and never records the error. Backends run concurrently, each in its own session, but each loops its type groups sequentially. The only bound is the agent's 30 s per-statement timeout, and there is no search-level deadline. The worst case is therefore per backend: 8 groups x 30 s = 240 s for an operational query on the backends with a per-group catch (chunk_vector, section_keyword, document_keyword, policy_scoped, amendment_context). fulltext and section_vector stop after the first failure, about 30 s. The 180 s turn budget it is compared against appears only as a comment in amendments.ts, not as an enforced limit. A section_vector failure rejects the whole search, so silent thinning applies to every other channel; in lexical-degraded mode section_vector is removed and a search can return [] with every remaining channel failed. Neither readiness nor /corpus-status probes index existence or state.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/keyword.ts:70-71 `} catch {` / `// Keyword fallback is best effort.`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/chunk-vector.ts:34-35 `} catch {` / `// chunk_embeddings index may not exist.`. packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/fulltext.ts:42-43 `} catch {` / `return [];`. The same pattern is at concept.ts:41, policy-scoped.ts:96, amendment-context.ts:73, keyword.ts:145 and document-expansion.ts:69, :121, :171. packages/agent/src/neo4j.ts:17 `const NEO4J_QUERY_TIMEOUT_MS = intEnv('NEO4J_QUERY_TIMEOUT_MS', 30_000);`
- **Impact:** If the chunk index is missing after a release, the full-text index is still populating, or a refactor breaks one backend's Cypher, searches keep returning plausible but thinner results indefinitely, and nothing in the logs, /corpus-status or the tool result says so; the recall regression then looks like a corpus or model problem. As a worst-case bound, an operational query (8 groups) against a backend whose statements all time out waits 8 x 30 s = 240 s before the search returns, beyond the 180 s turn budget cited in amendments.ts:19-20. In lexical-degraded mode the same silence covers the remaining channels, so a search can return [] with every channel having failed.
- **Fix:** Keep the goal, drop the per-backend result object, the deadline framework, group concurrency and the /corpus-status change. 1. Add `bestEffort: boolean` to RetrievalBackend and set it in backend-registry.ts, which becomes the single required-versus-best-effort table. Delete the ten in-backend catch blocks and catch once in collectHybridHitMap around each `backend.retrieve` and each expansion call. If bestEffort, call the failure hook and use []; otherwise rethrow. The first failure then ends that backend, so the worst case drops from 8 x 30 s to roughly one 30 s timeout per backend. The cost is losing partial hits from groups that succeeded before the failure, which is acceptable because these failures are not group-specific. 2. Inject `onBackendFailure?: (event: { backend: string; error: unknown }) => void` through createNeo4jPolicyCorpus and createHybridRetriever, the same way embedQuery is injected, defaulting to console.warn. No logger dependency in the library and no change to searchSectionsDetailed's return type. 3. In the agent, wire the hook to `log.warn('retrieval.backend_failed', { backend, code })` and push the backend name into the existing retrievalModeContext store. Return `degradedBackends: string[]` beside retrievalMode from searchPolicySections and add it to the search tool's outputSchema. Run the agent tests and an agent build, per AGENTS.md. 4. Make chunk_vector required, or at least correct its comment, since the pipeline always creates the index. 5. Update primitives.test.ts:394-444 to assert the hook fires for the chunk failure, and add a case where every best-effort backend fails in lexical mode and degradedBackends is non-empty. 6. If latency under a slow single backend is ever observed, lower NEO4J_QUERY_TIMEOUT_MS for retrieval first. Treat a single-statement UNWIND/CALL rewrite as a separate change gated on the retrieval eval.
- **How it was verified:** The claim is true in substance; I found no guard elsewhere that reports backend failures or bounds a search. What I opened: all eight files under packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends, collector.ts, hybrid.ts, backend-registry.ts, internal/base.ts (timeSearchStep / debugSearch), profiles/retrieval-signals.ts, internal/scoring-constants.ts and retrieval-planning.ts, policy-corpus/src/policy-corpus.ts, agent/src/neo4j.ts, agent/src/policy-corpus.ts, agent/src/tools/search.ts, agent/src/readiness.ts and corpus-status.ts, and tests/primitives.test.ts:394-444. What I found: - **Ten catch blocks:** a grep for `catch` across the backends returns exactly ten sites, at the cited lines, each either empty with a comment or `return []`. None binds the error. - **No telemetry:** the only logging in policy-corpus/src is `debugSearch` in internal/base.ts, gated on `POLICY_CORPUS_SEARCH_DEBUG =…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted; medium stands, with the 240 s worst case narrowed. What I opened: all retrieval backends, retrieval/collector.ts, retrieval/hybrid.ts, packages/agent/src/neo4j.ts, packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts, corpus-status.ts, health.ts, tests/primitives.test.ts, docs/development/gotchas.md, and the open issue list. Why it is not dismissible: - Not an accepted, documented trade-off. The only rationale is one-line comments ("best effort", "index may not exist") and one test asserting the swallow. gotchas.md has no entry on swallowed retrieval errors, and it repeatedly treats "silently" degrading behaviour as a defect class, so the finding fits the project's own standards. - Not tracked.…

### perf-cost-reliability-06 — Nine of ten retrieval queries turn any Neo4j error into an empty result with no log

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/fulltext.ts:42`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Every retrieval query site except section_vector (ten catch blocks covering eleven session.run sites, across fulltext, section_keyword, document_keyword, chunk_vector, concept, policy_scoped, amendment_context and the three document-expansion helpers) sits in a bare catch that returns nothing and logs nothing. For those backends a transaction timeout, dropped connection or missing index is indistinguishable from "no hits". retrievalMode reflects only Voyage embedding success, so a partial Neo4j fault with Voyage healthy is still reported as "hybrid". With Voyage healthy, a total Neo4j outage is not silent, because the unguarded section_vector backend rejects the whole search. With Voyage down, the vector backends are skipped, every remaining backend swallows errors, and a Neo4j fault yields an empty result labelled "lexical-degraded" (not "hybrid") with only the Voyage warning logged. The 150 s figure is 5 default type groups x the 30 s timeout for the sequential section_keyword loop (3 groups for federal queries, 8 for operational, 1 for explicit document types); it is arithmetic, not observed. fulltext's catch wraps its whole loop, so it stops at the first error and discards hits from groups that already succeeded.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/fulltext.ts:42-43 `} catch {` `return [];` | keyword.ts:70-71 `} catch {` `// Keyword fallback is best effort.` | same pattern at keyword.ts:145, chunk-vector.ts:34, concept.ts:41, policy-scoped.ts:96, amendment-context.ts:73, document-expansion.ts:69, :121, :171 | packages/agent/src/neo4j.ts:17 `const NEO4J_QUERY_TIMEOUT_MS = intEnv('NEO4J_QUERY_TIMEOUT_MS', 30_000);`
- **Impact:** Under load the section_keyword scan reaches the 30 s transaction timeout for each of five type groups in turn: the search takes 150 s, returns without keyword evidence, and nothing is logged or reported. When Voyage is also degraded the remaining backends are exactly the ones that swallow errors, so a Neo4j fault yields an empty frame that reads as 'the corpus is silent'. The operator has no signal that answers are being built on partial retrieval, and auto-commit session.run is never retried (maxTransactionRetryTime in neo4j.ts:44 only applies to managed transactions).
- **Fix:** Keep the required/best-effort split that primitives.test.ts pins; do not rethrow from best-effort backends and do not special-case index-not-found. 1. Remove the bare catches from the backends and let them throw. In keyword.ts, policy-scoped.ts, amendment-context.ts and chunk-vector.ts this also ends the per-type-group retry after a failure, which removes the 5 x 30 s case. 2. In collector.ts, wrap each best-effort branch (stage 1 backends other than section_vector, fulltext, and the three expansion calls) in one helper that catches, records `{ backend, code: err.code ?? err.name }` in a `failedBackends` list, and returns []. section_vector stays unwrapped so it still propagates. 3. Return `failedBackends` from collectHybridHitMap / searchSectionsDetailed. If every attempted stage-1 backend and fulltext failed, throw instead of returning an empty result (this covers the Voyage-down plus Neo4j-fault case). 4. In packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts, emit one `log.warn('retrieval.backends_failed', ...)` per search and add `failedBackends: string[]` (or a third mode, 'partial') to the search tool output next to retrievalMode. Skip the /corpus-status addition: that value is a process-global last-writer flag, and the log plus per-request field are enough. 5. For retries, change only withSession in packages/agent/src/neo4j.ts: make the session.run override delegate to `session.executeRead((tx) => tx.run(query, params), { timeout: NEO4J_QUERY_TIMEOUT_MS, ...config })`. No backend or SessionLike change is needed. Describe it accurately: it retries dropped connections and transient errors only, not timeouts. 6. Update the 'tolerates chunk-vector failures' test to also assert the failed backend is reported, and add a test for the all-backends-failed throw.
- **How it was verified:** The claim is true in substance; three details need correcting. I read all eight files under packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends, plus collector.ts, hybrid.ts, internal/base.ts, internal/scoring-constants.ts, profiles/retrieval-signals.ts, and agent/src/neo4j.ts and agent/src/policy-corpus.ts. I ran no tests; the code reading settles it. What holds: - All ten quoted catch sites exist at the cited lines. Each is a bare `catch {` that returns `[]` or falls through with only a comment. None binds the error, logs it or rethrows. - section-vector.ts is the only backend with no try/catch. - Nothing upstream compensates. collectHybridHitMap only merges the arrays it gets back. timeSearchStep is a try/finally that emits a timing line, and only when POLICY_CORPUS_SEARCH_DEBUG=1; it never emits the error. - retrievalMode is set only inside embedQuery, from Voyage success or failure. No N…
- **Why this severity:** I opened all eight files under packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends, retrieval/collector.ts, retrieval/hybrid.ts, packages/agent/src/neo4j.ts, packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts, packages/agent/src/readiness.ts, the retrievalMode references in the agent, docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/operations, RUNBOOK.md, and the open issue list. The finding survives this lens, with a narrower impact than the reviewer describes. Not an accepted trade-off. The only rationale is in the code comments ("Keyword fallback is best effort", "chunk_embeddings index may not exist"). That explains tolerating a missing index; it does not explain catching every error without a log. gotchas.md, docs/opera…

## RETR-23 · The agent's planning processor re-derives question intent and injects policy-specific directives

Severity medium · effort L

### agent-processors-tools-07 — AnswerFramePlanningProcessor is 841 lines of golden-question-specific prompt patches that leak into unrelated questions

- **Location:** `packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts:314`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** L · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The 841-line AnswerFramePlanningProcessor contains three question-family rule tables (coverage, prior-authorization, federal-authority; roughly lines 130-624) that re-derive question intent with local phrase lists duplicating PolicyCorpus QueryFlags predicates, and that hard-code policy-specific outline wording (speech-language telehealth, air ambulance, congregate PCS, CAP/DA layering, premiums, Attachment K, MLR, waiver applications). Section buckets are built by substring match over title plus content ('therapy', '3.1', 'eligibility' and similar), so given a matching frame the speech-language and Attachment K directives fire for unrelated policies, and the pharmacy rule renders an empty section reference when no quantity section is surfaced. The route-lane, amendment, variant and general tables and the plan header are generic. There are six family tables plus the route table, not five. 10 of the 15 question call sites in the unit test are verbatim golden questions. The line-307 directive is gated on the telehealth section containing one of TELEHEALTH_CONDITION_PHRASES; only line 314 is unconditional speech-language wording. The leak was demonstrated with hand-built frames, not with frames produced by live PolicyCorpus resolution.
- **Evidence:** answer-frame-planning.ts:314 `: \`Use ${listSections(ctx.serviceSpecificSections)} only to explain that the ordinary speech-language therapy criteria still apply, and cite that section separately from the telehealth rule when you mention documentation or service-specific limits.\`,` :307 `'State the beneficiary-specific telehealth conditions explicitly: why telehealth is medically necessary, when it must remain adjunct to in-person care, and that it cannot be used for therapist convenience or productivity.',` :143-152 SERVICE_SPECIFIC_PHRASES includes `'therapy',` :467 `'Do not present a partial sample drug list as if it were exhaustive. If you mention examples, label them as examples only; otherwise say Attachment K / the pharmacy prior-approval criteria contains the detailed list. ...`
- **Impact:** Ran the processor with 'What are the telehealth requirements for outpatient psychotherapy under NC Medicaid?' and a frame holding a generic telehealth section ('remote or underserved areas') plus an 'Outpatient Psychotherapy' section. The injected system checklist included 'State the beneficiary-specific telehealth conditions explicitly: ... adjunct to in-person care, and that it cannot be used for therapist convenience or productivity.' and 'Use § 3.2.1 Outpatient Psychotherapy only to explain that the ordinary speech-language therapy criteria still apply'. That is Policy 10A content asserted for a behavioural-health question with no supporting evidence. A pharmacy quantity question whose frame lacks a quantity section yields 'read and state the yes/no conclusion' (empty reference) and an instruction to name Attachment K. The model either repeats unsupported claims for the verifier to catch or ignores system instructions. Because benchmark questions have bespoke outlines, benchmark scores likely overstate behaviour on unseen questions (inference; no ablation was run).
- **Fix:** Do this as a deletion-first change with no new `answerPlan` type. 1. **Keep** `buildPlanMessage`'s summary lines, the `coverageScope` notice and requirements, ROUTE_RULES, AMENDMENT_RULES, VARIANT_RULES, GENERAL_RULES, and the evidence-gated EPSDT rule (line 293) with its three tests. 2. **Delete** PRIOR_AUTH_RULES and FEDERAL_RULES, and all of COVERAGE_RULES except the EPSDT rule. Delete their phrase-list constants and the duplicated intent detectors (`isYesNoCoverageQuestion`, `asksForTelehealthServiceList`, `isPharmacyQuantityPriorAuthQuestion`). Coverage, prior-auth and federal questions then get the route rules plus the general "Answer directly from <primary sections>" line. 3. **Render each section's existing `reason`** next to its label in the "Cover first", "supporting" and "Do not lead with" lines. PolicyCorpus's own selection rationale then reaches the model without a second classifier. 4. **Restore behaviour only where measurement shows a loss, and do it in PolicyCorpus.** Use section selection and ordering, a section `reason`, or one more boolean in `route.determinism_hints`, each rendered by a single generic ROUTE_RULES line. Do not base this on Jev section roles until that feature is qualified and on by default. 5. **Measure with repeated runs, per the gotchas guidance on single-run wobble.** Compare before and after on: - `dataset.json`; - `heldout-paraphrase.json`, treated as phrasing robustness only, since its entries are paraphrases of golden questions; - a small new set of 15 to 20 off-topic questions that share trigger words but target other policies, for example telehealth for psychotherapy, a quantity-limit question outside pharmacy prior approval, and a non-ambulance "not covered" question. This is the set that tests leakage. A temporary environment switch for the planner is enough for the ablation; remove it afterwards. 6. **Replace the verbatim golden question strings** in answer-frame-planning.test.ts with synthetic questions. Add one negative test from the reviewer's probe: a psychotherapy telehealth question must not yield speech-language directives.
- **How it was verified:** I read all of packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts (841 lines), its test file, the processor wiring in packages/agent/src/index.ts, the PolicyCorpus QueryFlags definitions, the two cited docs, and reran the reviewer's two scenarios with a throwaway script. What holds: - Every quoted line is present verbatim at the cited line (307, 314, 143-152, 467). - The processor re-derives intent with its own phrase lists. Its local asksForTelehealthServiceList (lines 24-42) is a phrase-for-phrase copy of the policy-corpus function in query-intent.ts:150-168. The pharmacy-quantity, congregate, MLR and waiver-application predicates are near-duplicates with slightly different phrase sets, so they can drift from the QueryFlags versions. - Matching is a plain substring test over lowercased title plus full content (sectionText line 59-61, includesAny line 16-18), with phrases such as 't…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted; medium stands. I looked for a reason a maintainer would dismiss this (accepted trade-off, open issue, prior decision) and found the opposite: the project's own docs say to stop doing it, and an earlier review's fix was never applied. What I opened: packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts (lines 1-560 and 700-841), docs/architecture/README.md, docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/reviews/2026-06-11-rag-faithfulness-review.md, docs/reviews/2026-06-11-remediation-plan.md, docs/refactor-2026-06-19.md, packages/pipeline/data/golden/heldout-paraphrase.json, git log for the file, and the open issue list. 1. **Not an accepted trade-off.** Both the architecture README and go…

### architecture-07 — Question classification is implemented three times and has drifted; 57 policy-family prompt directives live in the agent against the documented boundary

- **Location:** `packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts:20`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The agent's planning processor (packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts, registered in index.ts:141) re-derives question intent from raw text with its own regexes, 36 named phrase lists and about 8 inline ones. It injects 57 checklist rules as a system message, about 44 of them policy-family-specific, against docs/architecture/README.md:17. Its yes/no predicate (`^(does|is|can)` + `\bcover\b`) disagrees with the corpus isYesNoCoverageQuery on "Is/Are ... covered" and "Will ... cover" phrasings; the comment at :213-214 records this as an undone follow-up. Several lists are copies of corpus lists that have diverged (pharmacy quantity, history) or are gated differently (telehealth service list). Impact, corrected: in the default baseline_hybrid coverage lane the route rule at :713-716 still tells the model to answer yes or no first and pair scope with not-covered, because it reads the corpus hint. What is lost for "Is X covered" phrasings is the family rule at :275-285: the named scope and not-covered section citations and the "say plainly when the policy governs a different service category" directive. In the deterministic_evidence lane neither rule fires. The recorded q-026 wording ("Does NC Medicaid cover chiropractic services under Clinical Coverage Policy 5B?") is not affected; the failing example is a paraphrase.
- **Evidence:** packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts:20-22 `function isYesNoCoverageQuestion(questionLower: string): boolean {` / `return /^(does|is|can)\b/.test(questionLower) && /\bcover\b/.test(questionLower);`; :213-214 `// Local coverage matcher: the shared yes/no regex misses "covered"` / `// phrasing (separate follow-up).`; :368 `For this ambulance non-covered question, lead with ${listSections(ctx.specificLimitationSections, 1)} and keep general exclusions secondary unless they add a missing limit.`; packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:286-292 isYesNoCoverageQuery accepts does|is|can|are|will|under and cover(s|ed|ing|age), authoriz*, eligible; docs/architecture/README.md:17 `Policy-family routing belongs in `PolicyCorpus` answer-frame resolution.` Script result: for 'Is chiropractic care covered under Policy 5B?', 'Are dental implants covered for adults?', 'Is acupuncture a covered service?' and 'Will Medicaid cover a wheelchair ramp?' the corpus sets cite_scope_and_not_covered = true while the planner predicate returns false (4 of 6 phrasings disagree).
- **Impact:** For 'Is chiropractic care covered under Policy 5B?' (golden q-026 phrasing) the frame's route says to pair scope and not-covered sections, but the planner's family rule that tells the model to answer yes or no first and to say plainly when the policy governs a different service category does not fire, because 'covered' does not match the word-bounded 'cover'. Answers to the most natural yes/no phrasing get weaker guidance than 'Does Medicaid cover X', and each fix must be made in two packages and three vocabularies.
- **Fix:** Do this in three small steps, and do not ship `directives: string[]` in the tool result or move schemas into contracts. 1. **Fix the drift now (one line).** In `answer-frame-planning.ts`, replace `ctx.yesNoCoverageQuestion` at the rule near line 277 with `ctx.frame.route.determinism_hints.cite_scope_and_not_covered`, and delete `isYesNoCoverageQuestion`. Add a planner test for "Is chiropractic care covered under Policy 5B?". 2. **Expose the corpus flags and delete the local question predicates.** - Add `queryFlags: QueryFlags` to `AnswerFrame`, populated from `analysis.flags` in the pipelines and in the scoped path in `frame/service.ts`. Export the type. - Add it to the tool `outputSchema`, otherwise Mastra strips it. - Replace the planner's local question predicates and question phrase lists with `frame.queryFlags.*` (telehealth service list, pharmacy quantity, not-covered, ambulance not-covered, congregate, eligibility criteria, waiver application, history, implementation). - Add any question predicate the corpus lacks to `query-intent.ts` rather than keeping it in the agent. - Leave the section-content phrase buckets and rule tables where they are. - Before deleting each local predicate, run the existing 17 planner tests plus a small differential over the golden questions, and record intended behaviour changes. The corpus classifies the tool `query`; the planner classified the latest user message. 3. **Deduplicate the section schema inside the agent.** Export one `SectionViewSchema` from `tools/section-metadata.ts` (or a new `tools/schemas.ts`), reuse it in `read.ts`, and extend it with `reason` in `answer-frame.ts`. Add a compile-time check that `z.infer<typeof SectionViewSchema>` matches `Omit<SectionView, 'sectionRoles'>` from policy-corpus, so a new corpus field fails typecheck instead of being silently stripped. Keep policy-corpus free of zod. Only consider moving the rule tables into policy-corpus as a separate, optional step, and only if the checklist needs to appear in eval route capture. If so, expose it as a pure exported function (for example `buildAnswerChecklist(question, frame)`) that the processor calls, and add its output to the `---ROUTE---` emission. Do not embed it in the frame returned to the model, and repeat the differential-grid check used in #62.
- **How it was verified:** I read packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts in full, the corpus predicates in packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts and answer-routing.ts, and docs/architecture/README.md:17, then re-ran the two yes/no regexes on the reviewer's phrasings in a throwaway script. The core claim holds: - All quoted evidence is present verbatim at the cited lines (20-22, 213-214, 368; query-intent.ts:286-292; README:17). - The processor is registered in packages/agent/src/index.ts:141, so it is live. - It re-derives intent from the raw question with its own regexes and phrase lists and injects a system message. - The file has exactly 57 rule entries (`when:` count). - The predicate disagreement reproduces: 4 of the reviewer's 6 phrasings differ (planner false, corpus true). - The duplicated lists exist and have drifted. Telehealth service list: identical 13 phrases, different …
- **Why this severity:** I read packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts in full (841 lines), the corpus predicate and route selection in policy-corpus, docs/architecture/README.md, docs/development/gotchas.md, the June review and refactor log, and the open issue list. The finding survives this lens, but its impact scenario is overstated and it is not new. Why it is not dismissible: - It contradicts a documented boundary rather than a documented trade-off. Both the architecture guide and gotchas say policy-family behaviour belongs in resolveAnswerFrame / PolicyCorpus and the agent prompt should stay limited to durable rules. The planner injects ambulance, congregate PCS, telehealth speech-language, At…

## RETR-24 · The model's optional question-type hint overrides the router, and frame tests bypass it

Severity medium · effort M

### corpus-frames-04 — The agent model's optional questionType overrides the router; recorded routes differ from the router 40% of the time and frame tests bypass it

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/evidence-plan.ts:15`
- **Severity:** medium (reviewer proposed high) · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** True in substance, with three precision points. (1) The saved runs record the resulting route, not the tool arguments, so the 39.6% disagreement (99 of 250 at commit 210ced2, 20 of 50 questions) measures the model-authored tool call (optional `questionType` and/or a reworded `query`) against the router on the original question; it is not a direct count of `questionType` overrides. (2) The figure comes from the 50-question held-out paraphrase eval set in July runs stored in gitignored local files, not production traffic. (3) "Untested" should read: 28 of 29 frame-test resolutions force a type and one (`frames-coverage.test.ts:515`) runs through the router; the router also has its own unit tests (`query-intent.test.ts`, `query-analysis.test.ts`, `evidence-plan.test.ts`, `answer-routing.test.ts`). What is largely missing is the router-to-pipeline composition on the frame fixtures.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/evidence-plan.ts:15 `return analyzeQuery(input.query, input.questionType);` packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:432 `if (requestedType) return requestedType;` (line 430 comments: `// A model-supplied generic hint must not hide the governing EPSDT sources.`) packages/agent/src/tools/answer-frame.ts:82 `.describe('Optional family hint. Omit to let the corpus detect the question family automatically.'),`
- **Impact:** In the last five saved full answer runs (commit 210ced2, 250 question-runs) the recorded route differs from what the router returns for the same question in 99 cases (39.6%), covering 20 of 50 questions. Earlier groups show 32.4% and 37.7%. The same question on the same commit flips pipelines between runs: h-014 was prior-auth twice and coverage three times; h-045 was federal four times and amendment once. The hint often helps ("Will ... pay for ..." goes to coverage) and sometimes hurts: h-033 went to prior-auth in 5 of 5 runs with 42 CFR Part 412 (Medicare inpatient payment) as the authoritative document. Of 29 frame resolutions in the four frames-*.test.ts files, 28 force a type. Three scenarios tested as coverage (h-005, h-012, q-016) are `general` under the router. The router files differ from that commit only by the 10-line EPSDT special case.
- **Fix:** Keep the forced step-0 tool call and the model-supplied `query`; remove only the model's ability to choose the pipeline. 1. **Fix the router first** (next finding), so questions the hint currently rescues, such as q-047, do not regress. 2. **Check the flips at zero cost before removing the hint.** In the saved `*_answer.json` runs, compare per-question scores between runs of the same question on the same commit where the route flipped (h-014, h-045, h-033 and similar). This shows where the hint was actually load-bearing and yields router cases to fix. 3. **Delete `questionType` from the `resolveAnswerFrame` tool's input schema** in `packages/agent/src/tools/answer-frame.ts`. - Call `policyCorpus.resolveAnswerFrame({ query: inputData.query })`. - Update the "passes through an explicit questionType hint" test in `packages/agent/tests/tools/answer-frame.test.ts`. - Keep `ResolveAnswerFrameInput.questionType` in policy-corpus for unit tests and the offline Jev harness. - This fixes chat and deep research together and leaves the planning processor, verifier evidence extraction and UI untouched. 4. **Do not resolve from the raw latest user message in the prefetch processor.** That would route and retrieve follow-up turns on context-free text and would require synthesising tool-invocation parts. 5. **Drop the "required enum with per-type descriptions" alternative.** It is the prompt-level routing AGENTS.md rules out, and an LLM routing classifier was already evaluated and kept offline. 6. **Add one table-driven router test in policy-corpus.** Assert `analyzeQuery(query).questionType` with no forced type for every query used in the `frames-*.test.ts` fixtures and the golden questions, including h-005, h-012 and q-016. Leave the pipeline-isolation tests forcing a type. 7. **Skip the 155-input labelled evaluation.** No complete human labels exist and the owner declined further labelling. Use the existing Jev routing disagreement export only as a list of router cases to inspect.
- **How it was verified:** I could not refute it; the code path, the quoted evidence and every number reproduce. **Code path.** All three quotes are verbatim at the cited lines. `buildEvidencePlan` passes `input.questionType` straight into `analyzeQuery`, which calls `detectAnswerQuestionType`. That function returns the requested type before any router predicate runs; the only exception is the EPSDT overview case when the hint is absent or `general`. The agent tool hands the model's arguments to `policyCorpus.resolveAnswerFrame(inputData)` unchanged. **No guard exists.** The prefetch processor forces the tool call on step 0 but only asks the model, in a system message, to pass the question verbatim. It does not strip or fix `questionType`, and the model also authors `query`. No non-test code in `packages/agent/src` overrides the argument. **Saved-run numbers.** I extracted the recorded route from every local `*_a…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted, but high is not defensible; I would defend medium. What I opened: the three cited code sites, the prefetch and planning processors, the agent prompt, docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/development/jev.md, AGENTS.md, the open issue list, the four frames-*.test.ts files, and the last five full saved answer runs (all git_sha 210ced2). Why it survives: - The design is as described. The prefetch processor forces resolveAnswerFrame on step 0 and tells the model to pass the question verbatim, but says nothing about questionType. The schema leaves it optional, and the corpus returns any supplied type as-is. - It is not a documented trade-off. Nothing in gotchas.md, docs/decisions or the…

### r2-critic-stored-run-incidence-07 — Same-commit route flips are score-neutral: stored runs show the model-written frame call neither helping nor hurting, and cannot speak for the 20 questions that always diverge

- **Location:** `packages/agent/src/tools/answer-frame.ts:79`
- **Severity:** low · **Kind:** recommendation · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Replaying each commit's keyword router on the original question for all 2,178 stored rows with a recorded route gives 672 rows (30.9%) where the recorded route differs from the router. Twenty of 331 same-commit question groups flip route between runs. In the 15 flip groups with both arms scored (44 router-agreeing rows, 40 diverging), the diverging arm is 0.065 lower on the unweighted group mean (not 0.04): 3 groups lower, 2 higher, 10 equal. One 1-against-1 group (q-008, -1.00) accounts for nearly all of it. Scores are integers 0 to 3 with 69% at 3, so this is an underpowered null, not proof the route is score-neutral. Twenty of 100 questions diverge in every run and still do at HEAD. Fifteen are router general against model coverage, two are general against amendment, three are amendment against federal, and one (q-025) is router coverage against model general. Route decisions depend only on query text; the scores come from legacy-id graph builds. The recorded route also reflects the model's reworded query, so none of this isolates questionType.
- **Evidence:** packages/agent/src/tools/answer-frame.ts:79-82 `questionType: z` / `.enum(ANSWER_QUESTION_TYPES)` / `.optional()` / `.describe('Optional family hint. Omit to let the corpus detect the question family automatically.'),`. s5_routes.mjs output: `recorded lane+rationale == router(question) at that commit: 1506 / 2178`; `groups where the recorded route differs between runs: 20 (6.0%)`; `router-agreeing arm better 3, model-diverging arm better 2, equal 10`; `commit 210ced2: differ 99/250; questions 20`. Per-question examples: h-033, router federal_multi_hop 1 row at 3.00 against prior-auth 5 rows at 2.80; h-045, router latest_revision_marker 2 rows at 2.00 against federal_multi_hop 5 rows at 2.20.
- **Impact:** The stored runs do not show the hint to be load-bearing where it varies, so they do not argue against removing it. They also cannot predict what removal does to the 20 questions where the model always overrides the router, which is where the change would actually bite.
- **Fix:** Fold this into corpus-frames-04 as "step 2 done": the stored runs give no evidence the hint is load-bearing where it varies, and the 20 always-diverging questions are the only remaining unknown. 1. Fix the router first, using corpus-frames-05's list. Step 5 there (add "pay(s) for" and "eligible for" to coverage vocabulary and widen the yes/no opener) targets most of the 15 general-to-coverage questions. The SPA exclusion in step 3 targets q-047 and h-047. 2. Re-run HEAD's analyzeQuery on the 20 questions (free and offline) to confirm which now route as the model chose. Expect q-042, h-042 (currently-effective revision), q-045 and q-025 to need separate decisions. 3. Remove questionType from the resolveAnswerFrame tool schema, then compare the 20 questions over at least three runs each. Act only on a mean shift of about 0.3 or more with the same sign on factual accuracy. Drop the "under 0.1 on a single run" rule; scores are integers.
- **How it was verified:** Not refuted; low is right. This is round one's corpus-frames-04 step 2 carried out, with a null result, and belongs inside that finding rather than as a separate item. (1) Is the claim true? Yes, with one number off and one overstatement. - I opened packages/agent/src/tools/answer-frame.ts:79-82 (optional questionType is still in the tool schema) and packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/query-intent.ts:431-432 (a supplied type is returned as-is). - I read the reviewer's s5_routes.mjs and recomputed every figure from its stored row file with my own script. ## RETR-25 · The concept backend matches aliases as substrings and returns the five longest tagged sections

### corpus-retrieval-09 — The concept backend matches aliases as substrings, so 'outpatient' or 'participant' injects the longest Prior Authorization sections
