Executing the Cypher against the local graph confirms the simulated behaviour: for the golden outpatient-pharmacy question the backend returns five 119k-155k character sections (a managed-care contract attachment, SSA 1915, SSA 1927, 45 CFR 146.136, policy 10C section 8.0) at 0.7. Unfiltered findConceptSections returns 3,307 rows for DHHS and 3,773 for CMS.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/concept.ts:11-12 `WHERE toLower($query) CONTAINS toLower(c.name)` / `OR ANY(alias IN c.aliases WHERE toLower($query) CONTAINS toLower(alias))`; :15 `WITH c ORDER BY size(c.name) DESC, c.name ASC LIMIT 2`; :31 `ORDER BY s.content_length DESC`. packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/concepts/seed.py:33 `{"name": "Prior Authorization", "aliases": ["prior approval", "PA", "pre-authorization"], "category": "process"},`. packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/concepts/linker.py:15-16 `# Acronym terms (all-caps, e.g. "PA", "POC", "PACE", "DME") must match` / `# case-sensitively, otherwise they collide with common English words`. packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/corpus_types.py:8 `ConceptRelationshipType = Literal["COVERS", "DEFINES", "RESTRICTS"]`
- **Impact:** A Python simulation of the Cypher predicate over the 55 golden questions with the 33 seed concepts: 13 questions select Prior Authorization only because the letters 'pa' occur inside outpatient, pharmacy, part, participant or SPAs (one also selects Durable Medical Equipment from 'amendments'). Other examples: "local DSS" selects Level of Care Determination, HCPCS selects Personal Care Services, "dispensing fee" selects Individual Support Plan, "space maintainers" selects PACE. Each such query has the five longest PA-mentioning numbered sections of the whole corpus injected at 0.7; they add to their documents' hit counts (coverage boost, title expansion) and are returned outright when other channels are weak. findConceptSections({conceptName:'EPSDT', relationshipType:'GOVERNS'}) always returns [], indistinguishable from no sections, and an unfiltered call for a concept such as DHHS or CMS returns every linked section with no bound.
- **Fix:** Prefer deleting the concept retrieval backend over rebuilding it. 1. **Remove the backend.** Take `'concept'` out of `BASE_BACKENDS` in `packages/policy-corpus/src/profiles/retrieval-signals.ts`. Run the retrieval eval once with and without it, on isolated observability storage. If scores do not regress, delete `backends/concept.ts`, its registry entry, `CONCEPT_MATCH_SCORE`, and the `'concept'` member of the backend-name unions in `policy-corpus/src/contracts.ts` and `packages/contracts/src/schemas.ts`. Record the result next to the reranker-removal note. Concept edges are ingest-time whole-word keyword matches, so the fulltext and vector backends already cover the relevance-ranked version of this channel. 2. **Only if the ablation shows it helps, fix it in place without a cache.** - Fetch the 33 concepts' name and aliases in the same session and match in TypeScript with the linker's rule: `\b` boundaries, all-caps terms case-sensitive against the raw query, minimum lengths 2 and 3. - Replace `ORDER BY s.content_length DESC` with a `section_fulltext` query restricted to sections linked to the matched concept ids. - Loop `profile.typeGroups` as `fulltext.ts` does. - Add a unit test with "outpatient", "participant" and "local DSS" as negatives. 3. **Fix `findConceptSections` regardless.** - Drop `'GOVERNS'` and `'REFERENCES_CONCEPT'` from `ALLOWED_CONCEPT_RELATIONSHIPS`. - Make the tool's `relationshipType` a `z.enum(['COVERS','DEFINES','RESTRICTS'])` and fix the description, so an invalid value is a schema error rather than a silent empty list. - Update the mocked `'GOVERNS'` value in `packages/agent/tests/tools/concepts.test.ts`. - Add a deterministic `ORDER BY d.document_type, d.policy_number, s.section_number` and a `LIMIT` of about 50, and return a `totalCount` so the model knows the list was truncated.
- **How it was verified:** Opened concept.ts, primitives/concepts.ts, types.ts, the agent tool tools/concepts.ts, seed.py, linker.py, corpus_types.py, queries.py (MERGE_CONCEPT), profiles/retrieval-signals.ts, backend-registry.ts and retrieval/collector.ts. Every quoted line is present verbatim at the cited location. The concept backend is in BASE_BACKENDS for every profile and is registered in RETRIEVAL_BACKENDS, so it runs on every hybrid search with the raw input.query. Nothing normalises or tokenises the query before the Cypher CONTAINS predicate. CONCEPT_MATCH_SCORE is 0.7. The document-type filter is applied only when hasExplicitDocumentTypes is true. Hits are merged by mergeMax into the section map that feeds computeDocumentCoverageBoosts and selectDocumentTitlesForExpansion, as the finding says. The seed stores aliases including "PA", "LOC", "ISP", "POC", "MAD", "DME", "PCS", "CLS". The linker uses \b who…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted: the finding is real for this project, untracked and undocumented, and medium is the right severity. What I opened: concept.ts, backend-registry.ts, profiles/retrieval-signals.ts, collector.ts (merge semantics), primitives/concepts.ts, types.ts, agent tools/concepts.ts, scoring-constants.ts, docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/decisions, docs/reviews/2026-06-11-remediation-plan.md, the open issue list, and the local Neo4j graph (read-only). Is it an accepted trade-off? No. - gotchas.md and docs/decisions say nothing about concept matching. - The only comment near the code explains the ORDER BY for determinism, not the substring match. - None of the open issues (#144-#188) covers i…

### rag-design-10 — The graph is used as a document store; the concept layer returns the five longest tagged sections and cross-references are never used for retrieval

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/concept.ts:31`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** At query time no retrieval backend traverses cross-reference or semantic edges: retrieval joins are Document-HAS_SECTION-Section, Section-HAS_CHUNK-Chunk, Section-to-PolicyConcept, and AMENDS followed by HAS_SECTION. Variable-length traversal exists only in the navigation primitives (PARENT_OF*1..10, NEXT_SIBLING*0..), not in retrieval. The 15,169 REFERENCES and REFERENCES_DOCUMENT edges are reachable only through the follow-references tool. The concept layer is 33 concepts with 26,959 mention edges; its backend, which runs on every hybrid search, picks up to two concepts by substring match on the question and returns the five longest tagged sections at a fixed 0.7 score. Neo4j states filtered vector search is generally available from 2026.02; that this applies to Community Edition was not confirmed from a vendor statement.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/backends/concept.ts:30-32 `${CONCEPT_MATCH_SCORE} AS score` `ORDER BY s.content_length DESC` `LIMIT toInteger($k)`, | :11 `WHERE toLower($query) CONTAINS toLower(c.name)` | packages/policy-corpus/src/primitives/references.ts:7 `MATCH (s:Section {id: $id})-[r:REFERENCES|REFERENCES_DOCUMENT]->(target)` (the only use of those edges in policy-corpus or agent source) | packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/scoring-constants.ts:8 `export const CONCEPT_MATCH_SCORE = 0.7;`
- **Impact:** Reproduced read-only on the local graph: for a question containing 'prior authorization' the backend's own query returns a managed-care contract attachment (155,226 characters), SSA section 1915 (143,149), SSA section 1927 (140,880), 45 CFR 146.136 (134,964) and Policy 10C section 8.0 'Policy Implementation and History' (119,369), each at 0.7. None relates to the service asked about, each drags a body of over 100,000 characters through scoring, and each could only be read to character 6,000. The largest concepts are 'CMS' (3,773 edges), 'DHHS' (3,307) and 'Prior Authorization' (2,573), which cannot discriminate. Meanwhile the edges that do encode legal structure (policy cites regulation, section cites section) add nothing to recall unless the model asks.
- **Fix:** Do this in order and stop when the measurements stop paying. 1. Delete the concept layer now. Remove 'concept' from BASE_BACKENDS and the backend registry, delete backends/concept.ts and CONCEPT_MATCH_SCORE, and remove the findConceptSections tool from the agent, the deep-research agents and the prompt (it is unbounded). Drop the optional pipeline concept stage. Update collector.test.ts, search-ranking.test.ts, primitives.test.ts and helpers/graph-fixtures.ts. Confirm no regression with repeated `eval --level retrieval` runs. Do not rebuild a curated ontology unless a measured gap calls for it. 2. Prototype reference expansion as a guarded collector stage, not a fixed-score join. - Expand only from the top few hits and follow only Section-to-Section REFERENCES edges at first. - Cap per-source fan-out at about 3 and skip targets with high in-degree or very large content_length, which excludes hubs such as SSA section 1902. - Score each target relative to its source hit (source score minus a decay), not at a constant. - Audit the REFERENCES_DOCUMENT resolver before using those edges: the top target being a 1915(i) flyer with 565 inbound edges suggests mis-resolution. - Measure with `eval --level retrieval` filtered to reasoning_type multi_hop, cross_document and federal_reference, over repeated runs. Do not use `--level cross-ref`. - Per the c48a9d4 gotcha, trace whether the added hits reach answer-frame selection before merging. With only about 15 relevant golden questions, add questions first if the result is ambiguous. 3. Defer the keyword-to-fulltext collapse and RRF. If lexical cleanup is wanted, the minimal step is to add document title and policy number to the Section fulltext index (denormalised) and then retire sectionKeyword/documentKeyword one at a time behind the retrieval eval, keeping the existing score scale. Check hyphenated policy-number matching under Lucene tokenisation before removing the CONTAINS path. Do not introduce RRF unless the whole additive scoring layer is being recalibrated, and only with a frame-selection trace showing the new order survives. 4. Correct the overscan comment in scoring-constants.ts to say pre-filtering is available from Neo4j 2026.01 via the SEARCH clause but not yet adopted. File the real work as one pipeline change: write document_type onto Section and Chunk at load, recreate both vector indexes with `WITH [s.document_type]`, then replace the overscan in the section-vector, chunk-vector and policy-scoped backends. First verify on 2026.05 Community that `IN $docTypes` is a supported in-index predicate and w…
- **How it was verified:** The claim holds in every checked particular except one overstated phrase ("every traversal is a single-hop join"). What I opened: concept.ts, backend-registry.ts, collector.ts, profiles/retrieval-signals.ts, internal/scoring-constants.ts, internal/record-mapping.ts, primitives/references.ts, agent tools/navigate.ts, plus a grep of every relationship pattern in policy-corpus and agent non-test source. I ran read-only Cypher on the local graph and checked Neo4j's own blog. Confirmed: - **Quoted evidence is verbatim.** The concept backend matches concepts by `toLower($query) CONTAINS toLower(c.name)` (or alias), keeps the two longest-named, then returns sections at a constant 0.7 ordered by `s.content_length DESC`, capped at 5. Nothing in the ordering depends on the question beyond which concept matched. - **No guard prevents it.** `concept` is in `BASE_BACKENDS`, so it runs on every hybri…
- **Why this severity:** The finding survives this lens: it is not documented as an accepted trade-off, not tracked, not previously decided, and the scenario is realistic. Medium is defensible; nothing supports raising it, because nobody has shown these hits reach a final answer frame. **What I opened** - `concept.ts`, `backend-registry.ts`, `collector.ts`, `hybrid.ts`, `scoring-constants.ts`, `profiles/retrieval-signals.ts`, and `section-vector.ts` in `packages/policy-corpus/src`. - `docs/development/gotchas.md`, `docs/decisions`, `docs/architecture`, `docs/roadmap.md`, and the 2026-06-11 review and remediation plan. - Git history for `concept.ts`, the open issue list, and the local graph (read-only). **Is it an a…

## RETR-26 · Nothing checks that the agent's query embeddings match the corpus model and dimensions

Severity medium · effort M

### corpus-retrieval-11 — The embedding seam has no memo, no identity check and a dimension parameter the query side never sends

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/hybrid.ts:29`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim is true as stated, with three precisions. (1) In the federal-authority frame the four sequential searches carry at most two distinct texts: lines 228 and 559 use `query`, lines 235 and 252 use `focusedFederalQuery`. (2) The roughly 31 s wait per search applies to timeouts and network hangs; a non-retryable HTTP error such as 400 or 401 degrades to lexical immediately. (3) The `CorpusRelease` node and the agent carry no embedding identity, but section and chunk nodes do store an opaque plan digest that hashes model and dimensions, which only the pipeline release gate reads. The branch "without `EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` on the agent the vector statement errors and the search fails" was not exercised and is inferred from the collector's uncaught `Promise.all`.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/hybrid.ts:29 `const embedding = await timeSearchStep('embed query', () => embedQuery(queryInput.query));`. packages/policy-corpus/src/types.ts:15 `export type EmbedQuery = (query: string) => Promise<number[]>;`. packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts:40-44 `body: JSON.stringify({` / `model: VOYAGE_MODEL,` / `input: queries,` / `input_type: 'query',` / `}),`; :17 `const VOYAGE_TIMEOUT_MS = Math.max(1, intEnv('VOYAGE_TIMEOUT_MS', 15_000));`. packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/embeddings/voyage_client.py:75 `json={"input": texts, "model": model, "input_type": input_type, "truncation": False, "output_dimension": EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS},`. packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/federal-authority.ts:228 and :559 both search with the unmodified `query`.
- **Impact:** A federal-authority frame makes up to four sequential searches (federal-authority.ts:228, :235, :252, :559) and so up to four paid Voyage calls, two of them for text already embedded; a coverage frame makes two, and the agent's own search-sections calls add more. During a Voyage outage each search independently waits up to about 31 s before degrading, so one federal frame can spend about two minutes waiting before any lexical result returns. If a corpus is built at a non-default dimension (config.py documents 2048 for A/B runs), the query side still requests the 1024 default: with EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS set on the agent every query fails validation and silently degrades to lexical with one warn log; without it the section-vector statement errors and the search fails. If VOYAGE_MODEL and EMBEDDING_MODEL diverge at 1024 dimensions, nothing fails; Voyage documents the 4-series as mutually compatible, so a 4-series mix would work while a 3.x mix would return meaningless similarities.
- **Fix:** Keep every change in the agent and the release stamp; leave the `EmbedQuery` type as `(query) => Promise<number[]>`. 1. **Send the dimension.** In packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts add `output_dimension: EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` to the Voyage request body. Have the health.ts probe reuse the same request builder so it validates the request production actually makes. 2. **Stamp and compare identity.** In release.py's `stamp()`, add `release.embedding_model = $embedding_model, release.embedding_dimensions = $embedding_dimensions` from `manifest["embeddingModel"]` and `manifest["embeddingDimensions"]`. In readiness.ts's `defaultNeo4jProbe`, return both and fail readiness, logging through the existing `readyz.release_identity_failed` path, when they are present and differ from `VOYAGE_MODEL` or `EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS`. Skip the comparison when the properties are absent so local graphs without a release node still pass. 3. **Memoise with one small map.** In the agent's `embedQuery`, wrap the `embedQueries([query])` call (inside the try, so mode tracking still runs on a hit) with a module-level `Map<string, Promise<number[]>>` keyed by exact query text. Cap it at about 200 entries with oldest-first eviction, and delete the entry on rejection so failures are never cached. This covers the within-request duplicates and cross-request repeats without a request scope or a new dependency. 4. **Shorten the wait.** Lower the `VOYAGE_TIMEOUT_MS` default from 15 s to about 4 s. In the existing catch block set `skipVoyageUntil = Date.now() + 30_000` and return `[]` immediately, marking lexical-degraded, while it is in the future; the first call after expiry probes Voyage again. Keep the window short because it applies to all users. 5. **Do not change the `EmbedQuery` return type.** The library has no release identity to assert against, and the readiness comparison in step 2 already covers it.
- **How it was verified:** I could not refute the finding; every quoted line is present verbatim and no guard elsewhere prevents the described behaviour. Nothing was executed, so one impact branch rests on reading only. What I opened: `hybrid.ts`, `types.ts`, the agent's `policy-corpus.ts`, `config.ts` and `health.ts` (by grep), the pipeline's `voyage_client.py`, `config.py`, `release.py`, `planner.py` and `plan_graph.py`, every non-test `searchSectionsDetailed` call site, and the live Voyage embeddings documentation. - **No memo:** `searchSectionsDetailed` calls `embedQuery` unconditionally on every search. A grep for memo/cache/LRU in the retrieval directory and for all `embedQuery`/`embedQueries` users found no cache or dedupe layer. The only reuse is the eval CLI runner, which pre-batches embeddings and is not the chat path. - **Repeated text in the federal frame:** the four searches are sequential awaits. Li…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted, severity unchanged at medium, but only one of the four bundled parts carries that severity. I opened packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts, packages/agent/src/config.ts, packages/policy-corpus/src/retrieval/hybrid.ts, the federal-authority frame call sites, packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/config.py and release.py, docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/operations, RUNBOOK.md, the 2026-10-03 model audit, and the open issue list. **Not already tracked or accepted.** None of the open issues (#144-#188) covers embedding identity, memoisation or the query-side dimension. The model/dimension match is documented only as a "MUST match" comment in both config files and a "must match" row in the…

### model-inventory-08 — One embedding model, two environment names and an unsent dimension: query embeddings can silently diverge from the corpus

- **Location:** `packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts:40`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** bug · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The corpus is embedded under EMBEDDING_MODEL and EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS (pipeline) and queried under VOYAGE_MODEL (agent). Comments on both sides say they must match, but nothing checks it. The pipeline sends output_dimension; the agent's query request and its health probe do not, although the agent validates the response width against its own EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS. CorpusRelease is not stamped with the embedding model or dimensions that the release manifest already holds, so /readyz cannot compare the graph with the agent's query model. This is latent, not live: the deployed defaults agree (voyage-4, 1024 on both sides) and no tracked deploy config overrides them. If EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS is set to anything other than 1024, every chat search degrades to lexical-only. The degradation produces a warning log and retrievalMode 'lexical-degraded' in the search tool output and corpus status, while /dependencyz still reports Voyage ok and /readyz is unaffected. If only one model variable is changed, same-width vectors from different models are compared with no error. That is harmful across model families (for example voyage-law-2 against voyage-4). Within the voyage-4 series Voyage documents the embeddings as mutually compatible. scripts/eval-ab.sh also carries a stale comment saying the pipeline sends no output_dimension, and its eval arm does not pass EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS to the query side.
- **Evidence:** packages/agent/src/config.ts:5-7 "MUST match the pipeline's EMBEDDING_MODEL used to build the corpus being queried, or vector similarity is meaningless." / `export const VOYAGE_MODEL = strEnv('VOYAGE_MODEL', 'voyage-4');` | packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts:40-44 `body: JSON.stringify({` / `model: VOYAGE_MODEL,` / `input: queries,` / `input_type: 'query',` / `}),` and :102 `embedding.length !== EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS ||` | packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/embeddings/voyage_client.py:75 `json={"input": texts, "model": model, "input_type": input_type, "truncation": False, "output_dimension": EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS},` | packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/release.py:298-305 `MERGE (release:CorpusRelease {id: 'current'})` / `SET release.release_id = $release_id,` ... `release.jev_annotation_digest = $jev_annotation_digest` (no embedding fields) | Voyage docs 2026-10-04: voyage-4 embedding dimension "1024 (default), 256, 512, 2048".
- **Impact:** Set EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS=2048 on both sides (docker-compose.ab.yml:3 and config.py:47 describe exactly this candidate): documents are embedded at 2048, the agent receives 1024-wide query vectors, its own check throws, embedQuery catches the error, and every chat search degrades to lexical-only with a warning log while /dependencyz still reports Voyage ok. Set only one of the two model variables and same-width vectors from different models are compared with no error at all. The October audit had to note that the production graph "contains no embedding-model provenance".
- **Fix:** In priority order: 1. **Stamp the graph.** In `mark_graph_release` (packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/release.py:296-305), add `release.embedding_model = $embedding_model, release.embedding_dimensions = $embedding_dimensions` from `manifest["embeddingModel"]` and `manifest["embeddingDimensions"]`. 2. **Compare in /readyz.** In `defaultNeo4jProbe` (packages/agent/src/readiness.ts), also return those two properties. In `handleReadiness`, set neo4j to 'error' and log both sides when they are present and differ from the agent's model and dimensions. - Absent fields: log a warning and pass for now, because the current production marker has none and `/readyz` is the Railway healthcheck. - Once the production graph is re-stamped or the next release is promoted, make absent fields a failure in production. - Rollout order: ship the pipeline stamp first, then the agent check. 3. **Send the dimension from the agent.** Move `EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` from policy-corpus.ts:19 into config.ts. Send `output_dimension: EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` in `voyageRequest` and in the `checkVoyage` probe body in health.ts. No separate width validation is needed in the probe: an unsupported dimension comes back as an HTTP error, which the probe already reports. 4. **Fix the stale comment** at scripts/eval-ab.sh:42-46; the pipeline does send `output_dimension`. 5. **Optional cleanup:** rename `VOYAGE_MODEL` to `EMBEDDING_MODEL` in the agent, and update eval-ab.sh and the Railway agent variable if one is set. This is naming hygiene only; the readiness comparison is what enforces the match.
- **How it was verified:** Every quoted piece of evidence is present verbatim and I found no guard that prevents the described behaviour. The finding is true in substance; three details need qualifying. What I opened: agent config.ts, policy-corpus.ts (whole embed path), health.ts, readiness.ts, corpus-status.ts references, policy-corpus retrieval/hybrid.ts, pipeline config.py, voyage_client.py, release.py, embeddings/generator.py and plan_graph.py, scripts/eval-ab.sh, docker-compose.ab.yml, the 2026-10-03 model audit, and the live Voyage embeddings page. Confirmed: - Two names for one setting. The pipeline reads EMBEDDING_MODEL and EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS; the agent reads VOYAGE_MODEL and, separately, EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS. Both comments say the models must match. A repo-wide grep finds no code comparing them. - Unsent dimension. The pipeline sends output_dimension; the agent's voyageRequest and the health probe sen…
- **Why this severity:** The finding survives at medium, but three parts of its impact statement are overstated and should be corrected before it goes into the report. What I opened: agent config.ts, policy-corpus.ts, health.ts, readiness.ts, corpus-status.ts, tools/search.ts; pipeline config.py, release.py, voyage_client.py, eval/runner.py, eval/levels/retrieval.py; scripts/eval-ab.sh, docker-compose.ab.yml; gotchas.md, docs/operations, RUNBOOK.md, the 2026-10-03 model audit; open issues (#188 body searched for embedding/provenance); the live Voyage embeddings page. Why it is not refuted: - **Asymmetry is real.** The pipeline sends `output_dimension`; the agent's `voyageRequest` and the health probe do not. The `C…

### contracts-seams-04 — No graph-level handshake for embedding model, dimensions or schema: the agent trusts two differently named env vars and the only schema marker is write-only

- **Location:** `packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts:40`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** The claim stands, with two refinements. (1) The graph does carry an indirect record of the model: per-section embedding plan hashes are computed from (inputs, model, dimensions) and `inspect_release_graph` verifies them through `embedding_coverage`, but only against the pipeline's own `EMBEDDING_MODEL`/`EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS`; nothing the agent reads exposes it and `/readyz` does not compare it. (2) The impact statement that release evals "pass" because they run in the corpus-job environment is unverified: no script under scripts/ops and no pipeline eval code sets or forwards `VOYAGE_MODEL`, so the eval agent's query model is whatever `VOYAGE_MODEL` the job environment has (default voyage-4), independent of `EMBEDDING_MODEL`.
- **Evidence:** packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts:40-44 `body: JSON.stringify({ model: VOYAGE_MODEL, input: queries, input_type: 'query', }),` packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/embeddings/voyage_client.py:75 `json={"input": texts, "model": model, "input_type": input_type, "truncation": False, "output_dimension": EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS},` packages/agent/src/config.ts:5-6 `// candidate model. MUST match the pipeline's EMBEDDING_MODEL used to build the // corpus being queried, or vector similarity is meaningless.` packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/release.py:298-299 `MERGE (release:CorpusRelease {id: 'current'}) SET release.release_id = $release_id,` packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/schema_version.py:4-5 `current load, when the load finished, and the pipeline package version. The agent / policy-corpus can read this back (via `getSchemaVersion` in TS) to detect drift` packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/schema.py:40 `session.run("MATCH (n) DETACH DELETE n")` packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/eval/results.py:159 `"embedding_model": EMBEDDING_MODEL,`
- **Impact:** Embedding model change through a blue/green release: corpus-job builds with EMBEDDING_MODEL=X and its release evals run the agent CLI in the corpus-job environment, so they pass; scripts/ops/corpus-release.sh never touches VOYAGE_MODEL; the production agent keeps the old value. With equal width (voyage-law-2 and every current default are 1024; the repo switched law-2 to voyage-4 in b739229 on 2026-06-14) Neo4j accepts the vectors, cosine scores are noise, `/readyz` is green, and keyword/fulltext backends mask the loss, so answers are grounded in the wrong sections. Rollback has the same hazard reversed. Non-default width: an agent set to EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS=2048 receives 1024-wide vectors, throws at policy-corpus.ts:98-108, and `embedQuery` catches it and returns [] so every search runs lexical-only. Conditional on a reused volume: a width change leaves the old index, new vectors are silently not indexed, and the embedding gates still pass. Eval provenance is also wrong: scripts/eval-ab.sh:158 sets only VOYAGE_MODEL for the retrieval arm, so the candidate's result file is stamped with the pipeline default.
- **Fix:** Keep the stamp-and-compare design, but make it one coherent change: 1. **Stamp.** In `mark_graph_release`, also set `release.embedding_model = manifest["embeddingModel"]` and `release.embedding_dimensions = manifest["embeddingDimensions"]`. Do not stamp a plan version: the manifest has none and the agent cannot compare one. 2. **One variable name.** Have the agent read `EMBEDDING_MODEL` and `EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS`, the pipeline's names, and delete `VOYAGE_MODEL` from config.ts and eval-ab.sh. This also fixes the eval result provenance in results.py with no further change. Pass `EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` on eval-ab.sh line 158 too. 3. **One request builder.** Export a single Voyage query-body builder that sends `output_dimension: EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS`, and use it in both policy-corpus.ts and health.ts. 4. **Compare in `/readyz`.** Extend the existing `CorpusRelease` probe in readiness.ts to return the two properties and add them to `releaseMatches`. In production, treat a stamp missing them as a failure, as a null release id already is, and log expected versus actual in `readyz.release_identity_failed`. Re-stamp the current graph with `release-mark-graph` instead of tolerating nulls. 5. **Push the values at promotion.** In corpus-release.sh, have `set_agent_target` set `EMBEDDING_MODEL` and `EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` from the target manifest (`jq -r .embeddingModel` / `.embeddingDimensions`) in the same `railway variable set` call as `NEO4J_URI` and `CORPUS_RELEASE_ID`, on both promote and rollback. Without this, the new check blocks every model-change release until someone hand-edits the variable. 6. **Check the index.** In `inspect_release_graph`, run `SHOW VECTOR INDEXES YIELD name, state, options` and report a metric that fails the existing embedding gate unless `section_embeddings` and `chunk_embeddings` are ONLINE with `vector.dimensions` equal to the manifest value. 7. **Remove `SchemaVersion` with its dependents.** Delete schema_version.py, its test and the CLI command, and in the same change: replace `graph_is_ready` (corpus-release.sh:229-236) with a plain connectivity probe such as `RETURN 1`, and remove the `schemaVersion` stage from release.py:265, prepare-qualification.py:283 and the `run-all` call in cli/build.py:340. `CorpusRelease.commit_sha` already pins the corpus manifest, so no fields need to move.
- **How it was verified:** The claim holds: every structural statement I checked is true of the current code, and I found no guard that closes the gap. What I opened: packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts, config.ts, readiness.ts and health.ts; the pipeline's voyage_client.py, config.py, release.py, graph/schema.py, graph/schema_version.py, embeddings/indexer.py, embeddings/plan_graph.py, eval/results.py and eval/runner.py; scripts/eval-ab.sh and scripts/ops. I also ran one read-only query against the local Neo4j. Confirmed: - **Two env vars, no shared name.** The agent's query request sends `VOYAGE_MODEL`; the pipeline's document request sends `EMBEDDING_MODEL`. Both default to voyage-4, and the only link between them is a comment on each side saying they must match. - **Query request omits `output_dimension`.** The agent body is `{model, input, input_type}` only (the health probe is the same), while the pipeline …
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted; medium stands. What I opened: packages/agent/src/config.ts, policy-corpus.ts, readiness.ts, corpus-status.ts; packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/release.py, config.py, graph/schema.py, graph/schema_version.py, graph/loader.py, embeddings/indexer.py, evaluation_evidence.py; scripts/eval-ab.sh, scripts/ops/corpus-release.sh; docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/operations/blue-green-corpus-releases.md and embedding-input-plans.md; deploy/railway/; open issues via gh. Is it an accepted or tracked trade-off? No. - The only acknowledgement is a pair of "MUST match" code comments (agent config.ts:5-6, pipeline config.py:48). They name the hazard; they do not enforce it or record a decision.…

### pipeline-parse-graph-09 — The graph does not say what it contains: SchemaVersion is never read, and embedding model and dimension are not recorded

- **Location:** `packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/schema_version.py:5`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** `SchemaVersion` stores a manifest hash, a timestamp and a constant package version. Its docstring says the agent reads it through `getSchemaVersion`, but no TypeScript reader exists; the only reader is the Python `schema-version` CLI, which corpus-release.sh uses as a connectivity probe with output discarded. The embedding model and dimension are not on `CorpusRelease` or any graph node the agent reads; they exist only inside the per-node `embedding_plan_hash` and in the release manifest file. The vector indexes are created `IF NOT EXISTS` with the dimension interpolated, nothing drops or inspects indexes, and `reset_database` deletes nodes only, so a dimension change leaves the old index in place. Per Neo4j documentation, vectors of the new dimension are then silently not indexed and queries with the new dimension return an error, while the property-based coverage and `invalidEmbeddingDimensions` gates still pass. This is reachable through scripts/eval-ab.sh `gate_embeddings`, which builds a 1024 arm and then a CANDIDATE_DIMS arm on the same container with `--reset`, if CANDIDATE_DIMS is overridden from its 1024 default. The agent takes its query model from `VOYAGE_MODEL` while the pipeline uses `EMBEDDING_MODEL`; `EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` has the same name on both sides but is configured independently, and no readiness check compares either with the graph.
- **Evidence:** packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/schema_version.py:4-5 `current load, when the load finished, and the pipeline package version. The agent /` / `policy-corpus can read this back (via `getSchemaVersion` in TS) to detect drift`; a search of packages/agent, packages/policy-corpus, packages/web, e2e and scripts finds no `SchemaVersion` or `getSchemaVersion` reader (only scripts/ops/corpus-release.sh:235 runs `schema-version >/dev/null` as a probe); packages/pipeline/pyproject.toml:3 `version = "1.0.0"`; packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/embeddings/indexer.py:11 `CREATE VECTOR INDEX section_embeddings IF NOT EXISTS` and :15 `` `vector.dimensions`: {EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS}, ``; packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/schema.py:40 `session.run("MATCH (n) DETACH DELETE n")`; packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/embeddings/planner.py:53 `digest = hashlib.sha256(f"{PLAN_VERSION}:{model}:{dimensions}:{TOKENIZER_SHA256}".encode())`; packages/agent/src/config.ts:7 `export const VOYAGE_MODEL = strEnv('VOYAGE_MODEL', 'voyage-4');`; packages/agent/src/policy-corpus.ts:19 `const EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS = Math.max(1, intEnv('EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS', 1024));`. gotchas.md:140-141 record two occasions on which a healthy-looking graph held an older document-ID generation.
- **Impact:** An operator sets EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS=2048 for a rebuild on a graph that previously held 1024-dimension vectors (config.py:46-50 anticipates exactly this experiment). `ensure_vector_index` does nothing, 2048-dimension vectors are written, the property-based coverage check reports 100%, and vector search returns nothing useful (inferred from Neo4j index semantics; not executed). Separately, nothing detects the corpus job and the agent drifting to different models, and a restored graph of an older identity generation passes readiness, as it already has.
- **Fix:** 1. **Stamp the embedding identity on CorpusRelease.** In `mark_graph_release` (packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/release.py, the MERGE on CorpusRelease), also set `release.embedding_model = manifest["embeddingModel"]`, `release.embedding_dimensions = manifest["embeddingDimensions"]` and `release.graph_schema_version = GRAPH_SCHEMA_VERSION`. The last is a module constant, bumped by hand when document or section identity or graph shape changes. Do not add `plan_version` or `tokenizer_sha256`: nothing would read them, and both are already inside every `embedding_plan_hash`. 2. **Compare at agent readiness; do not make configuration graph-driven.** Extend the existing readiness query in packages/agent/src/readiness.ts to return the three properties. Report neo4j as not ready, with a log line naming both sides, when `embedding_model !== VOYAGE_MODEL`, when `embedding_dimensions !== EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS`, or when `graph_schema_version` is missing or below a `MIN_GRAPH_SCHEMA_VERSION` constant. Export EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS from config.ts beside VOYAGE_MODEL so there is one definition. Optionally send `output_dimension: EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` in the agent's Voyage request so the two sides are symmetric. 3. **Make `ensure_vector_index` check the existing indexes.** In packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/embeddings/indexer.py, after the two CREATE statements, run `SHOW VECTOR INDEXES YIELD name, options WHERE name IN ['section_embeddings','chunk_embeddings']` and raise if `options.indexConfig['vector.dimensions']` differs from EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS. This works on the installed Neo4j 2026.05. 4. **Drop the vector indexes on reset.** In `reset_database` (packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/graph/schema.py), add `DROP INDEX section_embeddings IF EXISTS` and `DROP INDEX chunk_embeddings IF EXISTS`. Otherwise step 3 turns the eval-ab and local `--reset` rebuilds into a hard failure with no way through. 5. **Remove SchemaVersion only together with its callers.** - First change `graph_is_ready` in scripts/ops/corpus-release.sh:229-236 to a probe that does not depend on the command, for example a `RETURN 1` through `pipeline.graph.connection.get_session`. Otherwise every `wait_for_graph` times out after five minutes. - Then delete schema_version.py, the `schema-version` command and the `run-all` call in cli/build.py, the `schemaVersion` stage in release.py:265 and prepare-qualification.py:283, the vulture whitelist entry, and tests/test_schema_version.py. - If that is more churn than wanted now, the minimum is to correct the docstring, which names a TypeScript reader that does not exist.
- **How it was verified:** I opened schema_version.py, indexer.py, graph/schema.py, release.py (manifest builder, run stages, mark_graph_release, inspect_release_graph), planner.py and plan_graph.py, config.py, the agent's config.ts, policy-corpus.ts and readiness.ts, scripts/eval-ab.sh and corpus-release.sh. I ran case-insensitive git grep over packages, scripts and e2e. Nothing was executed against Neo4j. Every structural claim holds: - SchemaVersion stores only manifest_hash, loaded_at and pipeline_version. The docstring names a TypeScript `getSchemaVersion`, and no TypeScript file in packages, scripts or e2e contains `SchemaVersion` or `getSchemaVersion` as a graph reader. - The only reader is the Python `schema-version` CLI, which corpus-release.sh uses as a connectivity probe with output discarded. - The CorpusRelease node carries no embedding model, dimension or schema version, and agent readiness reads on…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted. The defect is real and untracked, but the reviewer's impact mechanism is partly wrong and one impact clause is already handled in production. Medium holds, anchored on undetected agent/pipeline embedding-model drift rather than on the dimension scenario. What I opened: schema_version.py, embeddings/indexer.py, graph/schema.py, config.py, release.py (manifest, CorpusRelease stamp, inspect_release_graph, gates), embeddings/plan_graph.py, generator.py, voyage_client.py, eval/levels/retrieval.py, agent readiness.ts, policy-corpus.ts, config.ts, the policy-corpus vector backends, scripts/eval-ab.sh, scripts/ops/corpus-release.sh (read only), blue-green-corpus-releases.md, gotchas.md…

## RETR-27 · Specialised frame selection is not shown to surface the right section better than search

Severity medium · effort L

### corpus-frames-11 — Recorded frames hold the golden primary section about 42% of the time, about what the top six search hits give; a much simpler frame is plausible and testable

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/coverage.ts:132`
- **Severity:** medium · **Kind:** recommendation · **Effort:** L · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** On the repo's saved held-out runs from July (five full runs at commit 210ced2, 48 questions with observable labels, 240 frames), the roughly 4,000 non-test lines under frame/ placed the labelled primary section in primary or supporting in 42.5% of frames (35.0% primary, 7.5% supporting only). Plain hybrid search on the same questions at an earlier commit and corpus (June, b60efd7) had it in the top 6 for 39.6% and the top 10 for 50-52%. So the frame was not measurably better than plain search at surfacing it, though the two numbers are not from a controlled comparison. For 7 questions that were routed both ways within commits eb6bcde and 8dcea40 (not 210ced2), the 33-line general pipeline's frame contained the golden primary in 17 of 26 runs against 14 of 34 for the specialised pipelines, with equal mean scores (2.96 and 2.97). That difference is driven by three questions, and the agent's query or question type differed between arms. Frame selection code is not unchanged since 210ced2: 4 files changed (44 insertions, 5 deletions), adding an env-gated Jev section-role bonus, env-gated prior-auth role ranking, a federal-statute fallback search, and a coverage-scope bypass in service.ts. The core selection logic is the same. Selection does contain program-specific phrase rules (for example CAP/DA body phrases applied to coverage questions flagged as eligibility-criteria queries). That this is why selection fails to transfer to paraphrases is a hypothesis, not something the saved runs establish.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/coverage.ts:132 `export async function resolveCoverageFrame(` packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/coverage-sections/selection/groups.ts:101-107 `includesPhrase(combineText(section.sectionNumber, section.title, section.content), [` `'18 years of age',` `'adult with disabilities',` `'risk of institutionalization',` (CAP/DA body phrases used for every eligibility question) packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/pipelines/general.ts:24 `primaryCandidates: buildSectionCandidates(hits, searchTerms, [], FRAME_PRIMARY_LIMIT * 2, 'Top search result.'),`
- **Impact:** Five saved full runs at commit 210ced2, 48 held-out questions whose labelled ids were observed, 240 frames. The golden primary section was in primarySections in 35.0%, in supporting only in 7.5%, in avoid only in 0.8%, and absent in 56.7%. The first primary section was golden in 18.3%. 27 questions never had it in any run. Plain search on the same 48 questions (June, commit b60efd7) had it in the top 6 for 39.6% and the top 10 for 50-52%. Answers scored 3 of 3 in 89% of runs when the frame had it in primary and 74% when absent; the agent fetched it with another tool in 77 of 148 absent cases. For 7 questions routed both ways on the same commit, the general pipeline's frame contained the golden primary in 17 of 26 runs (65%) against 14 of 34 (41%) for the specialised pipelines, with mean scores of 2.96 and 2.97. Caveats: sparse labels (about 2 per question), small samples, July corpus, and the search and frame numbers come from different commits. The recorded reranker finding in gotchas (its order "did not survive answer-frame selection") is the same effect.
- **Fix:** Stage it, and make the cheap measurement the gate for any rewrite. 1. **Measure before building.** Write a thin standalone script that calls the eval worker's `frames` mode twice per question: once as-is, once with `questionType: 'general'`. Do not use `run_frames`; it is tied to Jev qualification. Use the roughly 50 golden questions with primary labels plus the 100 paraphrases, mapped through `paraphrase_of` and the identity catalog. Report golden-first, in-primary and in-primary-plus-supporting, per question type. This needs no frame code changes; it does need Voyage query embeddings, a small paid cost the owner should run. 2. **Build the simple frame only if step 1 shows general matching or beating the specialised pipelines.** Delete specialised selection one pipeline at a time, coverage first since it is the largest. Keep statute-plus-regulation pairing, amendment lookup through `getAmendments`, and the existing policy-lookup-then-top-hit document resolution. 3. **Drop the ingest-time role tags from the plan.** Jev roles are unqualified and gated, and moving the phrase heuristics to ingest keeps the same tuning. If structural companions are wanted, choose them by section number within the resolved document (the scope and not-covered sections) and measure that as its own arm. 4. **Add named regression checks to the acceptance gate.** Alongside the aggregate metric, require no regression on q-021 (managed-care-contract path), the CAP/DA general eligibility question, and the Policy 5B chiropractic scope plus not-covered question. Then run at least three complete answer runs per arm with the grounding and withholding gates. 5. **Treat avoid-bucket removal as separate agent cleanup.** Do it after the frame decision, with agent tests and an agent build, because it changes the tool schema, the prompt, the verifier evidence renderer and `answer-frame-planning.ts`.
- **How it was verified:** The measured numbers reproduce exactly and the quoted code is verbatim, so the finding stands in substance. Two details are wrong and one sentence is inference rather than measurement. What I checked: - **Quoted code.** All three quotes are present at the cited lines: `resolveCoverageFrame` at coverage.ts:132, the CAP/DA body phrases at groups.ts:98-107, and general.ts:24. general.ts is 33 lines. Non-test source under `frame/` is 3,984 lines in 23 files. - **Frame statistics.** I re-ran the reviewer's read-only Python scripts against the five saved held-out runs at 210ced2 (50 questions each). Two questions (h-027, h-034) have labelled ids never observed, leaving 48 questions and 240 frames. Every figure in the impact text matched: 35.0% primary, 7.5% supporting only, 0.8% avoid only, 56.7% absent, 18.3% first-primary, 27 questions never hit. - **Plain-search baseline.** Three June retr…
- **Why this severity:** I did not recompute the reviewer's percentages; under this lens I assumed them and asked whether the finding matters here. It does, and it is not already known or decided. What I opened: docs/development/gotchas.md (every frame mention), docs/development/jev.md, docs/decisions (one ADR, on repository knowledge only), issue #188, a title search of issues for "frame" (no hits), frame/pipelines/general.ts, frame/coverage-sections/selection/groups.ts, run_frames in packages/pipeline/src/pipeline/jev/benchmarks.py, and git diff --stat 210ced2..HEAD on the frame directory. Why it survives: - **Not an accepted trade-off.** The gotchas record why individual program-specific rules were added (CAP/DA…

## RETR-28 · An empty frame still tells the model to finalize from existing evidence

Severity low · effort S

### corpus-frames-10 — Empty or thin frames carry no evidence status, and route strictness is computed from the question text alone

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/finalize.ts:98`
- **Severity:** low (reviewer proposed medium) · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** S · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** With zero candidates every pipeline returns a normally shaped frame with empty arrays and the same route it would have with good evidence. Lane, widening policy and determinism hints come from `selectAnswerRoute(queryLower, questionType)` in `analyzeQuery`, before retrieval, and are never revised from what was found. `AnswerFrame` has no evidence-status field. `buildEmptyAnswerFrame` is used only by the policy-variant path; a second inline empty frame exists at `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/service.ts:31` for the dated coverage-scope path, and that path alone can carry a textual `coverageScope.notice` when a required source is missing. The planning processor still emits the plan for an empty frame, including "Do not widen beyond the current structured frame… Finalize from the verified evidence you already have" (`packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts:743-744`) on `deterministic_evidence` routes with widening `none`.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/context/finalize.ts:98-100 `return {` `questionType,` `route,` packages/policy-corpus/src/internal/answer-routing.ts:82-86 `if (isDeterministicCoverageQuery(queryLower)) {` `return buildAnswerRoute({` `lane: 'deterministic_evidence',` `rationale: 'numeric_thresholds',` `wideningPolicy: 'none',`
- **Impact:** Probe at HEAD with no hits. "What is the income limit for a family of four to get Medicaid coverage?" returns type coverage, lane deterministic_evidence, widening none, numeric_evidence_only true, and no documents or sections. The plan built from it says "Do not widen beyond the current structured frame for extra numeric details. Finalize from the verified evidence you already have." There is no evidence. "When was the most recent change notice issued for the hospice policy?" returns latest_revision_only with nothing found. The system prompt separately tells the model to use searchSections when the frame is incomplete, so the instructions conflict. The likely result is a non-answer rather than a wrong one, but that is inferred; I did not run the agent.
- **Fix:** Leave `route`, `widening_policy` and the frame schema alone. The route is the question-derived retrieval intent that the retrieval profile, coverage.ts:168 and the eval's route fields depend on. Fix the plan in packages/agent/src/processors/answer-frame-planning.ts instead: 1. Add a local predicate: `const frameIsEmpty = frame.primarySections.length === 0 && frame.supportingSections.length === 0 && frame.authoritativeDocuments.length === 0`. 2. In `buildPlanMessage`, when the frame is empty, replace "Use the structured frame as the answer outline for this turn." with an explicit line. For example: "The structured frame found no documents or sections for this question. Run searchSections (or lookupPolicy) before answering; if that also finds nothing, say the corpus does not contain the answer. Do not answer from memory." 3. Guard the "Do not widen... Finalize from the verified evidence you already have." rule (line 743) with `ctx.frame.primarySections.length > 0`. Give the baseline_hybrid "Answer directly from the current frame before widening further." rule (line 711) the same guard. 4. Keep the strictness hints (`numeric_evidence_only`, `latest_revision_only`) active on empty frames. They are what stops the model inventing a number or a date. 5. Add zero-hit tests: one planning-processor test per route lane asserting the "found nothing / search" line appears and the "Finalize" line does not, plus one zero-candidate `resolveAnswerFrame` test per pipeline in policy-corpus pinning the empty shape. Skip the `ok/thin/empty` status field and the "thin" tier until an eval shows a thin-frame failure and gives a threshold to calibrate against. If a corpus-level signal is wanted later, add a separate optional field such as `evidenceCounts`; do not overload `route`.
- **How it was verified:** The claim holds; I reproduced it at HEAD. **What I read** - `finalize.ts`: `finalizeFrame` returns the route it was handed, unchanged, alongside whatever arrays hydrate. There is no count, status or emptiness check. - `answer-routing.ts`: `selectAnswerRoute(queryLower, questionType)` takes only the normalised question and the question type. It is called once, in `analyzeQuery` (`query/analysis.ts:65`), before any retrieval. - A grep across `policy-corpus/src` and `agent/src` found no code that rewrites `route`, `widening_policy` or `determinism_hints` after retrieval. Pipelines only read the route, to pick `topK`. - `AnswerFrame` has no evidence-status field. `buildEmptyAnswerFrame` (`section-views.ts:132`) is used only in `policy-variant.ts:24` and also has none. - `AnswerFramePlanningProcessor` returns early only when the frame is missing, not when it is empty. The "Do not widen… Fina…
- **Why this severity:** Not refuted, but I would lower it to low. The gap is real and nothing documents it as accepted, yet the headline scenario is contrived and the outcome fails safe. What I opened: frame/context/finalize.ts, internal/answer-routing.ts, frame/service.ts, scope/coverage.ts, frame/pipelines/coverage.ts and retrieval/collector.ts in policy-corpus; processors/answer-frame-planning.ts, answer-frame-prefetch.ts, verifier/evidence.ts, tools/answer-frame.ts and prompt.ts in agent; docs/development/gotchas.md, docs/reviews/2026-06-11-rag-faithfulness-review.md, and the open issue list. Why it is not dismissed: - The code is as described. finalizeFrame returns the same shape with no evidence indicator, a…

## RETR-29 · The frame layer carries unread flags, unreachable branches and repeated section fetches

Severity low · effort M

### corpus-frames-12 — Duplicated and dead machinery: 24 of 29 QueryFlags unread, signals recomputed per pipeline, identical branches, 25 section fetches for 9 sections

- **Location:** `packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/federal-authority.ts:316`
- **Severity:** low · **Kind:** design · **Effort:** M · **Status:** confirmed
- **Claim:** Confirmed by reading the code: 24 of 29 QueryFlags have no production reader (only isChangeNoticeQuery, isLatestChangeNoticeQuery, isOperationalRoutingQuery, isFederalAuthorityQuery and asksForHistory are read; the Jev eval worker returns the whole analysis). buildCoverageQueryFlags runs twice per coverage frame. buildFederalAuthoritySignals recomputes focus terms, quoted phrases and the focused federal query that QueryAnalysis already carries, so the evidence-plan.ts comment is inaccurate. Both arms of the waiver ternary at federal-authority.ts:316-331 are identical. The coverage topK 24 and 12 arms and the general pipeline's deterministic arm are unreachable. RetrievalProfile.name, lane and wideningPolicy are read only by one test. The agent's planning processor has its own copies of two predicates (drift not verified). The per-frame query counts (82 queries, 25 getSection calls for 9 sections, 59 queries for a waiver frame) and the three-way statute pairing are the reviewer's measurements and were not reproduced; getSection is uncached and called from several hydration paths, so repeats are plausible.
- **Evidence:** packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/federal-authority.ts:318-319 and again :329-330 `waiverAnchorHits.find((hit) => hit.policyNumber?.toUpperCase() === '42-CFR-441')?.documentTitle,` / `waiverAnchorHits.find((hit) => hit.policyNumber?.toUpperCase() === 'SSA-TITLE-19')?.documentTitle,` (both arms of the ternary are identical) packages/policy-corpus/src/frame/evidence-plan.ts:12-13 `// The analysis IS the plan: its questionType selects the pipeline and it` / `// carries every query-derived value each pipeline needs.`
- **Impact:** Only 5 of the 29 analysis flags are read by production code; the rest are computed on every call and consumed only by the offline Jev routing worker. buildCoverageQueryFlags runs in the pipeline and again in the rebalance. buildFederalAuthoritySignals recomputes focus terms, quoted phrases and the focused query already on the analysis. Statute pairing is implemented three times: title pairing, candidate splice, and rebalance retention. Coverage lane branches for topK 24 and 12 and the general pipeline's deterministic branch are unreachable, because those types never get those lanes. RetrievalProfile.name, lane and wideningPolicy are never read. Measured on a 9-section document: one yes/no coverage frame issued 82 session queries and 2 embedding calls, including 25 getSection round trips for 9 distinct sections. A federal waiver frame issued 59 queries and 4 embedding calls. Every rule change must be made in several places, and the agent's planning processor holds drifted copies of the same predicates.
- **Fix:** Split into two steps. Step 1, behaviour-neutral deletions: collapse the identical ternary to `waiverApplicationQuery || waiverAuthorityQuery`; remove the unreachable topK arms; drop RetrievalProfile.name/lane/wideningPolicy (or keep name only as a trace label); fix the evidence-plan.ts comment; pass the already-built coverage flags into the rebalance instead of rebuilding; have buildFederalAuthoritySignals take QueryAnalysis for focusTerms/quotedPhrases/focusedFederalQuery. For the 24 unread flags, first confirm which the Jev routing eval actually scores, then delete the rest or move them into the eval worker. Step 2, behaviour-sensitive and gated on the pinned frame tests plus an answer-eval comparison: add a per-frame getSection memo in createFrameContext (cheaper and safer than re-plumbing hydrated sections), merge CoverageQueryFlags/FederalAuthoritySignals into one analysis object, unify statute pairing, and expose the needed flags on the frame so answer-frame-planning.ts stops re-deriving them.
- **How it was verified:** (1) Factual check: the statically checkable claims hold. I opened frame/federal-authority.ts, frame/evidence-plan.ts, frame/pipelines/{coverage,federal-authority,amendment,general,prior-auth}.ts, frame/rebalance/coverage.ts, frame/context/hydration.ts, internal/answer-routing.ts, profiles/retrieval.ts, query/analysis.ts and contracts.ts. - Identical ternary arms: confirmed at federal-authority.ts:318-319 and 329-330. Only the comment differs. - Flags: QueryFlags has 29 fields. Grepping every `.flags` reader outside analysis.ts, contracts.ts and tests finds exactly 5 read: isChangeNoticeQuery, isLatestChangeNoticeQuery, isOperationalRoutingQuery, isFederalAuthorityQuery, asksForHistory. The other 24 are computed on every analyzeQuery call. The only other consumer is agent/src/jev/eval-worker.ts, which returns the whole analysis. - Recomputation: buildCoverageQueryFlags(queryLower) runs i…
